is multilinear for all
\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}
.

Lemma 2.23
.

Given integers
n,h>0,m>1
, let
\mathbf{P}\in L^{2}\left(\mathcal{M}_{m}^{\otimes n},\gamma_{h}\right)
with an associated vector-valued function
p
under a standard orthonormal basis. It holds that
N_{2}\left(\mathbf{P}\right)=\left\|p\right\|_{2}.

Proof.

Consider

N_{2}\left(\mathbf{P}\right)^{2}=\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathbf{P}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}\right]=\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{g}\sim\gamma_{n}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}\left|p_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{g}\right)\right|^{2}\right]=\left\|p\right\|_{2}^{2},

where the second equality follows from
2.11
item 3.
∎

Lemma 2.24
.

Given a multilinear random operator
\mathbf{P}\in L^{2}\left(\mathcal{M}_{m}^{\otimes h},\gamma_{n}\right)
with degree
d
and the associated vector-valued function
p
under a standard orthonormal basis, it holds that

\mathrm{Inf}\left(p\right)\leq\deg\left(\mathbf{P}\right)N_{2}\left(\mathbf{P}\right)^{2}.

Proof.

Consider

\mathrm{Inf}\left(p\right)\leq\deg\left(p\right)\mathrm{Var}\>\!\!\left[p\right]\leq\deg\left(p\right)\left\|p\right\|_{2}^{2}=\deg\left(\mathbf{P}\right)\left\|p\right\|_{2}^{2}=\deg\left(\mathbf{P}\right)N_{2}\left(\mathbf{P}\right)^{2},

where the first inequality follows from
2.6
item 5; the second inequality follows from
2.6
item 2; the first equality follows from the definition of the degree of a random operator; the second equality follows from
Lemma

2.23
.
∎

We say a pair of random operators
\left(\mathbf{P},\mathbf{Q}\right)\in L^{p}\left(\mathcal{M}_{m}^{\otimes h},\gamma_{n}\right)\times L^{p}\left(\mathcal{M}_{m}^{\otimes h},\gamma_{n}\right)
are
joint random operators
if the random variables
\left(\mathbf{g},\mathbf{h}\right)
in
\left(\mathbf{P},\mathbf{Q}\right)
are drawn from a joint distribution
\mathcal{G}_{\rho}^{\otimes n}
for
0\leq\rho\leq 1
.

2.5
Miscellaneous

Throughout this paper, functions
f:{\mathbb{R}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}
are also viewed as maps
f:\mathcal{H}_{m}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}_{m}
defined as

f\left(P\right)=\sum_{i}f\left(\lambda_{i}\right)\left|v_{i}\middle\rangle\middle\langle v_{i}\right|,

where

P=\sum_{i}\lambda_{i}\left|v_{i}\middle\rangle\middle\langle v_{i}\right|

is a spectral decomposition of
P
.

Given a closed convex set
\Delta\subseteq{\mathbb{R}}^{k}
, we say a map
\mathcal{R}:{\mathbb{R}}^{k}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{k}
is a rounding map of
\Delta
if for any
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k}
,
\mathcal{R}\left(x\right)
is the element in
\Delta
that is closest to
x
in
\left\|\cdot\right\|_{2}
distance. The following well-known fact states that the rounding maps of closed convex sets are Lipschitz continuous with Lipschitz constant being
1
.

Fact 2.25
.

[
13
, Page 149, Proposition 3.2.1]

Let
\Delta
be a nonempty closed convex set in
{\mathbb{R}}^{k}
with the rounding map
\mathcal{R}
. It holds that

\left\|\mathcal{R}\left(x\right)-\mathcal{R}\left(y\right)\right\|_{2}\leq\left\|x-y\right\|_{2},

for any
x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k}
.

Thus, if
\Delta
contains the element
\left(0,\ldots,0\right)
, then
\mathcal{R}
is a
contraction
. Namely,
\left\|\mathcal{R}\left(x\right)\right\|_{2}\leq\left\|x\right\|_{2}
for any
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k}
.

3
Markov super-operators, noise operators and maximal correlation

Definition 3.1
.

Given quantum systems
A
and
B
and a bipartite state
\psi_{AB}
, we define a
Markov super-operator

\mathcal{T}:\mathcal{M}\left(B\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{M}\left(A\right)
as follows.

\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(M^{\dagger}\otimes Q\right)\psi_{AB}\right)=\left\langle M,\mathcal{T}\left(Q\right)\right\rangle,

for any
M\in\mathcal{M}\left(A\right)
and
Q\in\mathcal{M}\left(B\right)
.

Lemma 3.2
.

Given quantum systems
A
and
B
and a bipartite state
\psi_{AB}
,
\mathcal{T}\left(Q\right)=m_{A}\mathrm{Tr}_{B}\left(\left(\mathds{1}\otimes Q\right)\psi_{AB}\right)
.

Proof.

By
Definition

3.1
,
\mathcal{T}\left(Q\right)
must satisfy that

m_{A}\mathrm{Tr}\left(M^{\dagger}\cdot\mathrm{Tr}_{B}\left(\left(\mathds{1}\otimes Q\right)\psi_{AB}\right)\right)=\mathrm{Tr}M^{\dagger}\mathcal{T}\left(Q\right)

for any
M\in\mathcal{M}\left(A\right)
. We conclude the result.
∎

Lemma 3.3
.

Given quantum systems
A
and
B
and a bipartite state
\psi_{AB}
, let
\mathcal{T}_{B}:\mathcal{M}\left(B\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{M}\left(A\right)
and
\mathcal{T}_{B^{n}}:\mathcal{M}\left(\mathcal{B}^{n}\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{M}\left(A^{n}\right)
be the Markov super-operator from
\mathcal{M}\left(B\right)
to
\mathcal{M}\left(A\right)
and the one from
\mathcal{M}\left(B^{n}\right)
to
\mathcal{M}\left(A^{n}\right)
with the corresponding bipartite states
\psi_{AB}
and
\psi_{AB}^{\otimes n}
, respectively. Then
\mathcal{T}_{B^{n}}=\otimes_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{T}_{B}
.

Proof.

By the linearity of
\mathcal{T}_{B}
and
\mathcal{T}_{B^{\otimes n}}
, it suffices to show
\mathcal{T}_{B^{n}}(Q)=\otimes_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{T}_{B}\left(Q_{i}\right)
when
Q=\otimes_{i=1}^{n}Q_{i}
. By
Lemma

3.2
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}\left(Q\right)

\displaystyle=m_{A}^{n}\mathrm{Tr}_{B^{n}}\left(\left(\mathds{1}_{A^{n}}\otimes Q\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes n}\right)

\displaystyle=\bigotimes_{i=1}^{n}\left(m_{A}\mathrm{Tr}_{B}\left(\left(\mathds{1}_{A}\otimes Q_{i}\right)\psi_{AB}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\otimes_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{T}_{B}\left(Q_{i}\right).

∎

Definition 3.4
.

Given a system
A
of dimension
m
,
\rho\in[0,1]
, a noise operator
\Delta_{\rho}:\mathcal{M}\left(A\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{M}\left(A\right)
on
\mathcal{M}(A)
is defined as follows. For any
P\in\mathcal{M}\left(A\right)
,

\Delta_{\rho}\left(P\right)=\rho P+\frac{1-\rho}{m}\left(\mathrm{Tr}P\right)\cdot\mathds{1}_{m}.

With a slight abuse of notations, the noise operator on the space
\mathcal{M}\left(A^{n}\right)
, again denoted by
\Delta_{\rho}
, is defined as
\Delta_{\rho}=\Delta_{\rho}^{(1)}\cdot\Delta_{\rho}^{(2)}\cdots\Delta_{\rho}^{(n-1)}\cdot\Delta_{\rho}^{(n)}
, where
\Delta_{\rho}^{(i)}
applies the noise operator
\Delta_{\rho}
to the
i
’th register and keeps other registers untouched.

The noise operators
\Delta_{\rho}
are also called
depolarizing channels

[
65
]
in quantum information theory, which are also analogs of the
Bonami-Beckner operators
in Fourier analysis
[
7
,
15
]
.

Recall that
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\cdot\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{p}
represents the normalized
p
-norm defined in
Eq.

20
.

Lemma 3.5
.

Given integers
d,n,m>0
,
\rho\in[0,1]
, a standard orthonormal basis of
\mathcal{M}_{m}
:
\mathcal{B}=\left\{\mathcal{B}_{i}\right\}_{i=0}^{m^{2}-1}
, the following holds.

1.

For any
P\in\mathcal{M}_{m}^{\otimes n}
with a Fourier expansion
P=\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{n}}\widehat{P}\left(\sigma\right)\mathcal{B}_{\sigma}
, it holds that

\Delta_{\rho}\left(P\right)=\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{n}}\rho^{\left|\sigma\right|}\widehat{P}\left(\sigma\right)\mathcal{B}_{\sigma}.

2.

For any
P\in\mathcal{M}_{m}^{\otimes n}
,
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\Delta_{\rho}\left(P\right)\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}\leq{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|P\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}
and
\left\|\Delta_{\rho}\left(P\right)\right\|\leq\left\|P\right\|
.

3.

If
P\geq 0
, then
\Delta_{\rho}\left(P\right)\geq 0
.

Proof.

Note that
\mathcal{B}_{0}=\mathds{1}_{m}
. Item 1 follows from the definition directly.

For item 2,
consider

{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\Delta_{\rho}\left(P\right)\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}=\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{n}}\rho^{2\left|\sigma\right|}\left|\widehat{P}\left(\sigma\right)\right|^{2}\leq\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{n}}\left|\widehat{P}\left(\sigma\right)\right|^{2}={\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|P\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}.

For the second inequality, in item 2,
note that
\Delta_{\rho}=\Delta_{\rho}^{(1)}\cdot\Delta_{\rho}^{(2)}\cdots\Delta_{\rho}^{(n)}
. Here
\Delta_{\rho}^{(i)}=\mathds{1}\otimes\ldots\mathds{1}\otimes\Delta_{\rho}\otimes\mathds{1}\otimes\ldots\otimes\mathds{1}
, where
\Delta_{\rho}
acts on the
i
-th register. Thus by induction it suffices to show
\left\|\Delta_{\rho}^{(i)}(P)\right\|\leq\left\|P\right\|
for
i\in[n]
. Without loss of generality, we may assume that
i=1
. From
Definition

3.4
, it is easy to see
\Delta_{\rho}^{(1)}(P)=\rho P+\frac{1-\rho}{m}\mathds{1}_{m}\otimes\left(\mathrm{Tr}_{1}P\right)
. Thus

\displaystyle\left\|\Delta_{\rho}^{(1)}(P)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\rho\left\|P\right\|+\frac{1-\rho}{m}\left\|\mathds{1}_{m}\otimes\left(\mathrm{Tr}_{1}P\right)\right\|

\displaystyle=\rho\left\|P\right\|+\frac{1-\rho}{m}\left\|\mathrm{Tr}_{1}P\right\|

\displaystyle=\rho\left\|P\right\|+\frac{1-\rho}{m}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{m}\left(\left\langle i\right|\otimes\mathds{1}_{m^{n-1}}\right)P\left(\left|i\right\rangle\otimes\mathds{1}_{m^{n-1}}\right)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\rho\left\|P\right\|+\frac{1-\rho}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\left\|\left\langle i\right|\otimes\mathds{1}_{m^{n-1}}\right\|\cdot\left\|P\right\|\cdot\left\|\left|i\right\rangle\otimes\mathds{1}_{m^{n-1}}\right\|

\displaystyle=\rho\left\|P\right\|+\frac{1-\rho}{m}\cdot m\left\|P\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|P\right\|

For item 3, it again suffices to show
\Delta_{\rho}^{(1)}(P)\geq 0
by induction. This is obvious since
\Delta_{\rho}^{(1)}(P)=\rho P+\frac{1-\rho}{m}\mathds{1}_{m}\otimes\left(\mathrm{Tr}_{1}P\right)
.
∎

Quantum maximal correlations introduced by Beigi
[
9
]
are crucial to our analysis. They are a generalization of maximal correlation coefficients
[
39
,
30
,
71
]
in classical information theory to the quantum setting.

Definition 3.6

(Maximal correlation)
.

[
9
]

Given quantum systems
A,B
and a bipartite state
\psi_{AB}
with
\psi_{A}=\frac{\mathds{1}_{m_{A}}}{m_{A}}
and
\psi_{B}=\frac{\mathds{1}_{m_{B}}}{m_{B}}
, the maximal correlation of
\psi_{AB}
is defined to be

\rho\left(\psi_{AB}\right)=\sup\left\{\left|\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(P^{\dagger}\otimes Q\right)\psi_{AB}\right)\right|~:P\in\mathcal{M}\left(A\right),Q\in\mathcal{M}\left(B\right),\atop\mathrm{Tr}~P=\mathrm{Tr}~Q=0,{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|P\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}={\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|Q\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}=1\right\}.

Fact 3.7
.

[
9
]

Given quantum systems
A,B
and a bipartite quantum state
\psi_{AB}
with
\psi_{A}=\frac{\mathds{1}_{m_{A}}}{m_{A}}
and
\psi_{B}=\frac{\mathds{1}_{m_{B}}}{m_{B}}
, it holds that
\rho\left(\psi_{AB}\right)\leq 1
.

Definition 3.8
.

Given quantum systems
A
and
B
with
\dim\left(A\right)=\dim\left(B\right)=m
, a bipartite state
\psi_{AB}\in\mathcal{D}\left(A\otimes B\right)
is an
m
-dimensional noisy maximally entangled state (MES) if
\psi_{A}=\psi_{B}=\frac{\mathds{1}_{m}}{m}
and its maximal correlation
\rho=\rho\left(\psi_{AB}\right)<1
.

An interesting class of noisy MESs is the states obtained by depolarizing MESs with an arbitrarily small noise.

Lemma 3.9
.

For any
0\leq\epsilon<1
integer
m>1
, it holds that

\rho\left(\left(1-\epsilon\right)\left|\Psi\middle\rangle\middle\langle\Psi\right|+\epsilon\frac{\mathds{1}_{m}}{m}\otimes\frac{\mathds{1}_{m}}{m}\right)=1-\epsilon,

where
\left|\Psi\right\rangle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}\sum_{i=0}^{m-1}|m,m\rangle
is an
m
-dimensional MES.

Proof.

The case that
m=2
is proved by Beigi in
[
9
]
. His proof can be directly generalized to any
m
. Here we provide a proof for completeness. Let
\Psi_{\epsilon}=\left(1-\epsilon\right)\left|\Psi\middle\rangle\middle\langle\Psi\right|+\epsilon\frac{\mathds{1}_{m}}{m}\otimes\frac{\mathds{1}_{m}}{m}
. From the definition of maximal correlations,

\displaystyle\rho\left(\Psi_{\epsilon}\right)=

\displaystyle\max\left|\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(X^{\dagger}\otimes Y\right)\Psi_{\epsilon}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\mathrm{s.t.}\mathrm{Tr}X=\mathrm{Tr}Y=0

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt\mathrm{Tr}X^{\dagger}X=\mathrm{Tr}Y^{\dagger}Y=m

For
X
and
Y
satisfying the constraints above, we have

\displaystyle\left|\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(X^{\dagger}\otimes Y\right)\Psi_{\epsilon}\right)\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(1-\epsilon\right)\left|\left\langle\Psi\right|\left(X^{\dagger}\otimes Y\right)\left|\Psi\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1-\epsilon}{m}\left|\mathrm{Tr}X^{T}Y^{\dagger}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1-\epsilon}{m}\left\|X\right\|_{2}\left\|Y\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1-\epsilon,

where the inequality is achieved by

X=Y=\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}e^{2\pi\mathrm{i}\cdot j/m}\left|j\middle\rangle\middle\langle j\right|.

∎

The following proposition provides a useful characterization of quantum maximal correlations.

Proposition 3.10
.

Given quantum systems
A,B
and a bipartite state
\psi_{AB}
with
\psi_{A}=\frac{\mathds{1}_{m_{A}}}{m_{A}}
and
\psi_{B}=\frac{\mathds{1}_{m_{B}}}{m_{B}}
, for any
Q\in\mathcal{M}\left(B\right)
,

\max\left\{\left|\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(P^{\dagger}\otimes Q\right)\psi_{AB}\right)\right|:P\in\mathcal{M}\left(A\right),{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|P\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}=1\right\}

(26)

is achieved by

P^{*}=\frac{\mathcal{T}\left(Q\right)}{{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{T}\left(Q\right)\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}},

with the maximum value
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{T}\left(Q\right)\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}
, where
\mathcal{T}:\mathcal{M}\left(B\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{M}\left(A\right)
is a Markov super-operator in
Definition

3.1
.
Thus,

\rho\left(\psi_{AB}\right)=\max\left\{{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{T}\left(Q\right)\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}:Q\in\mathcal{M}\left(B\right),\mathrm{Tr}~Q=0,{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|Q\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}=1\right\}.

(27)

Moreover, the maximal correlation in
Definition

3.6
can be achieved by a pair of Hermitian operators
\left(P,Q\right)
.

Proof.

The proof complies with the one for Lemma 2.8 in
[
61
]
. Let
P\in\mathcal{M}\left(A\right)
achieves the maximum value in Eq.
Eq.

26
. Then it satisfies that
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|P\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}=1
. Write
P=\alpha P^{*}+\beta P^{\prime}
, where
\left|\alpha\right|^{2}+\left|\beta\right|^{2}=1
,
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|P^{\prime}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}=1
and
\left\langle P^{*},P^{\prime}\right\rangle=0
. By the definition of Markov super-operators

0=\left\langle P^{\prime},\mathcal{T}\left(Q\right)\right\rangle=\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(\left(P^{\prime}\right)^{\dagger}\otimes Q\right)\psi_{AB}\right).

So we should set
\left|\alpha\right|=1.
Moreover,

\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(\mathcal{T}\left(Q\right)^{\dagger}\otimes Q\right)\psi_{AB}\right)={\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{T}\left(Q\right)\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}^{2}_{2}.

Note that if
\mathrm{Tr}~Q=0
, then by the definition of Markov super-operators,

\mathrm{Tr}~\mathcal{T}\left(Q\right)=m_{A}\left\langle\mathds{1},\mathcal{T}\left(Q\right)\right\rangle=m_{A}\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(\mathds{1}\otimes Q\right)\psi_{AB}\right)=\frac{m_{A}}{m_{B}}\mathrm{Tr}~Q=0

where the third equality follows from
2.1
.

By
Lemma

3.2
,
\mathcal{T}\left(Q\right)
is Hermitian if
Q
is Hermitian. Thus, to prove that the maximal correlation in
Definition

3.6
can be achieved by a pair of Hermitian operators
\left(P,Q\right)
, it suffices to prove that the maximum in Eq.
Eq.

27
can be achieved by a Hermitian matrix
Q
. Suppose
Q=Q_{1}+\mathrm{i}\cdot Q_{2}
achieves the maximum value in Eq.
Eq.

26
with Hermitian matrices
Q_{1}\neq 0
and
Q_{2}\neq 0
. Then

\mathrm{Tr}~Q=\mathrm{Tr}~Q_{1}+\mathrm{i}\cdot\mathrm{Tr}~Q_{2}=0

implies that
\mathrm{Tr}~Q_{1}=\mathrm{Tr}~Q_{2}=0
and

1={\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|Q\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}={\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|Q_{1}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}+{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|Q_{2}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}

By
Lemma

3.2
, both
\mathcal{T}\left(Q_{1}\right)
and
\mathcal{T}\left(Q_{2}\right)
are Hermitian. By the linearity of Markov super-operators, we have

\displaystyle{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{T}\left(Q\right)\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{T}\left(Q_{1}\right)\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}+{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{T}\left(Q_{2}\right)\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}}{{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|Q_{1}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}+{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|Q_{2}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\max\left\{{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{T}\left(\frac{Q_{1}}{{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|Q_{1}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}}\right)\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2},{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{T}\left(\frac{Q_{2}}{{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|Q_{2}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}}\right)\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}\right\}.

Thus at least one of
\frac{Q_{1}}{{\left|\kern-0.75346pt\left|\kern-0.75346pt\left|Q_{1}\right|\kern-0.75346pt\right|\kern-0.75346pt\right|}_{2}}
and
\frac{Q_{2}}{{\left|\kern-0.75346pt\left|\kern-0.75346pt\left|Q_{2}\right|\kern-0.75346pt\right|\kern-0.75346pt\right|}_{2}}
also achieves the maximum in Eq.
Eq.

26

∎

Lemma 3.11
.

Given quantum systems
A,B
with
\dim A=m_{A}
and
\dim B=m_{B}
, a bipartite quantum state
\psi_{AB}
with
\psi_{A}=\frac{\mathds{1}_{m_{A}}}{m_{A}}
and
\psi_{B}=\frac{\mathds{1}_{m_{B}}}{m_{B}}
, let
\left\{\mathcal{A}_{\sigma}\right\}_{\sigma\in[m_{A}^{2}]_{\geq 0}}
and
\left\{\mathcal{B}_{\sigma}\right\}_{\sigma\in[m_{B}^{2}]_{\geq 0}}
be standard orthonormal bases in
\mathcal{M}\left(A\right)
and
\mathcal{M}\left(B\right)
, respectively. It holds that

\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(\mathcal{A}_{\sigma}\otimes\mathcal{B}_{\tau}\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes n}\right)=0,

whenever
\mathrm{supp}\left(\sigma\right)\neq\mathrm{supp}\left(\tau\right)
. Thus

\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(A[S]\otimes B[T]\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes n}\right)=0,

whenever
S\neq T
, where
A[S]
and
B[T]
are obtained from the Efron-Stein decompositions of
A
and
B
in Definition
2.17
.

Proof.

For any
\sigma\in[m_{A}^{2}]_{\geq 0},\tau\in[m_{B}^{2}]_{\geq 0}
and
\sigma\neq 0
and
\tau\neq 0
, it holds that

\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(\mathcal{A}_{\sigma}\otimes\mathcal{B}_{0}\right)\psi_{AB}\right)=\frac{1}{m_{A}}\mathrm{Tr}~\mathcal{A}_{\sigma}=\left\langle\mathcal{A}_{0},\mathcal{A}_{\sigma}\right\rangle=0.

Symmetrically,

\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(\mathcal{A}_{0}\otimes\mathcal{B}_{\tau}\right)\psi_{AB}\right)=0
.
Thus, for any
\sigma\in[m_{A}^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{n}
and
\tau\in[m_{B}^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{n}
with
\mathrm{supp}\left(\sigma\right)\neq\mathrm{supp}\left(\tau\right)
, it holds that

\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(\mathcal{A}_{\sigma}\otimes\mathcal{B}_{\tau}\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes n}\right)=\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(\mathcal{A}_{\sigma_{i}}\otimes\mathcal{B}_{\tau_{i}}\right)\psi_{AB}\right)=0.

∎

Proposition 3.12
.

Given integers
n,m_{A},m_{B}>0
, quantum systems
A
and
B
, a bipartite quantum state
\psi_{AB}
with
\psi_{A}=\frac{\mathds{1}_{m_{A}}}{m_{A}}
and
\psi_{B}=\frac{\mathds{1}_{m_{B}}}{m_{B}}
,
Q\in\mathcal{M}\left(B^{n}\right)
and
S\subseteq[n]
, it holds that

\mathcal{T}\left(Q[S]\right)=\mathcal{T}\left(Q\right)[S],

where
\mathcal{T}:\mathcal{M}\left(B^{n}\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{M}\left(A^{n}\right)
is a Markov super-operator with respect to
\psi_{AB}^{\otimes n}
defined in
Definition

3.1
.

Proof.

It suffices to show that

\left\langle X,\mathcal{T}\left(Q[S]\right)\right\rangle=\left\langle X,\mathcal{T}\left(Q\right)[S]\right\rangle,

for any
X\in\mathcal{M}\left(A^{n}\right)
.
By the definition,

\text{LHS}=\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(X^{\dagger}\otimes Q[S]\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes n}\right).

By
Definition

2.17
and
Proposition

2.18
, we have

\text{RHS}=\left\langle X[S],\mathcal{T}\left(Q\right)\right\rangle=\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(X[S]^{\dagger}\otimes Q\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes n}\right).

By
Lemma

3.11
and the Efron-Stein decompositions of
X
and
Q
defined in
Definition

2.17
,

\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(X[S]^{\dagger}\otimes Q\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes n}\right)=\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(X^{\dagger}\otimes Q[S]\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes n}\right)=\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(X[S]^{\dagger}\otimes Q[S]\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes n}\right).

∎

Proposition 3.13
.

Given integers
n,m_{A},m_{B}>0
, quantum systems
A,B
, a bipartite quantum state
\psi_{AB}
with
\psi_{A}=\frac{\mathds{1}_{m_{A}}}{m_{A}}
and
\psi_{B}=\frac{\mathds{1}_{m_{B}}}{m_{B}}
,
Q\in\mathcal{M}\left(B^{n}\right)
and
S\subseteq[n]
, it holds that

{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{T}\left(Q[S]\right)\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}\leq\rho^{|S|}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|Q[S]\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2},

where
\rho=\rho\left(\psi_{AB}\right)
.

Proof.

We may assume that
Q=Q[S]
without loss of generality. It suffices to show the case that
S=[n]
. Let
\left\{\mathcal{A}_{i}\right\}_{i\in[m_{A}^{2}]_{\geq 0}}
and
\left\{\mathcal{B}_{i}\right\}_{i\in[m_{B}^{2}]_{\geq 0}}
be standard orthonormal bases in
\mathcal{M}\left(A\right)
and
\mathcal{M}\left(B\right)
, respectively. We use a hybrid argument over
n
registers. For
r\in[n]
, set
\mathcal{T}^{\left(r\right)}=\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{M}\left(A\right)}^{\otimes\left(r-1\right)}\otimes\mathcal{T}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{M}\left(B\right)}^{\otimes\left(n-r\right)}
,
Q^{(0)}=Q
and
Q^{\left(r\right)}=\mathcal{T}^{(r)}\left(Q^{(r-1)}\right)
, where
\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{M}\left(A\right)}
and
\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{M}\left(B\right)}
are the identity maps mapping
\mathcal{M}\left(A\right)
to
\mathcal{M}\left(A\right)
and
\mathcal{M}\left(B\right)
to
\mathcal{M}\left(B\right)
, respectively. By
Lemma

3.3
, we have
Q^{(n)}=\mathcal{T}\left(Q\right)
. By induction it suffices to show that

{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|Q^{(r)}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}\leq\rho^{2}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|Q^{(r-1)}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2},

(28)

and

Q^{(r)}=Q^{(r)}[[n]],

(29)

where
Q^{(r)}[[n]]
is defined by expanding
Q^{(r)}
over

\left\{\mathcal{A}_{\sigma_{\leq r}}\otimes\mathcal{B}_{\sigma_{>r}}\right\}_{\sigma\in[m_{A}^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{r}\times[m_{B}^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{n-r}},

because
\mathcal{T}=\mathcal{T}^{(n)}\circ\cdots\circ\mathcal{T}^{\left(1\right)}
.
Let
\left\{\left|u_{i}\right\rangle\right\}_{i\in[m_{A}]}
and
\left\{\left|v_{i}\right\rangle\right\}_{i\in[m_{B}]}
be orthonormal bases of
{\mathbb{C}}^{m_{A}}
and
{\mathbb{C}}^{m_{B}}
, respectively. For any
s\in[m_{A}]^{r-1}\times[m_{B}]^{n-r}
, we define

\left|w_{s}\right\rangle=\left|u_{s_{1}}\right\rangle\otimes\ldots\otimes\left|u_{s_{r-1}}\right\rangle\otimes\left|v_{s_{r+1}}\right\rangle\otimes\ldots\otimes\left|v_{s_{n}}\right\rangle.

For any
s,t\in[m_{A}]^{r-1}\times[m_{B}]^{n-r}
, we define

P^{(r)}_{s,t}=\left(\left\langle w_{s}\right|\otimes\mathds{1}\right)Q^{(r)}\left(\left|w_{t}\right\rangle\otimes\mathds{1}\right)~\mbox{and}~Q^{(r-1)}_{s,t}=\left(\left\langle w_{s}\right|\otimes\mathds{1}\right)Q^{(r-1)}\left(\left|w_{t}\right\rangle\otimes\mathds{1}\right),

where
\left|w_{s}\right\rangle
and
\left|w_{t}\right\rangle
lie in the registers
\left\{1,\ldots,r-1,r+1,\ldots,n\right\}
. Note that
\mathcal{T}^{\left(r\right)}
applies
\mathcal{T}
to the
r
-th register and leaves other registers unchanged. Then
Q^{\left(r\right)}=\mathcal{T}^{\left(r\right)}\left(Q^{\left(r-1\right)}\right)
implies that

P^{(r)}_{s,t}=\mathcal{T}\left(Q_{s,t}^{(r-1)}\right).

(30)

And we also have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}~Q_{s,t}^{(r-1)}=0,

by the induction
Q^{(r-1)}=Q^{(r-1)}[n]
.
By
Proposition

3.10
,
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|P^{(r)}_{s,t}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}\leq\rho{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|Q^{(r-1)}_{s,t}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}
.
Consider

\displaystyle\sum_{s,t}\left\|P^{(r)}_{s,t}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{s,t}\mathrm{Tr}~\left(P^{(r)}_{s,t}\right)^{\dagger}P^{(r)}_{s,t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{s,t}\mathrm{Tr}~\left(\left\langle w_{t}\right|\otimes\mathds{1}\right)\left(Q^{(r)}\right)^{\dagger}\left(\left|w_{s}\right\rangle\otimes\mathds{1}\right)\left(\left\langle w_{s}\right|\otimes\mathds{1}\right)Q^{\left(r\right)}\left(\left|w_{t}\right\rangle\otimes\mathds{1}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{t}\mathrm{Tr}~\left(\left\langle w_{t}\right|\otimes\mathds{1}\right)\left(Q^{(r)}\right)^{\dagger}Q^{\left(r\right)}\left(\left|w_{t}\right\rangle\otimes\mathds{1}\right)\hskip 11.38109pt(\mbox{because $\sum_{s}\left|w_{s}\middle\rangle\middle\langle w_{s}\right|=\mathds{1}$})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}~\left(Q^{(r)}\right)^{\dagger}Q^{(r)}

Similarly,

\sum_{s,t}\left\|Q^{(r-1)}_{s,t}\right\|_{2}^{2}=\mathrm{Tr}~\left(Q^{(r-1)}\right)^{\dagger}Q^{(r-1)}.

Combining with Eq.
Eq.

30
and
Proposition

3.10
, we conclude Eq.
Eq.

28
.

For Eq.
Eq.

29
, compute

\displaystyle\widehat{Q^{(r)}}\left(\sigma\right)=\widehat{\mathcal{T}^{\left(r\right)}\left(Q^{(r-1)}\right)}\left(\sigma\right)=\left\langle\mathcal{A}_{\sigma_{\leq r}}\otimes\mathcal{B}_{\sigma_{>r}},\mathcal{T}^{\left(r\right)}\left(Q^{(r-1)}\right)\right\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\tau:\left|\tau\right|=n}\widehat{Q^{(r-1)}}\left(\tau\right)\left\langle\mathcal{A}_{\sigma_{\leq r}}\otimes\mathcal{B}_{\sigma_{>r}},\mathcal{T}^{\left(r\right)}\left(\mathcal{A}_{\tau_{<r}}\otimes\mathcal{B}_{\tau_{\geq r}}\right)\right\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\tau:\left|\tau\right|=n,\tau_{-r}=\sigma_{-r}}\widehat{Q^{(r-1)}}\left(\tau\right)\left\langle\mathcal{A}_{\sigma_{r}},\mathcal{T}\left(\mathcal{B}_{\tau_{r}}\right)\right\rangle.

Note that

\left\langle\mathcal{A}_{0},\mathcal{T}\left(\mathcal{B}_{\tau_{r}}\right)\right\rangle=\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(\mathds{1}\otimes\mathcal{B}_{\tau_{r}}\right)\psi_{AB}\right)=0,

as
\left|\tau\right|=n
. Therefore,
\widehat{\mathcal{T}^{\left(r\right)}\left(Q\right)}\left(\sigma\right)=0
if
\left|\sigma\right|<n
. We conclude Eq.
Eq.

29
.
∎

A useful property of (classical) maximal correlation coefficients is
tensorization
, which states that the maximal correlation of multiple independent identical copies of a distribution is the same as the one of one copy. The same property also holds for the quantum maximal correlation shown by Beigi
[
9
]
. Here we provide a different proof.

Fact 3.14
.

[
9
]
Given quantum systems
A,B
with
\dim A=m_{A}
and
\dim B=m_{B}
, a bipartite quantum state
\psi_{AB}
with
\psi_{A}=\frac{\mathds{1}_{m_{A}}}{m_{A}}
and
\psi_{B}=\frac{\mathds{1}_{m_{B}}}{m_{B}}
, it holds that

\rho\left(\psi_{AB}^{\otimes n}\right)=\rho\left(\psi_{AB}\right).

Proof.

Given
Q\in\mathcal{M}\left(\mathcal{B}^{n}\right)
with
\mathrm{Tr}~Q=0
and
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|Q\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}=1
, we use the Efron-Stein decomposition
Q=\sum_{S\neq\emptyset}Q[S]
to obtain

\displaystyle{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{T}\left(Q\right)\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\sum_{S\neq\emptyset}\mathcal{T}\left(Q[S]\right)\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}\quad\quad\mbox{(linearity of $\mathcal{T}$)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\sum_{S\neq\emptyset}\mathcal{T}\left(Q\right)[S]\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}\quad\quad\mbox{(\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{prop:markovenfronstein})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{S\neq\emptyset}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{T}\left(Q\right)[S]\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}\quad\quad\mbox{(orthogonality of the Efron-Stein decomposition)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{S\neq\emptyset}\rho^{2\left|S\right|}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|Q[S]\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}\quad\quad\mbox{(\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{prop:markovoperatornorm})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\rho^{2}\sum_{S\neq\emptyset}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|Q[S]\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\rho^{2}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|Q\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}\quad\quad\mbox{(orthogonality of the Efron-Stein decomposition)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\rho^{2}.

From
Proposition

3.10
,
\rho\left(\psi^{\otimes n}_{AB}\right)\leq\rho\left(\psi_{AB}\right)
. The other direction trivially follows by definition.
∎

4
Main results

Theorem 4.1
.

Let
\epsilon\in(0,1)
, integers
n,m,a,b,t>0
,
\psi_{AB}
be a noisy MES. Namely,
\psi_{A}=\psi_{B}=\frac{\mathds{1}_{m}}{m}
. Let
\rho=\rho\left(\psi_{AB}\right)<1
be the maximal correlation of
\psi_{AB}
defined in
Definition

3.6
. Then there exists an explicitly computable
D=D\left(\rho,\epsilon,a,b,m,t\right)
, such that for any sequences of POVMs

\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{P_{1}}},\ldots,\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{P_{a}}}~\mbox{and}~\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{Q_{1}}},\ldots,\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{Q_{b}}},

where

\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{P_{u}}}=\left(P_{u,1},\ldots,P_{u,t}\right)~\mbox{and}~\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{Q_{v}}}=\left(Q_{v,1},\ldots,Q_{v,t}\right)

and
P_{u,i},Q_{v,j}\in\mathcal{H}_{m}^{\otimes n}
for
u\in[a],v\in[b],i,j\in[t]
, there exist sequences of positive semidefinite operators

\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{\widetilde{P}_{1}}},\ldots,\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{\widetilde{P}_{a}}}~\mbox{and}~\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{\widetilde{Q}_{1}}},\ldots,\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{\widetilde{Q}_{b}}},

where

\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{\widetilde{P}_{u}}}=\left(\widetilde{P}_{u,1}\ldots,\widetilde{P}_{u,t}\right)~\mbox{and}~\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{\widetilde{Q}_{v}}}=\left(\widetilde{Q}_{v,1},\ldots,\widetilde{Q}_{v,t}\right)

for
\widetilde{P}_{u,i},\widetilde{Q}_{v,j}\in\mathcal{H}_{m}^{\otimes D}
,
u\in[a],v\in[b],i,j\in[t]
, such that the following holds.

1.

For
u\in[a],v\in[b]
,
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{\widetilde{P}_{u}}},\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{\widetilde{Q}_{v}}}
are sub-POVMs. Namely,

\sum_{j=1}^{t}\widetilde{P}_{u,j}\leq\mathds{1},\sum_{j=1}^{t}\widetilde{Q}_{v,j}\leq\mathds{1}~\mbox{and}~\widetilde{P}_{u,j}\geq 0,\widetilde{Q}_{v,j}\geq 0~\mbox{for}~j\in[t].

2.

For any
u\in[a],v\in[b],i,j\in[t]
,

\left|\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(P_{u,i}\otimes Q_{v,j}\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes n}\right)-\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(\widetilde{P}_{u,i}\otimes\widetilde{Q}_{v,j}\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes D}\right)\right|\leq\epsilon.

In particular, one may choose

D=\exp\left(\mathrm{poly}\left(a,b,t,\exp\left(\mathrm{poly}\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon},\frac{1}{1-\rho},\log m\right)\right)\right)\right).

Remark 4.2
.

Here we assume for simplicity that the sizes of the output sets of the measurements on both sides are same. However, the same argument also holds when they are different by setting
t
to be the larger one.

The proof is deferred to the end of this section. The following is an application of
Theorem

4.1
to the decidability of nonlocal games.

Theorem 4.3
.

Given parameters
0<\epsilon,\rho<1
, an integer
m\geq 2
, a noisy MES state
\psi_{AB}
, i.e.,
\psi_{A}=\psi_{B}=\frac{\mathds{1}_{m}}{m}
with the maximal correlation
\rho=\rho\left(\psi_{AB}\right)<1

as defined in
Definition

3.6
, let
G
be a nonlocal game with the question sets
\mathcal{X},\mathcal{Y}
and the answer sets
\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B}
. Suppose the players share arbitrarily many copies of
\psi_{AB}
. Let
\omega^{*}(G,\psi_{AB})
be the supremum of the winning probability that the players can achieve. Then there exists an explicitly computable bound
D=D\left(\left|\mathcal{X}\right|,\left|\mathcal{Y}\right|,\left|\mathcal{A}\right|,\left|\mathcal{B}\right|,m,\epsilon,\rho\right)
such that it suffices for the players to share
D
copies of
\psi_{AB}
to achieve the winning probability at least
\omega^{*}(G,\psi_{AB})-\epsilon
. In particular, one may choose

D=\exp\left(\mathrm{poly}\left(\left|\mathcal{X}\right|,\left|\mathcal{Y}\right|,\exp\left(\mathrm{poly}\left(\left|\mathcal{A}\right|,\left|\mathcal{B}\right|,\frac{1}{\epsilon},\frac{1}{1-\rho}\right),\log m\right)\right)\right).

Proof.

We assume that
\left|\mathcal{A}\right|=\left|B\right|=t
without loss of generality. Suppose the players share
n
copies of
\psi_{AB}
and employ the strategies

\left(\left\{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{P_{x}}}=\left(P_{x,1},\ldots,P_{x,t}\right)\right\}_{x\in\mathcal{X}},\left\{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{Q_{y}}}=\left(Q_{y,1},\ldots,Q_{y,t}\right)\right\}_{y\in\mathcal{Y}}\right)

with the winning probability
\omega
. For simplicity, we set
\mathcal{X}=\left\{1,2,\ldots,\left|\mathcal{X}\right|\right\}
and
\mathcal{Y}=\left\{1,2,\ldots,\left|\mathcal{Y}\right|\right\}
.

We apply
Theorem

4.1
to the following two sequences of POVMs

\left(\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{P_{1}}},\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{P_{2}}},\ldots\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{P_{\left|\mathcal{X}\right|}}}\right)~\mbox{and}~\left(\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{Q_{1}}},\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{Q_{2}}},\ldots,\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{Q_{\left|\mathcal{Y}\right|}}}\right)

with parameters
\epsilon\leftarrow\epsilon/t^{2},t\leftarrow t
. Let
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{\widetilde{P}_{1}}},\ldots,\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{\widetilde{P}_{|\mathcal{X}|}}},\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{\widetilde{Q}_{1}}},\ldots,\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{\widetilde{Q}_{|Y|}}}
be the sequences sub-POVMs induced by
Theorem

4.1
.
We claim that the strategy

\left(\left\{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{\widetilde{P_{x}}}}\right\}_{x\in\mathcal{X}},\left\{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{\widetilde{Q_{y}}}}\right\}_{y\in\mathcal{Y}}\right)

wins the game with probability
\widetilde{\omega}\geq\omega-\epsilon
. Moreover, they can be easily converted to valid POVMs without lowering the winning probability.

Let

\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{\widetilde{P}_{x}}}=\left\{\widetilde{P}_{x,1},\ldots,\widetilde{P}_{x,t}\right\},\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{\widetilde{Q}_{y}}}=\left\{\widetilde{Q}_{y,1},\ldots,\widetilde{Q}_{y,t}\right\}

and

\nu_{xy}\left(a,b\right)=\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(P_{x,a}\otimes Q_{y,b}\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes n}\right)

and

\widetilde{\nu}_{xy}\left(a,b\right)=\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(\widetilde{P}_{x,a}\otimes\widetilde{Q}_{y,b}\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes D}\right).

From
Theorem

4.1
, for any
(x,y,a,b)\in\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\times[t]\times[t]
,

\displaystyle\left|\nu_{xy}\left(a,b\right)-\widetilde{\nu}_{xy}\left(a,b\right)\right|\leq\epsilon/t^{2}

Thus

\displaystyle\left|\omega-\tilde{\omega}\right|=\left|\sum_{xy}\mu\left(x,y\right)\left(\nu_{xy}\left(a,b\right)-\widetilde{\nu}_{xy}\left(a,b\right)\right)V(x,y,a,b)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{xy}\mu\left(x,y\right)\sum_{a,b}\left|\nu_{xy}\left(a,b\right)-\widetilde{\nu}_{xy}\left(a,b\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\epsilon.

∎

Remark 4.4
.

Theorem

4.1
and
Theorem

4.3
can be directly generalized to the case when
\psi_{A}=\frac{\mathds{1}_{m_{A}}}{m_{A}}
,
\psi_{B}=\frac{\mathds{1}_{m_{B}}}{m_{B}}
and
m_{A}\neq m_{B}
.

To prove
Theorem

4.1
, we need the following intermediate lemma for technical reasons.

Lemma 4.5
.

With the same setting of
Theorem

4.1
, the following holds.

1.

For
1\leq u\leq s
,
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{\widetilde{P}_{u}}},\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{\widetilde{Q}_{u}}}
are sub-POVMs. Namely,

\sum_{j=1}^{t}\widetilde{P}_{u,j}\leq\mathds{1},\sum_{j=1}^{t}\widetilde{Q}_{u,j}\leq\mathds{1}~\mbox{and}~\widetilde{P}_{u,j}\geq 0,\widetilde{Q}_{u,j}\geq 0~\mbox{for}~j\in[t].

2.

For any
u\in[s],i,j\in[t]
,

\left|\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(P_{u,i}\otimes Q_{u,j}\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes n}\right)-\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(\widetilde{P}_{u,i}\otimes\widetilde{Q}_{u,j}\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes D}\right)\right|\leq\epsilon.

3.

If
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{P_{u}}}=\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{P_{v}}}
, then
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{\widetilde{P}_{u}}}=\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{\widetilde{P}_{v}}}
. If
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{Q_{u}}}=\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{Q_{v}}}
, then
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{\widetilde{Q}_{u}}}=\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{\widetilde{Q}_{v}}}
.

Proof of
Theorem

4.1
.

Applying Lemma
4.5
to the following two sequences of POVMs

\left(\underbrace{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{P_{1}}},\ldots,\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{P_{1}}}}_{b~\text{times}},\underbrace{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{P_{2}}},\ldots,\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{P_{2}}}}_{b~\text{times}},\ldots,\underbrace{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{P_{a}}},\ldots,\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{P_{a}}}}_{b~\text{times}}\right)

and

\left(\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{Q_{1}}},\ldots,\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{Q_{b}}},\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{Q_{1}}},\ldots,\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{Q_{b}}},\ldots,\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{Q_{1}}},\ldots,\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{Q_{b}}}\right)

with parameters
n\leftarrow n,\rho\leftarrow\rho,\epsilon\leftarrow\epsilon,a\leftarrow ab,b\leftarrow ab,t\leftarrow t
, we obtain

\left(\underbrace{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{\widetilde{P_{1}}}},\ldots,\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{\widetilde{P_{1}}}}}_{b~\text{times}},\underbrace{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{\widetilde{P_{2}}}},\ldots,\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{\widetilde{P_{2}}}}}_{b~\text{times}},\ldots,\underbrace{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{\widetilde{P_{a}}}},\ldots,\widetilde{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{P_{a}}}}}_{b~\text{times}}\right)

and

\left(\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{\widetilde{Q_{1}}}},\ldots,\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{\widetilde{Q_{b}}}},\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{\widetilde{Q_{1}}}},\ldots,\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{\widetilde{Q_{b}}}},\ldots,\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{\widetilde{Q_{1}}}},\ldots,\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{\widetilde{Q_{b}}}}\right)

guaranteed by Item 3 of
Lemma

4.5
. Item 1 and Item 2 in
Theorem

4.1
follow by Item 1 and Item 2 in
Lemma

4.5
.
∎

It remains to show
Lemma

4.5
.

Proof of
Lemma

4.5
.

Let
\delta,\tau
be parameters which are chosen later. The proof is composed of several steps.

•

Smooth operators
. For
u\in[s],i\in[t]
, we apply the map
f
implied by
Lemma

6.1
to
\left\{P_{u,i}\right\}_{u\in[s],i\in[t]}
and
\left\{Q_{u,i}\right\}_{u\in[s],i\in[t]}
to get
\left\{P_{u,i}^{(1)}\right\}_{u\in[s],i\in[t]}
and
\left\{Q_{u,i}^{(1)}\right\}_{u\in[s],i\in[t]}
, respectively, and
d_{1}=\frac{2\log^{2}(1/\delta)}{C\left(1-\rho\right)\delta}
for some constant
C
satisfying the following.

1.

\left\{P_{u,i}^{(1)}\right\}_{1\leq i\leq t}
and
\left\{Q_{u,i}^{(1)}\right\}_{1\leq i\leq t}
are POVMs for
1\leq u\leq s
, which is implied by item 5 in
Lemma

6.1
.

2.

For any
i\in[t],u\in[s]
,

{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|P_{u,i}^{(1)}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}\leq{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|P_{u,i}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}~\mbox{and}~{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|Q_{u,i}^{(1)}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}\leq{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|Q_{u,i}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}.

3.

For any
i,j\in[t],u\in[s]
,

\left|\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(P_{u,i}^{(1)}\otimes\ Q^{(1)}_{u,j}\right)\psi^{\otimes n}_{AB}\right)-\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(P_{u,i}\otimes Q_{u,j}\right)\psi^{\otimes n}_{AB}\right)\right|\leq\delta.

4.

For any
i\in[t],u\in[s]
,

{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\left(P_{u,i}^{(1)}\right)^{>d_{1}}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}^{2}_{2}\leq\delta~\mbox{and}~{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\left(Q_{u,i}^{(1)}\right)^{>d_{1}}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}^{2}_{2}\leq\delta.

5.

If
P_{u,i}=P_{v,i}
, then
P_{u,i}^{(1)}=P_{v,i}^{(1)}
. If
Q_{u,i}=Q_{v,i}
, then
Q_{u,i}^{(1)}=Q_{v,i}^{(1)}
.

•

Regularization
. For any
u\in[s]
applying
Lemma

7.4
to
P_{u,i}^{(1)}
and
Q_{u,i}^{(1)}
with
\delta\leftarrow\delta,\epsilon\leftarrow\tau,d\leftarrow d_{1}
, we obtain sets
H_{u,i}\subseteq[n]
of size
\left|H_{u,i}\right|\leq\frac{2d_{1}}{\tau}
such that for all
i\in[t]

\left(\forall r\notin H_{u,i}\right)~\mathrm{Inf}_{r}\left(\left(P_{u,i}^{(1)}\right)^{\leq d_{1}}\right)\leq\tau,~\mbox{and}~\mathrm{Inf}_{r}\left(\left(Q_{u,i}^{(1)}\right)^{\leq d_{1}}\right)\leq\tau.

Set
H=\bigcup_{u\in[s],i\in[t]}H_{u,i}
. Then
h=\left|H\right|\leq\frac{2std_{1}}{\tau}
. Without loss of generality, assume
H=[h]
. It holds that for any
u\in[s]

1.

\left(\forall i\in[t],r\notin H\right)~\mathrm{Inf}_{r}\left(\left(P_{u,i}^{(1)}\right)^{\leq d_{1}}\right)\leq\tau,~\mbox{and}~\mathrm{Inf}_{r}\left(\left(Q_{u,i}^{(1)}\right)^{\leq d_{1}}\right)\leq\tau;

2.

\forall i,j\in[t]
, we have

\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(P_{u,i}^{(1)}\otimes Q_{u,j}^{(1)}\right)\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes n}=\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}c_{\sigma}\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(P_{u,i,\sigma}^{(1)}\otimes Q_{u,j,\sigma}^{(1)}\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes(n-h)}\right),

where

\left(c_{i}\right)_{i=0}^{m^{2}-1}
are the singular values of the matrix
\mathsf{Corr}\left(\psi_{AB}\right)
in non-increasing order defined in
Definition

7.1
,
c_{\sigma}=c_{\sigma_{1}}\cdot c_{\sigma_{2}}\cdots c_{\sigma_{h}}
and

P_{u,i,\sigma}^{(1)}=\sum_{\tau\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{n}:\tau_{H}=\sigma}\widehat{P_{u,i}^{(1)}}\left(\tau\right)\mathcal{A}_{\tau_{H^{c}}}

Q_{u,j,\sigma}^{(1)}=\sum_{\tau\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{n}:\tau_{H}=\sigma}\widehat{Q_{u,j}^{(1)}}\left(\tau\right)\mathcal{B}_{\tau_{H^{c}}},

for some standard orthonormal bases
\left\{\mathcal{A}_{i}\right\}_{i=0}^{m^{2}-1}
and
\left\{\mathcal{B}_{i}\right\}_{i=0}^{m^{2}-1}
.

•

Invariance from
\mathcal{H}_{m}^{\otimes n}
to
L^{2}\left(\mathcal{H}_{m}^{\otimes h},\gamma_{2(m^{2}-1)\left(n-h\right)}\right)
. For any
u\in[s]
, applying
Lemma

10.5
to
P_{u,i}^{(1)}
’s and
Q_{u,i}^{(1)}
’s and
H
, we obtain joint random operators

\left(\mathbf{P}_{u,i}^{(2)},\mathbf{Q}_{u,j}^{(2)}\right)\in L^{2}\left(\mathcal{M}_{m}^{\otimes h},\gamma_{2(m^{2}-1)\left(n-h\right)}\right)\times L^{2}\left(\mathcal{M}_{m}^{\otimes h},\gamma_{2(m^{2}-1)\left(n-h\right)}\right)

for
1\leq i,j\leq 2(m^{2}-1)(n-h)
with same joint random variables
\left(\mathbf{g}_{i},\mathbf{h}_{i}\right)_{i=1}^{2(m^{2}-1)\left(n-h\right)}\sim\mathcal{G}_{\rho}^{\otimes 2(m^{2}-1)(n-h)}
such that the followings hold.

1.

\mathbf{P}_{u,i}^{(2)}
and
\mathbf{Q}_{u,i}^{(2)}
are both multilinear and of degree at most
d_{1}
for
u\in[s],i\in[t]
.

2.

\sum_{i=1}^{t}\mathbf{P}_{u,i}^{(2)}=\mathds{1}
and
\sum_{i=1}^{t}\mathbf{Q}_{u,i}^{(2)}=\mathds{1}
for
u\in[s]
.

3.

For any
u\in[s],i\in[t]

N_{2}\left(\mathbf{P}_{u,i}^{(2)}\right)\leq{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|P_{u,i}^{(1)}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2},N_{2}\left(\mathbf{Q}_{u,i}^{(2)}\right)\leq{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|Q_{u,i}^{(1)}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}

4.

For any
u\in[s],i,j\in[t]

\left|\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(P_{u,i}^{(1)}\otimes Q_{u,j}^{(1)}\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes n}\right)-\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~\left(\left(\mathbf{P}_{u,i}^{(2)}\otimes\mathbf{Q}_{u,j}^{(2)}\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes h}\right)\right]\right|\leq\delta

5.

For any
u,v\in[s]

\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\frac{1}{m^{h}}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(\mathbf{P}_{u,i}^{(2)}\right)\right]\leq O\left(t\left(\left(3^{d_{1}}m^{d_{1}/2}\sqrt{\tau}d_{1}\right)^{2/3}+\sqrt{\delta}\right)\right)

\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\frac{1}{m^{h}}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(\mathbf{Q}_{u,i}^{(2)}\right)\right]\leq O\left(t\left(\left(3^{d_{1}}m^{d_{1}/2}\sqrt{\tau}d_{1}\right)^{2/3}+\sqrt{\delta}\right)\right)

6.

If
P_{u,i}^{(1)}=P_{v,i}^{(1)}
, then
\mathbf{P}_{u,i}^{(2)}=\mathbf{P}_{v,i}^{(2)}
. If
Q_{u,i}^{(1)}=Q_{v,i}^{(1)}
, then
\mathbf{Q}_{u,i}^{(2)}=\mathbf{Q}_{v,i}^{(2)}
.

•

Dimension reduction
. For any
u\in[s],i,j\in[t]
, applying
Lemma

11.1
to
\left(\mathbf{P}_{u,i}^{(2)},\mathbf{Q}_{v,j}^{(2)}\right)
with
\delta\leftarrow\delta/2st^{2},d\leftarrow d_{1},n\leftarrow 2(m^{2}-1)(n-h),\alpha\leftarrow 1/9st^{2}
, if we sample
\mathbf{M}\sim\gamma_{n\times n_{0}}
, then items 1 to 3 in
Lemma

11.1
hold for
f_{\mathbf{M}}\left(\mathbf{P}_{u,i}^{(2)}\right)
,
f_{\mathbf{M}}\left(\mathbf{Q}_{u,i}^{(2)}\right)
with probability at least
1-\delta/2st^{2}-2/9st^{2}
, where
f_{\mathbf{M}}
is the function introduced in
Lemma

11.1
. Further applying a union bound on
u\in[s],i,j\in[t]
, we obtain that items 1 to 3 in
Lemma

11.1
hold with probability at least
7/9-\delta/2>0
. Thus we have joint random operators
\left(\mathbf{P}_{u,i}^{(3)},\mathbf{Q}_{u,i}^{(3)}\right)\in L^{2}\left(\mathcal{H}_{m}^{\otimes h},\gamma_{n_{0}}\right)\times L^{2}\left(\mathcal{H}_{m}^{\otimes h},\gamma_{n_{0}}\right)
with the joint random variables drawn from
\mathcal{G}_{\rho}^{\otimes n_{0}}
such that the following holds for all
u\in[s]
.

1.

\sum_{i=1}^{t}\mathbf{P}_{u,i}^{(3)}=\mathds{1}
and
\sum_{i=1}^{t}\mathbf{Q}_{u,i}^{(3)}=\mathds{1}
.

2.

For all
i\in[t]
,

N_{2}\left(\mathbf{P}_{u,i}^{(3)}\right)\leq(1+\delta)N_{2}\left(\mathbf{P}_{u,i}^{(2)}\right)~\mbox{and}~N_{2}\left(\mathbf{Q}_{u,i}^{(3)}\right)\leq(1+\delta)N_{2}\left(\mathbf{Q}_{u,i}^{(2)}\right).

3.

\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\frac{1}{m^{h}}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(\mathbf{P}_{u,i}^{(3)}\right)\right]\leq 3t\sqrt{s}\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\frac{1}{m^{h}}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(\mathbf{P}_{u,i}^{(2)}\right)\right];

\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\frac{1}{m^{h}}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(\mathbf{Q}_{u,i}^{(3)}\right)\right]\leq 3t\sqrt{s}\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\frac{1}{m^{h}}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(\mathbf{Q}_{u,i}^{(2)}\right)\right].

4.

For all
i,j\in[t]
,

\left|\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(\mathbf{P}_{u,i}^{(3)}\otimes\mathbf{Q}_{u,j}^{(3)}\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes h}\right)\right]-\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(\mathbf{P}_{u,i}^{(2)}\otimes\mathbf{Q}_{u,j}^{(2)}\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes h}\right)\right]\right|\\
\leq\delta N_{2}\left(\mathbf{P}_{u,i}^{(2)}\right)N_{2}\left(\mathbf{Q}_{u,j}^{(2)}\right).

5.

If
\mathbf{P}_{u,i}^{(2)}=\mathbf{P}_{v,i}^{(2)}
, then
\mathbf{P}_{u,i}^{(3)}=\mathbf{P}_{v,i}^{(3)}
.
If
\mathbf{Q}_{u,i}^{(2)}=\mathbf{Q}_{v,i}^{(2)}
, then
\mathbf{Q}_{u,i}^{(3)}=\mathbf{Q}_{v,i}^{(3)}
.

Here
n_{0}=\frac{m^{O(h)}d_{1}^{O\left(d_{1}\right)}s^{6}t^{12}}{\delta^{6}}
.

Smooth random operators
. For any
u\in[s]
, we apply
Lemma

12.1
to
\left(\mathbf{P}_{u,i}^{(3)},\mathbf{Q}_{u,i}^{(3)}\right)
with
h\leftarrow h,n\leftarrow n_{0}
and obtain joint random operators
\left(\mathbf{P}_{u,i}^{(4)},\mathbf{Q}_{u,i}^{(4)}\right)\in L^{2}\left(\mathcal{H}_{m}^{\otimes h},\gamma_{n_{0}}\right)\times L^{2}\left(\mathcal{H}_{m}^{\otimes h},\gamma_{n_{0}}\right)
such that the following holds.

1.

\sum_{i=1}^{t}\mathbf{P}_{u,i}^{(4)}=\mathds{1}
and
\sum_{i=1}^{t}\mathbf{Q}_{u,i}^{(4)}=\mathds{1}
.

2.

For all
i\in[t]
,
\deg\left(\mathbf{P}_{u,i}^{(4)}\right)\leq d_{2}
and
\deg\left(\mathbf{Q}_{u,i}^{(4)}\right)\leq d_{2}
.

3.

For all
i\in[t]
,
N_{2}\left(\mathbf{P}_{u,i}^{(4)}\right)\leq N_{2}\left(\mathbf{P}_{u,i}^{(3)}\right)
and
N_{2}\left(\mathbf{Q}_{u,i}^{(4)}\right)\leq N_{2}\left(\mathbf{Q}_{u,i}^{(3)}\right)
.

4.

\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\frac{1}{m^{h}}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(\mathbf{P}_{u,i}^{(4)}\right)\right]\\
\leq 2\left(\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\frac{1}{m^{h}}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(\mathbf{P}_{u,i}^{(3)}\right)\right]+\delta\left(\sum_{i=1}^{t}N_{2}\left(\mathbf{P}_{u,i}^{(3)}\right)^{2}\right)\right);

\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\frac{1}{m^{h}}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(\mathbf{Q}_{u,i}^{(4)}\right)\right]\\
\leq 2\left(\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\frac{1}{m^{h}}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(\mathbf{Q}_{u,i}^{(3)}\right)\right]+\delta\left(\sum_{i=1}^{t}N_{2}\left(\mathbf{Q}_{u,i}^{(3)}\right)^{2}\right)\right).

5.

For all
i,j\in[t]
,

\left|\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(\mathbf{P}_{u,i}^{(4)}\otimes\mathbf{Q}_{u,j}^{(4)}\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes h}\right)\right]-\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(\mathbf{P}_{u,i}^{(3)}\otimes\mathbf{Q}_{u,j}^{(3)}\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes h}\right)\right]\right|\\
\leq\delta N_{2}\left(\mathbf{P}_{u,i}^{(3)}\right)N_{2}\left(\mathbf{Q}_{u,j}^{(3)}\right).

6.

If
\mathbf{P}_{u,i}^{(3)}=\mathbf{P}_{v,i}^{(3)}
, then
\mathbf{P}_{u,i}^{(4)}=\mathbf{P}_{v,i}^{(4)}
.
If
\mathbf{Q}_{u,i}^{(3)}=\mathbf{Q}_{v,i}^{(3)}
, then
\mathbf{Q}_{u,i}^{(4)}=\mathbf{Q}_{v,i}^{(4)}
.

Here
d_{2}=O\left(\frac{\log^{2}\frac{1}{\delta}}{\delta\left(1-\rho\right)}\right)
.

Multilinearization
. For any
u\in[s]
, suppose

\left(\mathbf{P}_{u,i}^{(4)},\mathbf{Q}_{u,i}^{(4)}\right)=\left(\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}p^{(4)}_{u,i,\sigma}\left(\mathbf{g}\right)\mathcal{A}_{\sigma},\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}q^{(4)}_{u,i,\sigma}\left(\mathbf{h}\right)\mathcal{B}_{\sigma}\right),

where
\left(\mathbf{g},\mathbf{h}\right)\sim\mathcal{G}_{\rho}^{\otimes n_{0}}
.
Applying
Lemma

13.1
with
d\leftarrow d_{2},h\leftarrow h,n\leftarrow n_{0},\delta\leftarrow\tau
, we obtain the joint random operators

\left(\mathbf{P}_{u,i}^{(5)},\mathbf{Q}_{u,i}^{(5)}\right)=\left(\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}p^{(5)}_{u,i,\sigma}\left(\mathbf{x}\right)\mathcal{A}_{\sigma},\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}q^{(5)}_{u,i,\sigma}\left(\mathbf{y}\right)\mathcal{B}_{\sigma}\right)

where
{\left(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}\right)\sim\mathcal{G}_{\rho}^{\otimes n_{0}n_{1}}}
, with
n_{1}=O\left(\frac{d_{2}^{2}}{\tau^{2}}\right)
such that the following holds.

1.

\mathbf{P}_{u,i}^{(5)}
and
\mathbf{Q}_{u,i}^{(5)}
are both multilinear for
1\leq i\leq t
.

2.

\sum_{i=1}^{t}\mathbf{P}_{u,i}^{(5)}=\mathds{1}
and
\sum_{i=1}^{t}\mathbf{Q}_{u,i}^{(5)}=\mathds{1}
.

3.

For all
i\in[t]
, both
\deg\left(\mathbf{P}_{u,i}^{(5)}\right)
and
\deg\left(\mathbf{Q}_{u,i}^{(5)}\right)
are at most
d_{2}
.

4.

For all
\left(i,j,k\right)\in[t]\times[n_{0}]\times[n_{1}]
and
\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}
,

\mathrm{Inf}_{(j-1)n_{1}+k}\left(p^{(5)}_{u,i,\sigma}\right)\leq\tau\cdot\mathrm{Inf}_{j}\left(p^{(4)}_{u,i,\sigma}\right),

\mathrm{Inf}_{(j-1)n_{1}+k}\left(q^{(5)}_{u,i,\sigma}\right)\leq\tau\cdot\mathrm{Inf}_{j}\left(q^{(4)}_{u,i,\sigma}\right).

5.

For all
i\in[t]
,
N_{2}\left(\mathbf{P}_{u,i}^{(5)}\right)\leq N_{2}\left(\mathbf{P}_{u,i}^{(4)}\right)~\mbox{and}~N_{2}\left(\mathbf{Q}_{u,i}^{(5)}\right)\leq N_{2}\left(\mathbf{Q}_{u,i}^{(4)}\right).

6.

\left|\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\frac{1}{m^{h}}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(\mathbf{P}_{u,i}^{(5)}\right)\right]-\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\frac{1}{m^{h}}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(\mathbf{P}_{u,i}^{(4)}\right)\right]\right|\\
\leq 4\tau\left(\sum_{i=1}^{t}N_{2}\left(\mathbf{P}_{u,i}^{(4)}\right)^{2}\right);

\left|\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\frac{1}{m^{h}}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(\mathbf{Q}_{u,i}^{(5)}\right)\right]-\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\frac{1}{m^{h}}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(\mathbf{Q}_{u,i}^{(4)}\right)\right]\right|\\
\leq 4\tau\left(\sum_{i=1}^{t}N_{2}\left(\mathbf{Q}_{u,i}^{(4)}\right)^{2}\right).

7.

For all
i,j\in[t]
,

\left|\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(\mathbf{P}_{u,i}^{(5)}\otimes\mathbf{Q}_{u,j}^{(5)}\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes h}\right)\right]-\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(\mathbf{P}_{u,i}^{(4)}\otimes\mathbf{Q}_{u,j}^{(4)}\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes h}\right)\right]\right|\\
\leq\delta N_{2}\left(\mathbf{P}_{u,i}^{(4)}\right)N_{2}\left(\mathbf{Q}_{u,j}^{(4)}\right).

8.

If
\mathbf{P}_{u,i}^{(4)}=\mathbf{P}_{v,i}^{(4)}
, then
\mathbf{P}_{u,i}^{(5)}=\mathbf{P}_{v,i}^{(5)}
.
If
\mathbf{Q}_{u,i}^{(4)}=\mathbf{Q}_{v,i}^{(4)}
, then
\mathbf{Q}_{u,i}^{(5)}=\mathbf{Q}_{v,i}^{(5)}
.

Invariance from
L^{2}\left(\mathcal{H}_{m}^{\otimes h},\gamma_{n_{0}n_{1}}\right)
to
\mathcal{H}_{m}^{\otimes h+n_{0}n_{1}}
. From item 1 and item 2 above, and item 3 of
2.6
and
Lemma

2.23
, we have

\sum_{\sigma}\mathrm{Inf}_{j}\left(p_{u,i,\sigma}^{(5)}\right)\leq\tau N_{2}\left(\mathbf{P}_{u,i}^{(4)}\right)^{2}.

Similarly, we have

\sum_{\sigma}\mathrm{Inf}_{i}\left(q_{u,i,\sigma}^{(5)}\right)\leq\tau N_{2}\left(\mathbf{Q}_{u,i}^{(4)}\right)^{2}.

For any
u\in[s]
, we apply
Lemma

10.11
to
\left(\mathbf{P}_{u,i}^{(5)},\mathbf{Q}_{u,i}^{(5)}\right)
with
n\leftarrow n_{0}n_{1},h\leftarrow h,d\leftarrow d_{2}
,

\tau\leftarrow\tau_{0}=\max_{u}\left\{\max\left\{\tau N_{2}\left(\mathbf{P}_{u,i}^{(4)}\right)^{2},\tau N_{2}\left(\mathbf{Q}_{u,i}^{(4)}\right)^{2}\right\}~:~u\in[s]\right\}

to get
\left(P_{u,i}^{(6)},Q_{u,i}^{(6)}\right)\in\mathcal{H}_{m}^{\otimes h+n_{0}n_{1}}\times\mathcal{H}_{m}^{\otimes h+n_{0}n_{1}}
satisfying that

1.

\sum_{i=1}^{t}P_{u,i}^{(6)}=\mathds{1}
and
\sum_{i=1}^{t}Q_{u,i}^{(6)}=\mathds{1}
.

2.

For all
i,j\in[t]

\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(P_{u,i}^{(6)}\otimes Q_{u,j}^{(6)}\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes(n_{0}n_{1}+h)}\right)=\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(\mathbf{P}_{u,i}^{(5)}\otimes\mathbf{Q}_{u,j}^{(5)}\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes h}\right)\right].

3.

For all
i\in[t]

N_{2}\left(\mathbf{P}_{u,i}^{(5)}\right)={\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|P_{u,i}^{(6)}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2},N_{2}\left(\mathbf{Q}_{u,i}^{(5)}\right)={\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|Q_{u,i}^{(6)}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}.

4.

For all
i\in[t]

\left|\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\frac{1}{m^{h}}\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(\mathbf{P}_{u,i}^{(5)}\right)-\frac{1}{m^{n_{0}n_{1}+h}}\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(P_{u,i}^{(6)}\right)\right]\right|\\
\leq O\left(\left(3^{d_{2}}m^{d_{2}/2}d_{2}\sqrt{\tau_{0}}\right)^{2/3}\right);

\left|\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\frac{1}{m^{h}}\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(\mathbf{Q}_{u,i}^{(5)}\right)-\frac{1}{m^{n_{0}n_{1}+h}}\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(Q_{u,i}^{(6)}\right)\right]\right|\\
\leq O\left(\left(3^{d_{2}}m^{d_{2}/2}d_{2}\sqrt{\tau_{0}}\right)^{2/3}\right).

5.

If
\mathbf{P}_{u,i}^{(5)}=\mathbf{P}_{v,i}^{(5)}
, then
P_{u,i}^{(6)}=P_{v,i}^{(6)}
.
If
\mathbf{Q}_{u,i}^{(5)}=\mathbf{Q}_{v,i}^{(5)}
, then
Q_{u,i}^{(6)}=Q_{v,i}^{(6)}
.

Rounding to measurement operators
. Let
\left(P_{u,i}^{(6)}\right)^{\mathpzc{pos}}
and
\left(Q_{u,i}^{(6)}\right)^{\mathpzc{pos}}
be the matrices defined in Eq.
Eq.

21
. Suppose

X_{u}=\sum_{i=1}^{t}\left(P_{u,i}^{(6)}\right)^{\mathpzc{pos}}~\mbox{and}~Y_{u}=\sum_{i=1}^{t}\left(Q_{u,i}^{(6)}\right)^{\mathpzc{pos}}.

Define

\left(\widetilde{P_{u,i}}\right)_{1\leq i\leq t}=\left(\left(X_{u}^{+}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(P_{u,1}^{(6)}\right)^{\mathpzc{pos}}\left(X_{u}^{+}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},\dots,\left(X_{u}^{+}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(P_{u,t}^{(6)}\right)^{\mathpzc{pos}}\left(X_{u}^{+}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)

\left(\widetilde{Q_{u,i}}\right)_{1\leq i\leq t}=\left(\left(Y_{u}^{+}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(Q_{u,1}^{(6)}\right)^{\mathpzc{pos}}\left(Y_{u}^{+}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},\dots,\left(Y_{u}^{+}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(Q_{u,t}^{(6)}\right)^{\mathpzc{pos}}\left(Y_{u}^{+}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)

It is easy to verify that
\left(\widetilde{P_{u,i}}\right)_{1\leq i\leq t}
and
\left(\widetilde{Q_{u,i}}\right)_{1\leq i\leq t}
are both sub-POVMs for
u\in[s]
. Then

\displaystyle\left|\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(P_{u,i}^{(6)}\otimes Q_{u,j}^{(6)}\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes n_{0}n_{1}+h}\right)-\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(\widetilde{P_{u,i}}\otimes\widetilde{Q_{u,j}}\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes n_{0}n_{1}+h}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(P_{u,i}^{(6)}\otimes\left(Q_{u,j}^{(6)}-\widetilde{Q_{u,j}}\right)\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes n_{0}n_{1}+h}\right)\right|+\left|\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(\left(P_{u,i}^{(6)}-\widetilde{P_{u,i}}\right)\otimes\widetilde{Q_{u,j}}\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes n_{0}n_{1}+h}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|P_{u,i}^{(6)}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|Q_{u,j}^{(6)}-\widetilde{Q_{u,j}}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}+{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\widetilde{Q_{u,j}}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|P_{u,i}^{(6)}-\widetilde{P_{u,i}}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|P_{u,i}^{(6)}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{t}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|Q_{u,j}^{(6)}-\widetilde{Q_{u,j}}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\left(\sum_{i=1}^{t}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|P_{u,i}^{(6)}-\widetilde{P_{u,i}}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|P_{u,i}^{(6)}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}\left(\frac{3(t+1)}{m^{h+n_{0}n_{1}}}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(Q_{u,i}^{(6)}\right)+6\left(\frac{t}{m^{h+n_{0}n_{1}}}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(Q_{u,i}^{(6)}\right)\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{3(t+1)}{m^{h+n_{0}n_{1}}}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(P_{u,i}^{(6)}\right)+6\left(\frac{t}{m^{h+n_{0}n_{1}}}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(P_{u,i}^{(6)}\right)\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

where the second inequality follows from
2.1
and the last inequality follows from
Lemma

9.2
.

Keeping track of the parameters in the construction, we are able to upper bound
\sum_{j=1}^{t}\frac{1}{m^{h+n_{0}n_{1}}}\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(Q_{u,j}^{(6)}\right)
. The dependency of parameters is pictorially described in
Fig.

4
. Choosing

\delta=\epsilon^{8}/\left(10^{16}t^{2}s\right),\tau=\epsilon^{12}/\left(s^{2}t^{3}\exp\left(\frac{\log m\log^{2}\frac{1}{\delta}}{\left(1-\rho\right)\delta}\right)\right)~\mbox{and}~D=n_{0}n_{1}+h,

(31)

we conclude the desired result.
∎

5
Summary and open problems

In this work, we prove that if the players in a nonlocal game are provided with arbitrarily many copies of noisy MESs, then the game is decidable. We prove it by reducing the problem to the decidability of the quantum non-interactive simulation
of joint distributions and uses the framework in
[
32
]
. To this end, we systematically generalize the theory of Boolean analysis to the space of matrices and the space of random matrices.

This work also raises many interesting open questions.

1.

In this paper, we prove a quantum invariance principle for
C^{3}
piecewise polynomial functions. Can we establish a quantum invariance principle for a larger class of functions? More specifically, can we prove a quantum analog of the classical invariance principle in
[
40
]
?

2.

As mentioned above, invariance principles have rich applications in theoretical computer science. Are there other applications of the quantum invariance principle established in this paper?

3.

We essentially use the dimension reduction for polynomials in Gaussian spaces
[
31
]
as an intermediate step. Is it possible to simplify the proof by proving a quantum dimension reduction directly?

4.

Given a nonlocal game
G
and a bipartite state
\psi
, we say
\left(G,\psi\right)
is a
mono-state game
if the players in
G
only share arbitrarily many copies of a bipartite state
\psi
. This paper proves that the mono-state games
\left(G,\psi\right)
are decidable if
\psi
is a noisy MES. It is known that the games are undecidable if
\psi
is an EPR state because of Ji et al.’s breakthrough work
[
47
]
. Due to PCP theorem
[
2
,
3
]
it is
\mathrm{NP}
-complete when
\psi
is separable. Hence it is interesting to identify the hardness of the mono-state games
\left(G,\psi\right)
for any given bipartite state
\psi
.

5.

Can we use the framework developed in this paper to design algorithms for other "tensored" quantities in quantum information theory and quantum complexity theory such as quantum channel capacities
[
35
]
, the regularizations of the various entanglement measures
[
68
]
, quantum information complexity
[
80
]
, etc.?

6.

Is it possible to generalize our result to multiplayer nonlocal games? Note that the decidability of non-interactive simulations of
k
-partite distributions for
k\geq 3
is still open
[
32
]
.

6
Smoothing operators

The main lemma in this section is the following.

Lemma 6.1
.

Given parameters
0\leq\rho<1
,
0<\delta<1
, integer
n>0,m>1
an
m
-dimensional noisy MES
\psi_{AB}
with the maximal correlation
\rho=\rho\left(\psi_{AB}\right)
, there exists
d=d\left(\rho,\delta\right)
and a map
f:\mathcal{H}_{m}^{\otimes n}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}_{m}^{\otimes n},
such that for any
P\in\mathcal{H}_{m}^{\otimes n},Q\in\mathcal{H}_{m}^{\otimes n}
satisfying
0\leq P\leq\mathds{1}
and
0\leq Q\leq\mathds{1}
, the operators
P^{(1)}=f\left(P\right)
and
Q^{(1)}=f\left(Q\right)
satisfy that

1.

0\leq P^{(1)}\leq\mathds{1}~\mbox{and}~0\leq Q^{(1)}\leq\mathds{1};

2.

{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|P^{(1)}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}\leq{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|P\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}~\mbox{and}~{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|Q^{(1)}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}\leq{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|Q\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2};

3.

\left|\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(P^{(1)}\otimes Q^{(1)}\right)\psi^{\otimes n}_{AB}\right)-\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(P\otimes Q\right)\psi^{\otimes n}_{AB}\right)\right|\leq\delta,

4.

{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\left(P^{(1)}\right)^{>d}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}^{2}_{2}\leq\delta~\mbox{and}~{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\left(Q^{(1)}\right)^{>d}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}^{2}_{2}\leq\delta,

5.

the map
f
is linear and unital.

In particular, we can take
d=\frac{2\log^{2}\frac{1}{\delta}}{C\left(1-\rho\right)\delta}
for some constant
C
.

Before proving
Lemma

6.1
, we need the following lemma, whose classical analog is proved in
[
61
]
.

Lemma 6.2
.

Given a noisy MES
\psi_{AB}
with the maximal correlation
\rho=\rho(\psi_{AB})<1
, a parameter
0<\epsilon<1
and operators
P\in\mathcal{H}_{m}^{\otimes n},Q\in\mathcal{H}_{m}^{\otimes n}
, it holds that

\left|\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(P\otimes Q\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes n}\right)-\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(\Delta_{1-\gamma}\left(P\right)\otimes\Delta_{1-\gamma}\left(Q\right)\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes n}\right)\right|\leq 2\epsilon\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}\>\!\!\left[P\right]\mathrm{Var}\>\!\!\left[Q\right]},

for

0\leq\gamma\leq 1-\left(1-\epsilon\right)^{\log\rho/\left(\log\epsilon+\log\rho\right)}.

(32)

In particular, we can take

\gamma=C\frac{\left(1-\rho\right)\epsilon}{\log\left(1/\epsilon\right)}.

where
C
is an absolute constant.

Proof.

We first show that for any
P,Q\in\mathcal{H}_{m}^{\otimes n}

\displaystyle\left|\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(P\otimes Q\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes n}\right)-\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(P\otimes\Delta_{1-\gamma}\left(Q\right)\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes n}\right)\right|

(33)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(P\otimes\left(\mathds{1}-\Delta_{1-\gamma}\right)\left(Q\right)\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes n}\right)\right|\leq\epsilon\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}\>\!\!\left[P\right]\mathrm{Var}\>\!\!\left[Q\right]}.

By
Lemma

3.5
item 1, for all
S\subseteq[n]
, we have

\left(\mathds{1}-\Delta_{1-\gamma}\right)\left(Q[S]\right)=\left(1-\left(1-\gamma\right)^{\left|S\right|}\right)Q[S].

Using
Proposition

3.12
and
Proposition

3.13
,

\left\|\mathcal{T}\left(Q[S]\right)\right\|_{2}=\left\|\mathcal{T}\left(Q\right)[S]\right\|_{2}\leq\rho^{\left|S\right|}\left\|Q[S]\right\|_{2}.

(34)

Let
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}=\mathcal{T}\circ\left(\mathds{1}_{B}-\Delta_{1-\gamma}\right)
.
From
Definition

3.1
,

\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(P\otimes\left(\mathds{1}-\Delta_{1-\gamma}\right)\left(Q\right)\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes n}\right)=\left\langle P,\mathcal{T}^{\prime}\left(Q\right)\right\rangle

Combining
Lemma

3.5
and Eq.
Eq.

34
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{T}^{\prime}\left(Q[S]\right)\right\|_{2}\leq\rho^{\left|S\right|}\cdot\left(1-\left(1-\gamma\right)^{\left|S\right|}\right)\left\|Q[S]\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\min\left\{\rho^{\left|S\right|},\left(1-\left(1-\gamma\right)^{\left|S\right|}\right)\right\}\left\|Q[S]\right\|_{2}\leq\epsilon\left\|Q[S]\right\|_{2},

(35)

where the second inequality follows from our choice of
\gamma
in Eq.
Eq.

32
.
From
Lemma

3.5
,
Proposition

2.18
and
Proposition

3.12
,
\left\langle\mathcal{T}^{\prime}\left(Q[S]\right),P[S^{\prime}]\right\rangle=0
if
S\neq S^{\prime}
. Note that
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}\left(\mathds{1}\right)=0
. And thus
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}\left(Q[\emptyset]\right)=0
.
Therefore,

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle P,\mathcal{T}^{\prime}\left(Q\right)\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\sum_{S\neq\emptyset}\left\langle P[S],\mathcal{T}^{\prime}\left(Q[S]\right)\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{1}{m^{n}}\sum_{S\neq\emptyset}\left\|P[S]\right\|_{2}^{2}}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{1}{m^{n}}\sum_{S\neq\emptyset}\left\|\mathcal{T}^{\prime}\left(Q[S]\right)\right\|_{2}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon\sqrt{\frac{1}{m^{n}}\sum_{S\neq\emptyset}\left\|P[S]\right\|_{2}^{2}}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{1}{m^{n}}\sum_{S\neq\emptyset}\left\|Q[S]\right\|_{2}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}\>\!\!\left[P\right]\mathrm{Var}\>\!\!\left[Q\right]},

where the second inequality follows from Eq. (
35
) and the last inequality follows from the orthogonality of the Efron-Stein decomposition.

Thus we obtain Eq.
Eq.

33
. Note that it holds for any
P,Q\in\mathcal{H}_{m}^{\otimes n}
. We apply the same argument to
P
and
\Delta_{1-\gamma}\left(Q\right)
and get

\left|\mathrm{Tr}~\left(\left(P-\Delta_{1-\gamma}\left(P\right)\right)\otimes\Delta_{1-\gamma}\left(Q\right)\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes n}\right|\leq\epsilon\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}\>\!\!\left[P\right]\mathrm{Var}\>\!\!\left[\Delta_{1-\gamma}\left(Q\right)\right]}.

Note that
\mathrm{Var}\>\!\!\left[\Delta_{1-\gamma}\left(Q\right)\right]\leq\mathrm{Var}\>\!\!\left[Q\right]
by
Lemma

2.12
and
Lemma

3.5
item 1. Thus

\displaystyle\left|\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(P\otimes Q\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes n}\right)-\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(\Delta_{1-\gamma}\left(P\right)\otimes\Delta_{1-\gamma}\left(Q\right)\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes n}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(P\otimes Q\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes n}\right)-\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(P\otimes\Delta_{1-\gamma}\left(Q\right)\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes n}\right)\right|

\displaystyle+\left|\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(P\otimes\Delta_{1-\gamma}\left(Q\right)\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes n}\right)-\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(\Delta_{1-\gamma}\left(P\right)\otimes\Delta_{1-\gamma}\left(Q\right)\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes n}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\epsilon\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}\>\!\!\left[P\right]\mathrm{Var}\>\!\!\left[Q\right]}.

We conclude the desired result.
∎

We are now ready to prove
Lemma

6.1
.

Proof of
Lemma

6.1
.

Given
\rho
and
\delta
, we choose
\epsilon=\delta/2
and
\gamma
in
Lemma

6.2
. We choose
d
sufficiently large such that
(1-\gamma)^{2d}\leq\delta
, that is,
d=\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{2\gamma}
. Given
P,Q
as in
Lemma

6.1
, we set

P^{(1)}=\Delta_{1-\gamma}\left(P\right)~\mbox{and}~Q^{(1)}=\Delta_{1-\gamma}\left(Q\right).

From
Lemma

3.5
item 2 and item 3,
0\leq P^{(1)}\leq 1
and
0\leq Q^{(1)}\leq 1
. By
Lemma

3.5
item 2,
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|P^{(1)}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}\leq{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|P\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}
and
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|Q^{(1)}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}\leq{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|Q\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}
. Note that
\mathrm{Var}\>\!\!\left[P\right]\leq{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|P\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}\leq 1~\mbox{and}~\mathrm{Var}\>\!\!\left[Q\right]\leq{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|Q\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}\leq 1

due to
Lemma

2.12
. Combing with
Lemma

6.2
, we get

\left|\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(P\otimes Q\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes n}\right)-\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(P^{(1)}\otimes Q^{(1)}\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes n}\right)\right|\leq 2\epsilon=\delta.

Notice that

\widehat{P^{(1)}}\left(\sigma\right)=\left(1-\gamma\right)^{\left|\sigma\right|}\widehat{P}\left(\sigma\right)~\mbox{and}~\widehat{Q^{(1)}}\left(\sigma\right)=\left(1-\gamma\right)^{\left|\sigma\right|}\widehat{Q}\left(\sigma\right)

by
Lemma

3.5
item 1. Thus, we get that

\displaystyle\sum_{\left|\sigma\right|>d}\widehat{P^{(1)}}\left(\sigma\right)^{2}\leq\left(1-\gamma\right)^{2d}\sum_{\left|\sigma\right|>d}\widehat{P}\left(\sigma\right)^{2}\leq\left(1-\gamma\right)^{2d}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|P\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}\leq\delta;

\displaystyle\sum_{\left|\sigma\right|>d}\widehat{Q^{(1)}}\left(\sigma\right)^{2}\leq\left(1-\gamma\right)^{2d}\sum_{\left|\sigma\right|>d}\widehat{Q}\left(\sigma\right)^{2}\leq\left(1-\gamma\right)^{2d}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|Q\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}\leq\delta,

where the second inequalities in both equations are from
2.11
item 3.

Item 5 follows from the fact that
\Delta_{1-\gamma}\left(\cdot\right)
is linear and unital by definition.
∎

7
Joint regularity lemma

From Lemma
6.1
,
P
and
Q
can be approximated by low-degree operators after the smoothing operation. Therefore, the number of registers that have high influences is bounded by Lemma
2.16
. Suppose
P
and
Q
are both diagonal. It is proved in
[
31
, Lemma 5.1]
that even if we randomly restrict the registers in
H
to the elements in computational basis, the influences of the registers not in
H
are still small
[
26
,
4
]
. More specifically, we assume that
P
and
Q
are diagonal matrices with
P=\sum_{s\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{|H|}}\left|s\middle\rangle\middle\langle s\right|\otimes P_{s}
and

Q=\sum_{s\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{|H|}}\left|s\middle\rangle\middle\langle s\right|\otimes Q_{s},
where
\left\{\left|s\right\rangle\right\}_{s\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{|H|}}
is the computational basis of the registers in
H
.
Then it is shown that all the registers of
P_{s}
and
Q_{s}
have low influences with high probability over sampling
s
. This fact is crucial to all the followup works
[
32
,
24
]
. However, random restrictions cannot be applied to
P
and
Q
if they are not diagonal. If the off-diagonal entries of
P
and
Q
are nonzero, then the information about the off-diagonal entries might be lost if we restrict the registers in
H
to a computational basis. Instead of random restrictions, we expand the operators in a properly chosen standard orthonormal basis. Before getting into the details, we introduce the notion of
correlation matrices
.

Definition 7.1
.

Given quantum systems
A
and
B
with dimensions
m_{A}
and
m_{B}
, respectively, and a bipartite quantum state
\psi_{AB}
, let
\mathcal{A}=\left\{\mathcal{A}_{i}\right\}_{i\in[m_{A}^{2}]_{\geq 0}}
and
\mathcal{B}=\left\{\mathcal{B}_{i}\right\}_{i\in[m_{B}^{2}]_{\geq 0}}
be standard orthonormal bases in the spaces
\mathcal{M}\left(A\right)
and
\mathcal{M}\left(B\right)
, respectively.
The correlation matrix of
\left(\psi_{AB},\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B}\right)
is an
m_{A}^{2}\times m_{B}^{2}
matrix, where

\mathsf{Corr}\left(\psi_{AB},\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B}\right)_{i,j}=\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(\mathcal{A}_{i}\otimes\mathcal{B}_{j}\right)\psi_{AB}\right).

for
i\in[m_{A}^{2}]_{\geq 0},j\in[m_{B}^{2}]_{\geq 0}
. The correlation matrix of
\left(\psi_{AB}^{\otimes n},\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B}\right)
is an
m_{A}^{2n}\times m_{B}^{2n}
matrix, where

\mathsf{Corr}\left(\psi_{AB}^{\otimes n},\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B}\right)_{\sigma,\tau}=\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(\mathcal{A}_{\sigma}\otimes\mathcal{B}_{\tau}\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes n}\right),

for
\sigma\in[m_{A}^{2}]^{n}_{\geq 0}
and
\tau\in[m_{B}^{2}]^{n}_{\geq 0}
.

The lemma below follows by the definition.

Lemma 7.2
.

Let
A
and
B
be two quantum systems of dimensions
m_{A}
and
m_{B}
, respectively. For any standard orthonormal bases in
\mathcal{M}\left(A\right)
and
\mathcal{M}\left(B\right)
and bipartite quantum state
\psi_{AB}
, it holds for all positive integer
n
that

\mathsf{Corr}\left(\psi_{AB}^{\otimes n},\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B}\right)=\mathsf{Corr}\left(\psi_{AB},\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B}\right)^{\otimes n}.

Lemma 7.3
.

Given quantum systems
A
and
B
with dimensions
m_{A}
and
m_{B}
, respectively, and a noisy MES
\psi_{AB}
, for any standard orthonormal bases
\mathcal{A}=\left\{\mathcal{A}_{i}\right\}_{i\in[m_{A}^{2}]_{\geq 0}}
and
\mathcal{B}=\left\{\mathcal{B}_{i}\right\}_{i\in[m_{B}^{2}]_{\geq 0}}
in
\mathcal{M}\left(A\right)
and
\mathcal{M}\left(B\right)
, respectively, it holds that
s_{1}\left(\mathsf{Corr}\left(\psi_{AB},\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B}\right)\right)=1
and
s_{2}\left(\mathsf{Corr}\left(\psi_{AB},\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B}\right)\right)=\rho
, where
\rho=\rho\left(\psi_{AB}\right)
and
s_{i}\left(\cdot\right)
is the
i
-th largest singular value.

Moreover, there exist standard orthonormal bases
\mathcal{A}=\left\{\mathcal{A}_{i}\right\}_{i\in[m_{A}^{2}]_{\geq 0}}
and
\mathcal{B}=\left\{\mathcal{B}_{i}\right\}_{i\in[m_{B}^{2}]_{\geq 0}}
in
\left(\mathcal{M}\left(A\right),\psi_{A}\right)
and
\left(\mathcal{M}\left(B\right),\psi_{B}\right)
, respectively, such that

\mathsf{Corr}\left(\psi_{AB},\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B}\right)_{i,j}=\begin{cases}c_{i}~&\mbox{if $i=j$}\\
0~&\mbox{otherwise},\end{cases}

where
c_{1}=1,c_{2}=\rho\left(\psi_{AB}\right)
and
c_{1}\geq c_{2}\geq c_{3}\geq\ldots
.

Proof.

We assume that the dimensions of
A
and
B
are both
m
. Similar arguments apply when the dimensions of the two systems are different. By
2.8
and
Lemma

7.2
, the correlation matrices of
\left(\psi_{AB},\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B}\right)
for different orthonormal bases
\left(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B}\right)
are equivalent up to left and right orthonormal transformations. Thus the singular values of the correlation matrix of
\left(\psi_{AB},\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B}\right)
are independent of the choices of
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
.

Let
M=\mathsf{Corr}\left(\psi_{AB},\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B}\right)
. Recall that
\mathcal{A}_{0}=\mathcal{B}_{0}=\mathds{1}
, by
2.1
item 1 we have that
M_{0,0}=1
,
M_{0,i}=M_{i,0}=0
for all
i\in[m^{2}-1]
. Note that
M
is a real matrix. Thus we set
M=U^{\dagger}DV
to be a singular value decomposition of
M
where
U
and
V
are orthogonal matrices and
U_{0,0}=V_{0,0}=1,U_{0,i}=U_{i,0}=V_{0,i}=V_{i,0}=0
for
1\leq i\leq m^{2}-1
. Let

\mathcal{P}_{i}=\sum_{j=1}^{m^{2}-1}U_{ij}\mathcal{A}_{j}~\mbox{and}~\mathcal{Q}_{i}=\sum_{j=1}^{m^{2}-1}V_{ji}^{\dagger}\mathcal{B}_{j}

for
1\leq i\leq m^{2}-1
. Then

\mathrm{Tr}~\mathcal{P}_{i}=\mathrm{Tr}~\mathcal{Q}_{i}=0
since
\mathrm{Tr}~\mathcal{A}_{j}=\mathrm{Tr}~\mathcal{B}_{j}=0
for
1\leq j\leq m^{2}-1
. And thus

\mathrm{Var}\>\!\!\left[\mathcal{P}_{i}\right]={\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{P}_{i}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}=\sum_{j}\left|U_{ij}\right|^{2}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{A}_{j}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}=1.

Similarly,
\mathrm{Var}\>\!\!\left[\mathcal{Q}_{i}\right]=1
. Thus by the definition of quantum maximal correlations,

\displaystyle\rho

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(\mathcal{P}_{i}\otimes\mathcal{Q}_{i^{\prime}}\right)\psi_{AB}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j,k=1}^{m^{2}-1}U_{ij}V^{\dagger}_{ki^{\prime}}\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(\mathcal{A}_{j}\otimes\mathcal{B}_{k}\right)\psi_{AB}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j,k=1}^{m^{2}-1}U_{ij}V_{ki^{\prime}}^{\dagger}M_{jk}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(UMV^{\dagger}\right)_{ii^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta_{i,i^{\prime}}D_{ii}.

Hence
\left\|M\right\|=1
and
s_{2}\left(M\right)\leq\rho
.

From
Proposition

3.10
, we assume that
P,Q
are two Hermitian operators with
\mathrm{Tr}~P=\mathrm{Tr}~Q=0
in
\mathcal{H}\left(A\right),\mathcal{H}\left(B\right)
which achieve
\rho
