. Then from
Definition

3.6
, both
\left\{\mathds{1}_{m_{A}},P\right\}
and
\left\{\mathds{1}_{m_{B}},Q\right\}
can be extended to orthonormal bases in the real Hilbert space consisting of all Hermitian matrices of dimension
m
over
{\mathbb{R}}
, say
\left\{\mathcal{P}_{i}\right\}_{i=0}^{m^{2}-1}
and
\left\{\mathcal{Q}_{i}\right\}_{i=0}^{m^{2}-1}
where
\mathcal{P}_{0}=\mathcal{Q}_{0}=\mathds{1}_{m},\mathcal{P}_{1}=P
and
\mathcal{Q}_{1}=Q
. Since
\left\{\mathcal{P}_{i}\right\}_{i=0}^{m^{2}-1}
and
\left\{\mathcal{Q}_{i}\right\}_{i=0}^{m^{2}-1}
are also orthonormal in
\mathcal{M}_{m}
, respectively, they are both standard orthonormal bases in
\mathcal{M}_{m}
as well. Let
M^{\prime}
be the corresponding correlation matrix. Again by
2.1
,
M^{\prime}_{0,0}=1,M^{\prime}_{1,1}=\rho
and
M^{\prime}_{0,i}=M^{\prime}_{i,0}=0
for
1\leq i\leq m^{2}-1
. Since the largest singular value of a matrix is not less than the largest diagonal element, we have
s_{2}\left(M^{\prime}\right)\geq\rho
. As argued above,
M
and
M^{\prime}
have the same singular values. Therefore,
s_{2}(M)=s_{2}(M^{\prime})=\rho
.

The second part of the lemma follows by
2.8
and
Lemma

2.20
.
∎

We reach the main lemma in this section.

Lemma 7.4
.

Given parameters
0\leq\delta,\epsilon<1
, integers
d\geq 0,n>0,m>1
, a noisy
m
-dimensional MES
\psi_{AB}
and operators
P\in\mathcal{H}_{m}^{\otimes n},Q\in\mathcal{H}_{m}^{\otimes n}
satisfying that
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|P\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}\leq 1,{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|Q\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}\leq 1,{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|P^{>d}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}^{2}_{2}\leq\delta
and
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|Q^{>d}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}^{2}_{2}\leq\delta
, let
\mathcal{A}=\left\{\mathcal{A}_{i}\right\}_{i=0}^{m^{2}-1}
and
\mathcal{B}=\left\{\mathcal{B}_{i}\right\}_{i=0}^{m^{2}-1}
be the standard orthonormal bases induced by
Lemma

7.3
and
\left(c_{i}\right)_{i=0}^{m^{2}-1}
be the singular values of
\mathsf{Corr}\left(\psi_{AB},\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B}\right)
in non-increasing order. Suppose
P
and
Q
have Fourier expansions

P=\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{n}}\widehat{P}\left(\sigma\right)\mathcal{A}_{\sigma}~\mbox{and}~Q=\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{n}}\widehat{Q}\left(\sigma\right)\mathcal{B}_{\sigma}.

Then there exists a subset
H\subseteq[n]
of size
h=\left|H\right|\leq\frac{2d}{\epsilon}
such that for any
i\notin H
,

\mathrm{Inf}_{i}\left(P^{\leq d}\right)\leq\epsilon~\mbox{and}~\mathrm{Inf}_{i}\left(Q^{\leq d}\right)\leq\epsilon

Without loss of generality, we assume
H=[h]
, then

\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(P\otimes Q\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes n}\right)=\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}c_{\sigma}\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(P_{\sigma}\otimes Q_{\sigma}\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes(n-h)}\right),

where
c_{\sigma}=\prod_{i=1}^{h}c_{\sigma_{i}}
for any
\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}
;
and

P_{\sigma}=\sum_{\tau\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{n}:\tau_{H}=\sigma}\widehat{P}\left(\tau\right)\mathcal{A}_{\tau_{H^{c}}}

Q_{\sigma}=\sum_{\tau\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{n}:\tau_{H}=\sigma}\widehat{Q}\left(\tau\right)\mathcal{B}_{\tau_{H^{c}}}.

Proof.

Set
H=\left\{i:\mathrm{Inf}_{i}\left(P^{\leq d}\right)\geq\epsilon~\mbox{or}~\mathrm{Inf}_{i}\left(Q^{\leq d}\right)\geq\epsilon\right\}
. From
Lemma

2.16
item 4 and the fact that
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|P^{\leq d}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}\leq{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|P\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}
and
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|Q^{\leq d}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}\leq{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|Q\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}
we have
\left|H\right|\leq\frac{2d}{\epsilon}
. Without loss of generality, we assume that
H=[h]
. By the definitions of
P_{\sigma}
and
Q_{\sigma}
, we have

P=\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}P_{\sigma}\otimes\mathcal{A}_{\sigma}~\mbox{and}~Q=\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}Q_{\sigma}\otimes\mathcal{B}_{\sigma}.

Thus

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}~\left(\left(P\otimes Q\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes n}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}\left(\mathrm{Tr}~\left(\left(P_{\sigma}\otimes Q_{\sigma^{\prime}}\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes\left(n-h\right)}\right)\right)\left(\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(\mathcal{A}_{\sigma}\otimes\mathcal{B}_{\sigma^{\prime}}\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes h}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}c_{\sigma}\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(P_{\sigma}\otimes Q_{\sigma}\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes(n-h)}\right),

where the second equality follows from
Lemma

7.2
and
Lemma

7.3
.

∎

8
Hypercontractive inequality for high dimensional random operators

Recall that
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\cdot\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{p}
represents the normalized
p
-norm defined in
Eq.

20
. For any linear map
L:\mathcal{M}_{k}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{k}
, we define two families of norms as follows.

Definition 8.1
.

Given
1\leq p,q\leq\infty
, integers
m,n\geq 1
, a linear map
L:\mathcal{M}_{m}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{n}
, the
p
-to-
q
norm of
L
is defined to be

\left\|L\right\|_{p\rightarrow q}=\sup_{M\neq 0}\frac{\left\|L(M)\right\|_{q}}{\left\|M\right\|_{p}}.

The normalized
p
-to-
q
norm of
L
is

{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|L\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{p\rightarrow q}=\sup_{M\neq 0}\frac{{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|L(M)\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{q}}{{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|M\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{p}}

The purpose of this section is to establish a hypercontractive inequality for random operators. A hypercontractive inequality on space
L^{2}\left({\mathbb{C}}^{k},\gamma_{n}\right)
asserts that the normalized
p
-to-
q
norm of the Ornstein-Uhlenbeck operator
U_{\rho}
is upper bounded by
1
if
0\leq\rho\leq\sqrt{\frac{p-1}{q-1}}
for any
0<p<q\leq\infty
. For matrix spaces, King
[
55
]
proved a hypercontractive inequality for all unital channels, which immediately implies hypercontractivity of the noise operators
\Delta_{\rho}
in
\mathcal{M}_{2}
. However, his proof can not be extended to higher dimensions. In this section, we first provide a hypercontractive inequality for
\Delta_{\rho}
in an arbitrary dimension. Then we introduce a noise operator acting on
L^{2}\left(\mathcal{M}^{\otimes h}_{m},\gamma_{n}\right)
, which is a hybrid of Ornstein-Uhlenbeck operators
U_{\rho}
and noise operators
\Delta_{\rho}
. Finally, we establish a hypercontractive inequality for random operators.

Lemma 8.2
.

For any integer
m\geq 2
and
0\leq\rho\leq\sqrt{\frac{1}{3\sqrt{m}}}
, let
\Delta_{\rho}:\mathcal{M}_{m}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{m}
be the noise operator defined in
Definition

3.4
. For any integer
n\geq 1
, it holds that

{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\Delta_{\rho}^{\otimes n}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2\rightarrow 4}\leq 1.

The proof is via induction on
n
. The following lemma is for the base case.

Lemma 8.3
.

Let
m>1,n\geq 1
be integers and
M\in\mathcal{M}_{m}
. For any
0\leq\rho\leq\sqrt{\frac{1}{3\sqrt{m}}}
, it holds that

{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\Delta_{\rho}(M)\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{4}\leq{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|M\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}.

Proof.

From
[
83
, Theorem 1]
, it suffices to prove the lemma for
M\in\mathcal{H}_{m}
. Note that
\ell_{p}
-norms are invariant under unitary transformations. The noise operator
\Delta_{\rho}
commutes with any unitary operation. Namely,
\Delta_{\rho}\left(UMU^{\dagger}\right)=U\left(\Delta_{\rho}\left(M\right)\right)U^{\dagger}
for any unitary
U
. Thus we may assume that
M=\mathrm{Diag}\left(d_{1},\ldots,d_{m}\right)
is diagonal without loss of generality. Note that the set of
m\times m
diagonal matrices forms an
m
-dimensional Hilbert space, denoted by
\mathcal{D}
. Consider the Hilbert space
\mathcal{F}=\left(\left\{f:[m]\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}\right\},\left\langle\cdot,\cdot\right\rangle\right)
, where
\left\langle f,g\right\rangle=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}f(i)g(i)
, which is isomorphic to
\mathcal{D}
. The noise operator
\Delta_{\rho}
in
\mathcal{D}
is isomorphic to the operator
\Delta^{\prime}_{\rho}
in
\mathcal{F}
, where
\Delta^{\prime}_{\rho}f\left(x\right)=\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{y}\sim_{\rho}x\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[f(\mathbf{y})\right]
and
\mathbf{y}\sim_{\rho}x
represents that

\mathbf{y}=\begin{cases}x&\mbox{ with probability $\rho$};\\
\mbox{drawn from $[m]$ uniformly},&\mbox{otherwise}.\end{cases}

It is known in
[
87
, Corollary 3.1]
[
66
, Page 288, Corollary 10.20]
that
\left\|\Delta^{\prime}_{\rho}f\right\|_{4}\leq\left\|f\right\|_{2}
for
0\leq\rho\leq\sqrt{\frac{1}{3\sqrt{m}}}
. From the isomorphism between
\mathcal{D}
and
\mathcal{F}
, we conclude the result.
∎

Note that
\Delta_{\rho}^{\otimes n}
only commutes with the product-unitary operations. But the operators
M\in\mathcal{M}_{m}^{\otimes n}
that achieve
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\Delta_{\rho}^{\otimes n}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2\rightarrow 4}
are not necessarily product states, which thus cannot be diagonalized by product-unitary operators in general. Hence, we cannot apply the hypercontractivity on function spaces directly as we did in
Lemma

8.3
. Instead, we use the same strategy as that in most of the proofs of hypercontractivity on function spaces by the induction on
n
.

Lemma 8.4
.

Let
k>0
be an integer and

M=\begin{pmatrix}M_{11}&\cdots&M_{1m}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
M_{m1}&\cdots&M_{mm}\end{pmatrix}

where
M_{ij}\in\mathcal{M}_{k}
for
i,j\in[m]
. And let

M^{\prime}=\begin{pmatrix}\left\|M_{11}\right\|_{p}&\cdots&\left\|M_{1m}\right\|_{p}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
\left\|M_{m1}\right\|_{p}&\cdots&\left\|M_{mm}\right\|_{p}\end{pmatrix}

Then it holds that

1.

\left\|M\right\|_{p}\leq\left\|M^{\prime}\right\|_{p}
if
p=4
;

2.

\left\|M\right\|_{p}=\left\|M^{\prime}\right\|_{p}
if
p=2
.

Proof.

\displaystyle\left\|M\right\|_{4}^{4}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Tr}M^{\dagger}MM^{\dagger}M

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Tr}~\sum_{ijkl}\left(M_{ji}\right)^{\dagger}M_{jk}\left(M_{lk}\right)^{\dagger}M_{li}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{ijkl}\left|\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(M_{ji}\right)^{\dagger}M_{jk}\left(M_{lk}\right)^{\dagger}M_{li}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{ijkl}\left\|\left(M_{ji}\right)^{\dagger}\right\|_{4}\left\|M_{jk}\right\|_{4}\left\|\left(M_{lk}\right)^{\dagger}\right\|_{4}\left\|M_{li}\right\|_{4}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Tr}~\left(M^{\prime}\right)^{\dagger}M^{\prime}\left(M^{\prime}\right)^{\dagger}M^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\left\|M^{\prime}\right\|_{4}^{4}.

The last inequality is by

\displaystyle\left|\mathrm{Tr}~X_{1}X_{2}X_{3}X_{4}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|X_{1}X_{2}\right\|_{2}\left\|X_{3}X_{4}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{\mathrm{Tr}~\left(X_{2}X_{2}^{\dagger}X_{1}^{\dagger}X_{1}\right)}\cdot\sqrt{\mathrm{Tr}~\left(X_{4}X_{4}^{\dagger}X_{3}^{\dagger}X_{3}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|X_{1}\right\|_{4}\left\|X_{2}\right\|_{4}\left\|X_{3}\right\|_{4}\left\|X_{4}\right\|_{4},

where both inequalities follow from Cauchy-Schwarz inequality.

For item 2,

\displaystyle\left\|M\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Tr}M^{\dagger}M=\mathrm{Tr}~\sum_{ij}\left(M_{ji}\right)^{\dagger}M_{ji}=\sum_{ij}\left\|M_{ji}\right\|_{2}^{2}=\left\|M^{\prime}\right\|_{2}^{2}.

∎

Lemma 8.5
.

For any integer
n\geq 1
, it holds that

\left\|\Delta_{\rho}\otimes\Delta_{\rho}^{\otimes n}\right\|_{2\rightarrow 4}\leq\left\|\Delta_{\rho}^{\otimes n}\right\|_{2\rightarrow 4}\cdot\left\|\Delta_{\rho}\right\|_{2\rightarrow 4}

Proof.

Given
A\in\mathcal{M}_{m^{n+1}}
, it can be written as an
m\times m
block matrix:

A=\begin{pmatrix}A_{11}&\cdots&A_{1m}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
A_{m1}&\cdots&A_{mm}\end{pmatrix},

where
A_{ij}\in\mathcal{M}_{m^{n}}
for
1\leq i,j\leq m
. It is sufficient to prove that

\left\|(\Delta_{\rho}\otimes\Delta_{\rho}^{\otimes n})\>\!\!\left(A\right)\right\|_{4}\leq\left\|\Delta_{\rho}^{\otimes n}\right\|_{2\rightarrow 4}\left\|\Delta_{\rho}\right\|_{2\rightarrow 4}\left\|A\right\|_{2}

Define
\displaystyle B_{ij}=\Delta_{\rho}^{\otimes n}\left(A_{ij}\right),C_{ii}=\rho B_{ii}+\frac{1-\rho}{m}\sum_{k=1}^{m}B_{kk}
and
C_{ij}=\rho B_{ij}
for
i\neq j
. Then

\displaystyle(\Delta_{\rho}\otimes\Delta_{\rho}^{\otimes n})\>\!\!\left(A\right)

\displaystyle=(\Delta_{\rho}\otimes\mathds{1}_{d^{n}})\begin{pmatrix}B_{11}&\cdots&B_{1m}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
B_{m1}&\cdots&B_{mm}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}C_{11}&\cdots&C_{1m}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
C_{m1}&\cdots&C_{mm}\end{pmatrix}.

By
Lemma

8.4

\left\|(\Delta_{\rho}\otimes\Delta_{\rho}^{\otimes n})\>\!\!\left(A\right)\right\|_{4}\leq\left\|\begin{pmatrix}\left\|C_{11}\right\|_{4}&\cdots&\left\|C_{1m}\right\|_{4}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
\left\|C_{m1}\right\|_{4}&\cdots&\left\|C_{mm}\right\|_{4}\end{pmatrix}.\right\|_{4}

Let
d_{i}=\rho\left\|B_{ii}\right\|_{4}+\frac{1-\rho}{m}\sum_{k=1}^{m}\left\|B_{kk}\right\|_{4}.
By the triangle inequality,
\left\|C_{ii}\right\|_{4}\leq d_{i}
.

Hence

\displaystyle\left\|(\Delta_{\rho}\otimes\Delta_{\rho}^{\otimes n})\>\!\!\left(A\right)\right\|_{4}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\begin{pmatrix}d_{1}&\cdots&\left\|C_{1m}\right\|_{4}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
\left\|C_{m1}\right\|_{4}&\cdots&d_{m}\end{pmatrix}\right\|_{4}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\|\Delta_{\rho}\begin{pmatrix}\left\|B_{11}\right\|_{4}&\cdots&\left\|B_{1m}\right\|_{4}~\hskip 8.53581pt\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
\left\|B_{m1}\right\|_{4}&\cdots&\left\|B_{mm}\right\|_{4}\end{pmatrix}\right\|_{4}\mbox{(By the definition of $\Delta_{\rho}$)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\Delta_{\rho}\right\|_{2\rightarrow 4}\left\|\begin{pmatrix}\left\|B_{11}\right\|_{4}&\cdots&\left\|B_{1m}\right\|_{4}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
\left\|B_{m1}\right\|_{4}&\cdots&\left\|B_{mm}\right\|_{4}\end{pmatrix}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\Delta_{\rho}^{\otimes n}\right\|_{2\rightarrow 4}\left\|\Delta_{\rho}\right\|_{2\rightarrow 4}\left\|\begin{pmatrix}\left\|A_{11}\right\|_{2}&\cdots&\left\|A_{1m}\right\|_{2}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
\left\|A_{m1}\right\|_{2}&\cdots&\left\|A_{mm}\right\|_{2}\end{pmatrix}\right\|_{2}~\hskip 8.53581pt\mbox{(by induction)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\|\Delta_{\rho}^{\otimes n}\right\|_{2\rightarrow 4}\left\|\Delta_{\rho}\right\|_{2\rightarrow 4}\left\|A\right\|_{2}\quad\quad\mbox{(\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{lem:norminequality} item 2)}

∎

Now we are ready to prove
Lemma

8.2
.

Proof of
Lemma

8.2
.

\displaystyle{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\Delta_{\rho}^{\otimes n}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2\rightarrow 4}

\displaystyle=m^{\frac{n}{4}}\left\|\Delta_{\rho}^{\otimes n}\right\|_{2\rightarrow 4}

\displaystyle\leq\left(m^{\frac{1}{4}}\left\|\Delta_{\rho}\right\|_{2\rightarrow 4}\right)^{n}\quad\quad\mbox{(\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{lem:tensorinequality})}

\displaystyle={\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\Delta_{\rho}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2\rightarrow 4}^{n}

\displaystyle\leq 1\quad\quad\mbox{(\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{lem:singlehypercontractivity})}

∎

The following fact is a well known hypercontractive inequality on Gaussian spaces.

Fact 8.6
.
2
2

2

The results in
[
87
,
62
]
are for
f\in L^{2}\left({\mathbb{R}},\gamma_{n}\right)
. But they can be extended to
L^{2}\left({\mathbb{C}},\gamma_{n}\right)
using the same argument in the proof of
Lemma

8.7
.

[
87
]
[
66
, Page 332, Theorem 11.23]

For any
0\leq\rho\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}
,
f\in L^{2}\left({\mathbb{C}},\gamma_{n}\right)
, it holds that

\left\|U_{\rho}f\right\|_{4}\leq\left\|f\right\|_{2},

where
U_{\rho}
is an Ornstein-Uhlenbeck operator given in
Definition

2.4
.

We need to generalize
Footnote

2
for multiple functions for technical reasons.

Lemma 8.7
.

Given functions
p_{1},\ldots p_{n}\in L^{2}\left({\mathbb{C}},\gamma_{n}\right)
, it holds that

\left(\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{x}\sim\gamma_{n}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left|\left(U_{\rho}p_{i}\right)\left(\mathbf{x}\right)\right|^{2}\right)^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}\leq\left(\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{x}\sim\gamma_{n}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left|p_{i}\left(\mathbf{x}\right)\right|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Proof.

Let
q_{i}=U_{\rho}p_{i}
. Then

\displaystyle\left(\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{x}\sim\gamma_{n}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left|\left(U_{\rho}p_{i}\right)\left(\mathbf{x}\right)\right|^{2}\right)^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{x}\sim\gamma_{n}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\left|q_{i}\left(\mathbf{x}\right)\right|^{4}\right]+\sum_{i\neq j}\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{x}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\left|q_{i}\left(\mathbf{x}\right)^{2}q_{j}\left(\mathbf{x}\right)^{2}\right|\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|q_{i}\right\|_{4}^{4}+\sum_{i\neq j}\left\|q_{i}\right\|_{4}^{2}\left\|q_{j}\right\|_{4}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}\quad\quad\mbox{(Cauchy-Schwarz inequality)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|p_{i}\right\|_{2}^{4}+\sum_{i\neq j}\left\|p_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}\left\|p_{j}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}\quad\quad\mbox{(\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{fac:gaussianhypercontractivity})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{i}\left\|p_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{x}\sim\gamma_{n}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left|p_{i}\left(\mathbf{x}\right)\right|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

∎

We introduce a noise operator
\Gamma_{\rho}
acting on
L^{2}\left(\mathcal{M}_{m}^{\otimes h},\gamma_{n}\right)
, which is a hybrid of the Ornstein-Uhlenbeck operator
U_{\rho}
in
Definition

2.4
and the noise operator
\Delta_{\rho}
in
Definition

3.4
.

Definition 8.8
.

Given
0\leq\rho\leq 1
and integers
h,n\geq 0,m\geq 2
, let
\mathbf{P}\in L^{2}\left(\mathcal{M}_{m}^{\otimes h},\gamma_{n}\right)
with an expansion

\mathbf{P}=\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}p_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{g}\right)\mathcal{B}_{\sigma},

where
\left\{\mathcal{B}_{i}\right\}_{i=0}^{m^{2}-1}
is a standard orthonormal basis in
\mathcal{M}_{m}
,
p_{\sigma}\in L^{2}\left({\mathbb{C}},\gamma_{n}\right)
and
\mathbf{g}\sim\gamma_{n}.

The noise operator
\Gamma_{\rho}:L^{2}\left(\mathcal{M}_{m}^{\otimes h},\gamma_{n}\right)\rightarrow L^{2}\left(\mathcal{M}_{m}^{\otimes h},\gamma_{n}\right)
is defined to be

\Gamma_{\rho}\left(\mathbf{P}\right)=\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}\left(U_{\rho}p_{\sigma}\right)\left(\mathbf{g}\right)\Delta_{\rho}\left(\mathcal{B}_{\sigma}\right),

where
\left\{\mathcal{B}_{i}\right\}_{i=0}^{m^{2}-1}
is a standard orthonormal basis in
\mathcal{M}_{m}
.

Remark 8.9
.

The definition is independent of the choice of bases. For simplicity, let’s assume that
h=1
. Let
\left\{\mathcal{A}_{i}\right\}_{i\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}}
be another standard orthonormal basis in
\mathcal{M}_{m}
and
\mathbf{P}=\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}}q_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{g}\right)\mathcal{A}_{\sigma}
. From
2.8
, there exists an orthogonal matrix
V=\left(V_{ij}\right)_{0\leq i,j\leq m^{2}-1}
with
V_{0,i}=V_{i,0}=\delta_{0,i}
satisfying that
\mathcal{A}_{\sigma}=\sum_{\sigma^{\prime}=0}^{m^{2}-1}V_{\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}}\mathcal{B}_{\sigma^{\prime}}
for
\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}
. Hence,
p_{\sigma^{\prime}}\left(\mathbf{g}\right)=\sum_{\sigma=0}^{m^{2}-1}V_{\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}}q_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{g}\right)
for
\sigma^{\prime}\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}
. Then

\displaystyle\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}}\left(U_{\rho}q_{\sigma}\right)\left(\mathbf{g}\right)\Delta_{\rho}\left(\mathcal{A}_{\sigma}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}}V_{\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}}\left(U_{\rho}q_{\sigma}\right)\left(\mathbf{g}\right)\Delta_{\rho}\left(\mathcal{B}_{\sigma^{\prime}}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\sigma^{\prime}\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}}\left(U_{\rho}p_{\sigma^{\prime}}\right)\left(\mathbf{g}\right)\Delta_{\rho}\left(\mathcal{B}_{\sigma^{\prime}}\right),

where the second equality follows from the linearity of Ornstein-Uhlenbeck operators.

Recall that
\left|\sigma\right|=\left|\left\{i:\sigma_{i}\neq 0\right\}\right|
and
\mathrm{wt}\left(\tau\right)=\sum_{i}\tau_{i}
. The lemma below directly follows from
2.6
and
Lemma

3.5
item 1.

Lemma 8.10
.

Given
0\leq\rho\leq 1
, integers
n,h>0,m>1
and a random operator
\mathbf{P}\in L^{2}\left(\mathcal{M}_{m}^{\otimes h},\gamma_{n}\right)
with an expansion

\mathbf{P}=\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}p_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{g}\right)\mathcal{B}_{\sigma},

where
\left\{\mathcal{B}_{i}\right\}_{i=0}^{m^{2}-1}
is a standard orthonormal basis in
\mathcal{M}_{m}
,
p_{\sigma}\in L^{2}\left({\mathbb{C}},\gamma_{n}\right)
and
\mathbf{g}\sim\gamma_{n},
it holds that

\Gamma_{\rho}\left(\mathbf{P}\right)=\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}\sum_{\tau\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{n}}\rho^{\left|\sigma\right|+\mathrm{wt}\left(\tau\right)}\widehat{p_{\sigma}}\left(\tau\right)H_{\tau}\left(\mathbf{g}\right)\mathcal{B}_{\sigma}.

(36)

where
H_{\tau}
’s are the Hermite polynomials defined in Eqs.
Eq.

15
Eq.

16
and

p_{\sigma}\left(\cdot\right)=\sum_{\tau\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{n}}\widehat{p_{\sigma}}\left(\tau\right)H_{\tau}\left(\cdot\right)
for
\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}
.

The main result in this section is the hypercontractivity of random operators, which is stated as follows.

Lemma 8.11
.

Given integers
n,h>0,m>1
and
0\leq\rho\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{3\sqrt{m}}}
, for any random operator
\mathbf{P}\in L^{2}\left(\mathcal{M}_{m}^{\otimes h},\gamma_{n}\right)
, it holds that

N_{4}\left(\Gamma_{\rho}\left(\mathbf{P}\right)\right)\leq N_{2}\left(\mathbf{P}\right),

where
\Gamma_{\rho}
is a noise operator acting on
L^{2}\left(\mathcal{M}_{m}^{\otimes h},\gamma_{n}\right)
defined in
Definition

8.8
and
N_{p}
is a normalized
p
-norm of a random operator in
Definition

2.22
.

Proof.

Let
\mathbf{P}=\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]^{h}_{\geq 0}}p_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{g}\right)\mathcal{B}_{\sigma}
, where

\left\{\mathcal{B}_{i}\right\}_{i=0}^{m^{2}-1}
is a standard orthonormal basis.
Set
\mathbf{Q}=\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]^{h}_{\geq 0}}\left(U_{\rho}p_{\sigma}\right)\left(\mathbf{g}\right)\mathcal{B}_{\sigma}
. Then by the definition of
\Gamma_{\rho}
,

\Gamma_{\rho}\left(\mathbf{P}\right)=\Delta_{\rho}\left(\mathbf{Q}\right).

Using
Lemma

8.2
,

N_{4}\left(\Gamma_{\rho}\left(\mathbf{P}\right)\right)=\left(\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\Delta_{\rho}\left(\mathbf{Q}\right)\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{4}^{4}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}\leq\left(\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathbf{Q}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{4}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}.

(37)

Let
p_{ij}\in L^{2}\left({\mathbb{C}},\gamma_{n}\right)
and
q_{ij}\in L^{2}\left({\mathbb{C}},\gamma_{n}\right)
be the entries of
\mathbf{P}
and
\mathbf{Q}
, respectively, for
1\leq i,j\leq m^{h}
. Then
q_{ij}=U_{\rho}p_{ij}
.
Notice that

\displaystyle\left(\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathbf{Q}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{4}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m^{h/2}}\left(\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{x}\sim\gamma_{n}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\left(\sum_{ij}\left|q_{ij}\left(\mathbf{x}\right)\right|^{2}\right)^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}

(38)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{m^{h/2}}\left(\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{x}\sim\gamma_{n}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\sum_{ij}\left|p_{ij}\left(\mathbf{x}\right)\right|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=N_{2}\left(\mathbf{P}\right),

where the inequality follows from
Lemma

8.7
. Combining Eqs.
Eq.

37

Eq.

38
, we conclude the result.

∎

The following is an application of
Lemma

8.11
.

Lemma 8.12
.

Given integers
h,n\geq 0
, for any multilinear random operator
\mathbf{P}\in L^{2}\left(\mathcal{M}_{m}^{\otimes h},\gamma_{n}\right)
, it holds that

N_{4}\left(\mathbf{P}\right)\leq 3^{d/2}m^{d/4}N_{2}\left(\mathbf{P}\right),

where
d=\max_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}\left(\deg\left(p_{\sigma}\right)+\left|\sigma\right|\right)
.

Proof.

Suppose

\mathbf{P}=\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}p_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{g}\right)\mathcal{B}_{\sigma},

where
\left\{\mathcal{B}_{i}\right\}_{i=0}^{m^{2}-1}
is a standard orthonormal basis in
\mathcal{M}_{m}
,
p_{\sigma}\in L^{2}\left({\mathbb{C}},\gamma_{n}\right)
is multilinear and
\mathbf{g}\sim\gamma_{n}
. Set

\mathbf{P}^{=i}=\sum_{\left(\sigma,\tau\right)\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}\times\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{n}:\atop\left|\sigma\right|+\mathrm{wt}\left(\tau\right)=i}\widehat{p_{\sigma}}\left(\tau\right)H_{\tau}\left(\mathbf{g}\right)\mathcal{B}_{\sigma}.

Notice that
p_{\sigma}
’s are multilinear as
\mathbf{P}
is multilinear. Thus
\left|\tau\right|=\mathrm{wt}\left(\tau\right)\leq\deg\left(p_{\sigma}\right)
whenever
\widehat{p_{\sigma}}\left(\tau\right)\neq 0
. Applying
Lemma

8.10
and
Lemma

8.11
,

\displaystyle N_{4}\left(\mathbf{P}\right)

\displaystyle=N_{4}\left(\Gamma_{\frac{1}{\sqrt{3\sqrt{m}}}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{d}\left(\sqrt{3\sqrt{m}}\right)^{i}\mathbf{P}^{=i}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq N_{2}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{d}\left(\sqrt{3\sqrt{m}}\right)^{i}\mathbf{P}^{=i}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\sum_{i=1}^{d}\left(\sqrt{3\sqrt{m}}\right)^{i}\mathbf{P}^{=i}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}^{2}_{2}\right]\right)^{1/2}

Note that

\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~\left(\mathbf{P}^{=i}\right)^{\dagger}\mathbf{P}^{=j}\right]=0,

whenever
i\neq j
.
Therefore,

\displaystyle N_{4}\left(\mathbf{P}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\sum_{i=1}^{d}\left(\sqrt{3\sqrt{m}}\right)^{2i}\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathbf{P}^{=i}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}^{2}_{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq 3^{d/2}m^{d/4}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{d}\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathbf{P}^{=i}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}^{2}_{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=3^{d/2}m^{d/4}N_{2}\left(\mathbf{P}\right).

∎

9
Reduction from POVMs to single operators

Let’s define the function
\zeta:{\mathbb{R}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}
as follows.

\displaystyle\zeta\left(x\right)=\begin{cases}x^{2}~&\mbox{if $x\leq 0$}\\
0~&\mbox{otherwise}\end{cases}.

(39)

Lemma 9.1
.

Given an integer
m>0
,
M\in\mathcal{H}_{m}
,
\Delta=\left\{X\in\mathcal{H}_{m}:X\geq 0\right\}
, let

\mathcal{R}\left(M\right)=\arg\min\left\{\left\|M-X\right\|_{2}:X\in\Delta\right\}

be a rounding map of
\Delta
with respect to the distance
\left\|\cdot\right\|_{2}
. It holds that

\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(M\right)=\left\|M-\mathcal{R}\left(M\right)\right\|_{2}^{2}.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we assume that
M
is diagonal. Let

X_{0}=\arg\min\left\{\left\|M-X\right\|_{2}:X\in\Delta\right\}.

The lemma is easy to verify if
X_{0}
is also diagonal. We now show that
X_{0}
is indeed diagonal. Note that

\left\|M-X_{0}\right\|_{2}^{2}=\mathrm{Tr}~X_{0}^{2}+\mathrm{Tr}~M^{2}-2\sum_{i}\lambda_{i}\left(M\right)X_{0}\left(i,i\right),

where
X_{0}\left(i,i\right)
is the
\left(i,i\right)
-th entry of
X_{0}
. It is known that
\left(\lambda_{1}\left(X\right),\ldots,\lambda_{n}\left(X\right)\right)
majorizes
\left(X_{0}\left(1,1\right),\ldots,X_{0}\left(d,d\right)\right)
by Schur’s theorem
[
14
, Page 35, Exercise II.1.12]
. Namely,
\sum_{j=1}^{i}\lambda_{j}\left(X\right)\geq\sum_{j=1}^{i}X_{0}\left(j,j\right)
for
1\leq i\leq d
. Note that
X_{0}\geq 0
. It is easy to verify that

\sum_{i}\lambda_{i}\left(M\right)X_{0}\left(i,i\right)\leq\sum_{i}\lambda_{i}\left(M\right)\lambda_{i}\left(X\right).

The equality is achieved only if
X_{0}
is also diagonal.

∎

The lemma below is the main result in this section, which states that for any
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{X}}
satisfying
\sum_{i}X_{i}=\mathds{1}
, the
\ell_{2}
distance between
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{X}}
and the set of sub-POVMs can be upper bounded in terms of
\sum_{i}\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(X_{i}\right)
.

Lemma 9.2
.

Given
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{X}}\in\left(\mathcal{H}_{m}^{\otimes n}\right)^{t}
satisfying that
\sum_{i=1}^{t}X_{i}=\mathds{1}
, define

\mathcal{R}\left(\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{X}}\right)=\arg\min\left\{{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{X}}-\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{P}}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}:\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{P}}~\mbox{is a sub-POVM}\right\}

It holds that

{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{R}\left(\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{X}}\right)-\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{X}}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{3\left(t+1\right)}{m^{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta(X_{i})+6\left(\frac{t}{m^{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta(X_{i})\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

Proof.

Recall the definitions of
X^{\mathpzc{pos}}
in Eq.
Eq.

21
and the Moore-Penrose inverse
X^{+}
in Eq.
Eq.

22
. Define

\mathcal{L}\left(\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{X}}\right)=\left(\left(Y^{+}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}X_{1}^{\mathpzc{pos}}\left(Y^{+}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},\dots,\left(Y^{+}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}X_{t}^{\mathpzc{pos}}\left(Y^{+}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)

where
Y=\sum_{i=1}^{t}X_{i}^{\mathpzc{pos}}
.

Then by the definition of
\mathcal{R}\left(\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{X}}\right)
,

{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{R}\left(\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{X}}\right)-\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{X}}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}\leq{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{L}\left(\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{X}}\right)-\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{X}}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}.

And

\displaystyle{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{L}\left(\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{X}}\right)-\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{X}}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{t}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\left(Y^{+}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}X_{i}^{\mathpzc{pos}}\left(Y^{+}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}-X_{i}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 3\sum_{i=1}^{t}\left({\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\left(Y^{+}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}X_{i}^{\mathpzc{pos}}\left(Y^{+}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}-\left(Y^{+}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}X_{i}^{\mathpzc{pos}}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\left(Y^{+}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}X_{i}^{\mathpzc{pos}}-X_{i}^{\mathpzc{pos}}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}+{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|X_{i}^{\mathpzc{pos}}-X_{i}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}\right)

Note that

\sum_{i=1}^{t}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|X_{i}-X_{i}^{\mathpzc{pos}}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{t}\frac{1}{m^{n}}\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta(X_{i})

Combining
9.3
and
9.4
, we have

{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{R}\left(\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{X}}\right)-\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longrightarrow}}{{X}}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{3(t+1)}{m^{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta(X_{i})+6\left(\frac{t}{m^{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta(X_{i})\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

∎

Claim 9.3
.

\sum_{i=1}^{t}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\left(Y^{+}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}X_{i}^{\mathpzc{pos}}\left(Y^{+}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}-\left(Y^{+}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}X_{i}^{\mathpzc{pos}}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}\leq\left(t\sum_{i=1}^{t}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|X_{i}-X_{i}^{\mathpzc{pos}}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Claim 9.4
.

\sum_{i=1}^{t}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\left(Y^{+}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}X_{i}^{\mathpzc{pos}}-X_{i}^{\mathpzc{pos}}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}\\
\leq\left(1+\left(t\sum_{i=1}^{t}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|X_{i}-X_{i}^{\mathpzc{pos}}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)\left(t\sum_{i=1}^{t}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|X_{i}-X_{i}^{\mathpzc{pos}}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Before proving these two claims, we need the following lemma, whose proof is deferred to the end of the section.

Lemma 9.5
.

Given Hermitian matrices
A
and
B
the following holds.

1.

If
A\geq B\geq 0
, then
BA^{+}B\leq B
.

2.

If
A\geq 0
, then
(\mathds{1}-A)^{2}\leq\left|\mathds{1}-A^{2}\right|
.

Proof of
9.3
.

Let
\Pi
be a projector onto the support of
Y
. We have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{t}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\left(Y^{+}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}X_{i}^{\mathpzc{pos}}\left(Y^{+}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}-\left(Y^{+}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}X_{i}^{\mathpzc{pos}}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{t}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\left(Y^{+}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}X_{i}^{\mathpzc{pos}}\left(\mathds{1}-\left(Y^{+}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{t}\frac{1}{m^{n}}\mathrm{Tr}~\left(\mathds{1}-\left(Y^{+}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)X_{i}^{\mathpzc{pos}}Y^{+}X_{i}^{\mathpzc{pos}}\left(\mathds{1}-\left(Y^{+}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{t}\frac{1}{m^{n}}\mathrm{Tr}~\left(\mathds{1}-\left(Y^{+}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)X_{i}^{\mathpzc{pos}}\left(\mathds{1}-\left(Y^{+}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)\quad\quad\mbox{(\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{lem:pinvinequality} item 1)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m^{n}}\mathrm{Tr}~\left(\mathds{1}-\left(Y^{+}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)Y\left(\mathds{1}-\left(Y^{+}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)\mbox{\quad(by definition $Y=\sum_{i=1}^{t}X_{i}^{\mathpzc{pos}}$)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m^{n}}\mathrm{Tr}~Y^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\mathds{1}-\left(Y^{+}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)^{2}Y^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{m^{n}}\mathrm{Tr}Y^{\frac{1}{2}}\left|\mathds{1}-Y^{+}\right|Y^{\frac{1}{2}}\quad\quad\mbox{(\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{lem:pinvinequality} item 2)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m^{n}}\mathrm{Tr}\left|Y-\Pi\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{m^{n}}\mathrm{Tr}\left|Y-\mathds{1}\right|

\displaystyle\leq{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathds{1}-Y\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}

\displaystyle={\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\sum_{i=1}^{t}X_{i}-\sum_{i=1}^{t}X_{i}^{\mathpzc{pos}}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{t}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|X_{i}-X_{i}^{\mathpzc{pos}}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(t\sum_{i=1}^{t}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|X_{i}-X_{i}^{\mathpzc{pos}}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

∎

Proof of
9.4
.

Let
\Pi
be a projector onto the support of
Y
. Note that
0\leq X_{i}^{\mathpzc{pos}}\leq Y
for all
i
. We have
\Pi X_{i}^{\mathpzc{pos}}=X_{i}^{\mathpzc{pos}}
. Thus

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{t}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\left(Y^{+}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}X_{i}^{\mathpzc{pos}}-X_{i}^{\mathpzc{pos}}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m^{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\mathrm{Tr}\left(X_{i}^{\mathpzc{pos}}\right)^{2}\left(\Pi-\left(Y^{+}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m^{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\mathrm{Tr}\left(X_{i}^{\mathpzc{pos}}\right)^{2}\left|\Pi-Y^{+}\right|\quad\quad\mbox{(\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{lem:pinvinequality} item 2)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m^{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\mathrm{Tr}\left(X_{i}^{\mathpzc{pos}}\right)^{2}\left(Y^{+}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left|\Pi-Y\right|\left(Y^{+}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m^{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\mathrm{Tr}\left(Y^{+}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(X_{i}^{\mathpzc{pos}}\right)^{2}\left(Y^{+}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left|\Pi-Y\right|.

(40)

Set

\displaystyle D=\begin{pmatrix}X_{1}^{\mathpzc{pos}}&&\\
&\ddots&\\
&&X_{t}^{\mathpzc{pos}}\end{pmatrix}~\mbox{and}~V=\begin{pmatrix}\left(X_{1}^{\mathpzc{pos}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(Y^{+}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
\vdots\\
\left(X_{t}^{\mathpzc{pos}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(Y^{+}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\end{pmatrix}.

From Eq.
Eq.

40
,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{t}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\left(Y^{+}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}X_{i}^{\mathpzc{pos}}-X_{i}^{\mathpzc{pos}}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m^{n}}\mathrm{Tr}~\left(V^{\dagger}DV\left|\Pi-Y\right|\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m^{n}}\left\|V^{\dagger}DV\right\|_{2}\cdot\left\|\Pi-Y\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m^{n}}\left\|V\right\|^{2}\cdot\left\|D\right\|_{2}\cdot\left\|\Pi-Y\right\|_{2},

(41)

where the second inequality follows from the fact that
\left\|ABC\right\|_{2}\leq\left\|A\right\|\left\|B\right\|_{2}\left\|C\right\|
.

Note that

\left\|V\right\|^{2}=\left\|V^{\dagger}V\right\|=\left\|\left(Y^{+}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\sum_{i}X_{i}^{\mathpzc{pos}}\right)\left(Y^{+}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\|=1,

and

\left\|D\right\|_{2}=\left(\sum_{i=1}^{t}\mathrm{Tr}~\left(X_{i}^{\mathpzc{pos}}\right)^{2}\right)^{1/2}\leq\left(\mathrm{Tr}~\left(\sum_{i=1}^{t}X_{i}^{\mathpzc{pos}}\right)^{2}\right)^{1/2}=\left\|Y\right\|_{2}.

Then from Eq.
Eq.

41
,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{t}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\left(Y^{+}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}X_{i}^{\mathpzc{pos}}-X_{i}^{\mathpzc{pos}}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m^{n}}\left\|Y\right\|_{2}\cdot\left\|\Pi-Y\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|Y\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathds{1}-Y\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left({\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathds{1}-Y\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}+{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathds{1}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}\right){\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathds{1}-Y\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(1+\left(t\sum_{i=1}^{t}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|X_{i}-X_{i}^{\mathpzc{pos}}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)\left(t\sum_{i=1}^{t}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|X_{i}-X_{i}^{\mathpzc{pos}}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

We conclude the result.
∎

Proof of
Lemma

9.5
.

For item 1, without loss of generality, we may asume that
A
is diagonal with the following form

A=\begin{pmatrix}\Lambda&\mathbf{0}\\
\mathbf{0}&\mathbf{0}\end{pmatrix}

where
\Lambda>0
is diagonal.

Because
A\geq B\geq 0
,
B
must have the same block structure. Namely,

B=\begin{pmatrix}\Lambda^{\prime}&\mathbf{0}\\
\mathbf{0}&\mathbf{0}\end{pmatrix}

where
\Lambda\geq\Lambda^{\prime}\geq 0
.
If
\Lambda^{\prime}
is invertible, then
\Lambda^{-1}\leq\left(\Lambda^{\prime}\right)^{-1}
by
[
14
, Proposition V.1.6]
. Thus
\Lambda^{\prime}\Lambda^{-1}\Lambda^{\prime}\leq\Lambda^{\prime}
. By continuity, it also holds when
\Lambda^{\prime}
is not invertible. Thus

BA^{+}B=\begin{pmatrix}\Lambda^{\prime}\Lambda^{-1}\Lambda^{\prime}&\mathbf{0}\\
\mathbf{0}&\mathbf{0}\end{pmatrix}\leq\begin{pmatrix}\Lambda^{\prime}&\mathbf{0}\\
\mathbf{0}&\mathbf{0}\end{pmatrix}=B.

For item 2, we again assume without loss of generality that
A
is diagonal of the following form

A=\begin{pmatrix}\Lambda&\mathbf{0}\\
\mathbf{0}&\mathbf{0}\end{pmatrix}

where
\Lambda>0
is diagonal. Then

\left(\mathds{1}-A\right)^{2}=\begin{pmatrix}\left(\mathds{1}-\Lambda\right)^{2}&\mathbf{0}\\
\mathbf{0}&\mathds{1}\end{pmatrix},

and

\left|\mathds{1}-A^{2}\right|=\begin{pmatrix}\left|\mathds{1}-\Lambda^{2}\right|&\mathbf{0}\\
\mathbf{0}&\mathds{1}\end{pmatrix}.

It is not hard to verify that

\left(\mathds{1}-\Lambda\right)^{2}\leq\left|\mathds{1}-\Lambda^{2}\right|
. Thus the result follows.
∎

10
Quantum invariance principle

In this section, we prove a quantum invariance principle with respect to the function
\zeta\left(\cdot\right)
given in Eq. (
39
). Before proving the main result, we need to investigate the analytical properties of
\zeta
.

10.1
Analytical properties of
\zeta

We introduce the Taylor expansions of matrix functions, for which we adopt Fréchet derivatives. The Fréchet derivatives are derivatives defined on Banach spaces. In this paper, we only concern ourselves with Fréchet derivatives on matrix spaces. Readers may refer to
[
20
]
for a more thorough treatment.

Definition 10.1
.

Given a map
f:\mathcal{M}_{m}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{m}
and
P,Q\in\mathcal{M}_{m}
, the Fréchet derivative of
f
at
P
with direction
Q
is defined to be

Df\left(P\right)\left[Q\right]=\frac{d}{dt}f\left(P+tQ\right)|_{t=0}.

The
k
-th order Fréchet derivative of
f
at
P
with direction
\left(Q_{1},\ldots,Q_{k}\right)
is defined to be

D^{k}f\left(P\right)\left[Q_{1},\ldots,Q_{k}\right]=\frac{d}{dt}\left(D^{k-1}f\left(P+tQ_{k}\right)\left[Q_{1},\ldots,Q_{k-1}\right]\right)|_{t=0}.

Fréchet derivatives share many common properties with the derivatives in Euclidean spaces, such as linearity, composition rules, etc. The most basic properties are summarized in
Appendix

A
.

Note that the function
\zeta
is in
\mathcal{C}^{1}
but not in
\mathcal{C}^{2}
. We define a
\mathcal{C}^{2}
-approximation of
\zeta
in the following, whose Fréchet derivatives are easier to calculate comparing with the
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}
-approximation considered in
[
61
,
62
]
.
For any
\lambda>0
, define
\zeta_{\lambda}:{\mathbb{R}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}
to be
3
3

3

The definition of
\zeta_{\lambda}
is derived from the following construction.

\psi\left(x\right)=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{2}~&\mbox{if $-1\leq x\leq 1$}\\
0~&\mbox{otherwise.}\end{cases}

\psi_{\lambda}\left(x\right)=\psi\left(x/\lambda\right)/\lambda

\zeta_{\lambda}\left(x\right)=\zeta*\psi_{\lambda}

\displaystyle\zeta_{\lambda}\left(x\right)=\begin{cases}x^{2}+\frac{1}{3}\lambda^{2}~&\mbox{if $x\leq-\lambda$}\\
\frac{\left(\lambda-x\right)^{3}}{6\lambda}~&\mbox{if $-\lambda\leq x\leq\lambda$}\\
0&\mbox{if $x\geq\lambda$}.\end{cases}

(42)

The following lemma can be verified by elementary calculus.

Lemma 10.2
.

For any
\lambda>0
, it holds that

1.

\left\|\zeta_{\lambda}-\zeta\right\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{\lambda^{2}}{2}.

2.

\zeta_{\lambda}\in\mathcal{C}^{2}
.
\zeta_{\lambda}^{\prime\prime}
is a continuous piecewise linear continuous function.
\zeta^{\prime\prime\prime}_{\lambda}\left(\cdot\right)
exists in
{\mathbb{R}}
except for finite number of points. And
\left|\zeta^{\prime\prime\prime}_{\lambda}\left(x\right)\right|\leq\frac{1}{\lambda}
for any
x
that
\zeta^{\prime\prime\prime}_{\lambda}\left(x\right)
exists.

Lemma 10.3
.

For any Hermitian matrices
P,Q
and
\lambda>0
, it holds that

\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta_{\lambda}\left(P+Q\right)=\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta_{\lambda}\left(P\right)+\mathrm{Tr}~D\zeta_{\lambda}\left(P\right)\left[Q\right]+\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{Tr}~D^{2}\zeta_{\lambda}\left(P\right)\left[Q\right]+O\left(\frac{\left\|Q\right\|_{2}\left\|Q\right\|_{4}^{2}}{\lambda}\right).

(43)

We prove Lemma
10.3
by calculating each order of the Fréchet derivatives combining with several techniques in matrix analysis, which is deferred to
Appendix

B
.

The following lemma enables us to remove the part of an operator with low
2
-norm without changing the value of
\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(\cdot\right)
much. The proof is also deferred to
Appendix

B
.

Lemma 10.4
.

For any Hermitian matrices
P
and
Q
, it holds that

\left|\mathrm{Tr}~\left(\zeta\left(P+Q\right)-\zeta\left(P\right)\right)\right|\leq 2\left(\left\|P\right\|_{2}\left\|Q\right\|_{2}+\left\|Q\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)

.

10.2
Quantum invariance principle for
\zeta\left(\cdot\right)

The following lemma is the main result in this section.

Lemma 10.5
.

Given
0<\tau,\delta,\rho<1
, integers
n>h\geq 0,d>0,m>1
,
H\subseteq[n]
of size
\left|H\right|=h
, a noisy MES state
\psi_{AB}
with the maximal correlation
\rho=\rho\left(\psi_{AB}\right)
, there exists a map
f:\mathcal{H}_{m}^{\otimes n}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2(m^{2}-1)(n-h)}\rightarrow L^{2}\left(\mathcal{H}_{m}^{\otimes h},\gamma_{2(m^{2}-1)(n-h)}\right)
such that the following holds.

For any
P,Q\in\mathcal{H}_{m}^{\otimes n},0\leq P,Q\leq\mathds{1}
, satisfying that
\mathrm{Inf}_{i}\left(P\right)\leq\tau,\mathrm{Inf}_{i}\left(Q\right)\leq\tau
for all
i\notin H
and
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|P^{>d}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}\leq\delta,{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|Q^{>d}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}\leq\delta
,

\left(\mathbf{P},\mathbf{Q}\right)=\left(f\left(P,\mathbf{g}\right),f\left(Q,\mathbf{h}\right)\right)_{\left(\mathbf{g},\mathbf{h}\right)\sim\mathcal{G}_{\rho}^{\otimes 2(m^{2}-1)(n-h)}}\\
\in L^{2}\left(\mathcal{H}_{m}^{\otimes h},\gamma_{2(m^{2}-1)(n-h)}\right)\times L^{2}\left(\mathcal{H}_{m}^{\otimes h},\gamma_{2(m^{2}-1)(n-h)}\right)

are degree-
d
multilinear joint random operators with the joint random variables drawn from
\mathcal{G}_{\rho}^{\otimes 2(m^{2}-1)(n-h)}
. And

1.

N_{2}\left(\mathbf{P}\right)\leq{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|P\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}
and
N_{2}\left(\mathbf{Q}\right)\leq{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|Q\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}
;

2.

\left|\mathrm{Tr}~\left(P\otimes Q\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes n}-\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~\left(\left(\mathbf{P}\otimes\mathbf{Q}\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes h}\right)\right]\right|\leq\delta
.

3.

\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(\mathbf{P}\right)\right]\leq O\left(m^{h}\left(\left(3^{d}m^{d/2}\sqrt{\tau}d\right)^{2/3}+\sqrt{\delta}\right)\right)
and

\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(\mathbf{Q}\right)\right]\leq O\left(m^{h}\left(\left(3^{d}m^{d/2}\sqrt{\tau}d\right)^{2/3}+\sqrt{\delta}\right)\right)
.

4.

The map
f\left(\cdot,\mathbf{g}\right):\mathcal{H}_{m}^{\otimes n}\rightarrow L^{2}\left(\mathcal{H}_{m}^{\otimes h},\gamma_{2\left(m^{2}-1\right)\left(n-h\right)}\right)
is linear and unital.

The main difficulty is to prove item 3, which is obtained via several lemmas. The logical flow of the proof is illustrated in
Fig.

5
.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P\hskip 1.99997pt}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hskip 1.99997ptP^{\leq d}\hskip 1.99997pt}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hskip 1.99997pt\mathbf{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{Given: }\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta(P)=0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta(P^{\leq d})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{Goal: }\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(\mathbf{P}\right)\right]\approx 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta_{\lambda}\left(P^{\leq d}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta_{\lambda}\left(\mathbf{P}\right)\right]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{truncating}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{invariance}}
Lemma

10.4
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\approx}
Lemma

10.2
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\approx}
Lemma

10.7
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\approx}
Lemma

10.2
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\approx}

Figure 5:
Logical flow of the proof of
Lemma

10.5

We define joint random variables
\left\{\mathbf{g}_{i,j}\right\}_{1\leq i\leq n,0\leq j\leq m^{2}-1}
, where

\textbf{g}_{i,0}=1
for all
i\in[n]
and
\left\{\mathbf{g}_{i,j}\right\}_{1\leq i\leq n,1\leq j\leq m^{2}-1}
are independent identical distributions of
\gamma_{1}
.

Given a standard orthonormal basis
\mathcal{B}=\left\{\mathcal{B}_{i}\right\}_{0\leq i\leq m^{2}-1}
, and
M\in\mathcal{M}_{m}^{\otimes n}
with a Fourier expansion

M=\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]^{n}_{\geq 0}}\widehat{M}\left(\sigma\right)\mathcal{B}_{\sigma}.

For any
0\leq i\leq n
, define the hybrid basis elements and the hybrid random operators as follows.

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}^{\left(i\right)}=\prod_{j=1}^{i}\mathbf{g}_{j,\sigma_{j}}\mathcal{B}_{\sigma_{>i}}~\mbox{for $\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{n}$};

(44)

\displaystyle\mathbf{M}^{\left(i\right)}=\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]^{n}_{\geq 0}}\widehat{M}\left(\sigma\right)\mathcal{X}^{\left(i\right)}_{\sigma}.

(45)

Note that
\mathbf{M}^{(i)}\in L^{2}\left(\mathcal{M}_{m}^{\otimes\left(n-i\right)},\gamma_{\left(m^{2}-1\right)i}\right)
is a random operator of dimension
m^{n-i}
.

Lemma 10.6
.

\mathbf{M}^{(i)}
is independent of the choice of bases. Namely, for any standard orthonormal basis
\left\{\mathcal{A}_{i}\right\}_{i=0}^{m^{2}-1}
in
\mathcal{M}_{m}
and
M=\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{n}}\lambda_{\sigma}\mathcal{A}_{\sigma}
, set
\mathbf{N}=\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{n}}\lambda_{\sigma}\left(\prod_{j=1}^{i}\textbf{g}_{j,\sigma_{j}}\right)\mathcal{A}_{\sigma>i}
. Then
\mathbf{N}
and
\mathbf{M}^{\left(i\right)}
have the same distribution.

Proof.

From
2.8
, all orthonormal bases are equivalent up to orthonormal transformations. The lemma follows from the well known fact that the Gaussian distribution
\gamma_{n}
is invariant under any orthonormal transformation. ∎

Lemma 10.7
.

For any integers
n>0,m>1
and
0\leq i\leq n-1
and
M\in\mathcal{H}_{m}^{\otimes n}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\left|\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\frac{1}{m^{n-i-1}}\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta_{\lambda}\left(\mathbf{M}^{\left(i+1\right)}\right)-\frac{1}{m^{n-i}}\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta_{\lambda}\left(\mathbf{M}^{\left(i\right)}\right)\right]\right|\leq O\left(\frac{m^{d/2}3^{d}}{\lambda}\mathrm{Inf}_{i+1}\left(M\right)^{3/2}\right),

where
d=\deg M
.

Proof.

Note that

\displaystyle\mathbf{M}^{\left(i\right)}=\sum_{\sigma:\sigma_{i+1}=0}\widehat{M}\left(\sigma\right)\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}^{\left(i\right)}+\sum_{\sigma:\sigma_{i+1}\neq 0}\widehat{M}\left(\sigma\right)\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}^{\left(i\right)},

\displaystyle\mathbf{M}^{\left(i+1\right)}=\sum_{\sigma:\sigma_{i+1}=0}\widehat{M}\left(\sigma\right)\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}^{\left(i+1\right)}+\sum_{\sigma:\sigma_{i+1}\neq 0}\widehat{M}\left(\sigma\right)\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}^{\left(i+1\right)},

Set

\displaystyle\mathbf{A}=\sum_{\sigma:\sigma_{i+1}=0}\widehat{M}\left(\sigma\right)\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}^{\left(i\right)}

\displaystyle\mathbf{B}=\sum_{\sigma:\sigma_{i+1}\neq 0}\widehat{M}\left(\sigma\right)\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}^{\left(i\right)},

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}=\sum_{\sigma:\sigma_{i+1}=0}\widehat{M}\left(\sigma\right)\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}^{\left(i+1\right)}

\displaystyle\mathbf{D}=\sum_{\sigma:\sigma_{i+1}\neq 0}\widehat{M}\left(\sigma\right)\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}^{\left(i+1\right)}.

Then we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{M}^{\left(i\right)}=\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{B};~\mathbf{M}^{\left(i+1\right)}=\mathbf{C}+\mathbf{D}.

Notice that
\mathbf{A}=\mathds{1}_{m}\otimes\mathbf{C}
, where
\mathds{1}_{m}
is placed in the
(i+1)
-th register. Thus

\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta_{\lambda}\left(\mathbf{A}\right)=m\cdot\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta_{\lambda}\left(\mathbf{C}\right).

(46)

From Eq.
Eq.

46
and
Lemma

10.3
,

\displaystyle\left|\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\frac{1}{m^{n-i-1}}\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta_{\lambda}\left(\mathbf{M}^{\left(i+1\right)}\right)-\frac{1}{m^{n-i}}\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta_{\lambda}\left(\mathbf{M}^{\left(i\right)}\right)\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\frac{1}{m^{n-i-1}}\left(\mathrm{Tr}~D\zeta_{\lambda}\left(\mathbf{C}\right)\left[\mathbf{D}\right]+\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{Tr}~D^{2}\zeta_{\lambda}\left(\mathbf{C}\right)\left[\mathbf{D}\right]\right)-\atop\frac{1}{m^{n-i}}\left(\mathrm{Tr}~D\zeta_{\lambda}\left(\mathbf{A}\right)\left[\mathbf{B}\right]+\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{Tr}~D^{2}\zeta_{\lambda}\left(\mathbf{A}\right)\left[\mathbf{B}\right]\right)\right]\right|

\displaystyle+O\left(\frac{1}{m^{n-i-1}}\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\frac{\left\|\mathbf{D}\right\|_{2}\left\|\mathbf{D}\right\|_{4}^{2}}{\lambda}\right]\right)+O\left(\frac{1}{m^{n-i}}\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\frac{\left\|\mathbf{B}\right\|_{2}\left\|\mathbf{B}\right\|_{4}^{2}}{\lambda}\right]\right)\quad\quad\mbox{(\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{lem:zetataylor})}

\displaystyle\leq\left|\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\frac{1}{m^{n-i-1}}\left(\mathrm{Tr}~D\zeta_{\lambda}\left(\mathbf{C}\right)\left[\mathbf{D}\right]+\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{Tr}~D^{2}\zeta_{\lambda}\left(\mathbf{C}\right)\left[\mathbf{D}\right]\right)-\atop\frac{1}{m^{n-i}}\left(\mathrm{Tr}~D\zeta_{\lambda}\left(\mathbf{A}\right)\left[\mathbf{B}\right]+\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{Tr}~D^{2}\zeta_{\lambda}\left(\mathbf{A}\right)\left[\mathbf{B}\right]\right)\right]\right|

\displaystyle+O\left(\frac{1}{\lambda}\left(N_{2}\left(\mathbf{D}\right)N_{4}\left(\mathbf{D}\right)^{2}+N_{2}\left(\mathbf{B}\right)N_{4}\left(\mathbf{B}\right)^{2}\right)\right)\quad\quad\mbox{(Cauchy-Schwarz inequality)}

We show below in
10.8
that the first term is zero. Thus

\displaystyle\left|\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\frac{1}{m^{n-i-1}}\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta_{\lambda}\left(\mathbf{M}^{\left(i+1\right)}\right)-\frac{1}{m^{n-i}}\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta_{\lambda}\left(\mathbf{M}^{\left(i\right)}\right)\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~O\left(\frac{1}{\lambda}\left(N_{2}\left(\mathbf{D}\right)N_{4}\left(\mathbf{D}\right)^{2}+N_{2}\left(\mathbf{B}\right)N_{4}\left(\mathbf{B}\right)^{2}\right)\right).

Applying
Lemma

8.12
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\frac{1}{m^{n-i-1}}\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta_{\lambda}\left(\mathbf{M}^{\left(i+1\right)}\right)-\frac{1}{m^{n-i}}\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta_{\lambda}\left(\mathbf{M}^{\left(i\right)}\right)\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~O\left(\frac{3^{d}m^{d/2}}{\lambda}\left(N_{2}\left(\mathbf{B}\right)^{3}+N_{2}\left(\mathbf{D}\right)^{3}\right)\right).

Notice that

N_{2}\left(\mathbf{B}\right)=N_{2}\left(\mathbf{D}\right)=\left(\sum_{\sigma:\sigma_{i+1}\neq 0}\left|\widehat{M}\left(\sigma\right)^{2}\right|\right)^{1/2}=\mathrm{Inf}_{i+1}\left(M\right)^{1/2}.

Therefore,

\left|\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\frac{1}{m^{n-i-1}}\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta_{\lambda}\left(\mathbf{M}^{\left(i+1\right)}\right)-\frac{1}{m^{n-i}}\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta_{\lambda}\left(\mathbf{M}^{\left(i\right)}\right)\right]\right|\leq O\left(\frac{m^{d/2}3^{d}}{\lambda}\mathrm{Inf}_{i+1}\left(M\right)^{3/2}\right).

∎

Claim 10.8
.

It holds that

\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~D\zeta_{\lambda}\left(\mathbf{C}\right)\left[\mathbf{D}\right]\right]=m\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~D\zeta_{\lambda}\left(\mathbf{A}\right)\left[\mathbf{B}\right]\right];

\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~D^{2}\zeta_{\lambda}\left(\mathbf{C}\right)\left[\mathbf{D}\right]\right]=m\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~D^{2}\zeta_{\lambda}\left(\mathbf{A}\right)\left[\mathbf{B}\right]\right].

The proofs of both claims above are deferred to
Appendix

C
.

Combining
Lemma

10.7
and
Lemma

10.2
, we have the following lemma.

Lemma 10.9
.

Given
M\in\mathcal{H}_{m}^{\otimes n}
, let
\left\{\mathcal{B}_{i}\right\}_{i=0}^{m^{2}-1}
be a standard orthonormal basis in
\mathcal{M}_{m}
. Then for any
\lambda>0
and
H\subseteq[n]
, it holds that

\displaystyle\left|\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\frac{1}{m^{h}}\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{n}}\widehat{M}\left(\sigma\right)\prod_{i\notin H}\mathbf{g}_{i,\sigma_{i}}\left(\bigotimes_{i\in H}\mathcal{B}_{\sigma_{i}}\right)\right)\right]-\frac{1}{m^{n}}\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(M\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O\left(\lambda^{2}+\frac{3^{d}m^{d/2}}{\lambda}\sum_{i\notin H}\mathrm{Inf}_{i}\left(M\right)^{3/2}\right),

where
d=\deg M
and
h=\left|H\right|
.

Proof.

Note that we can replace the quantum registers by Gaussian random variables in any order. Thus, we may assume without loss of generality that
H=[\left|H\right|]
. The conclusion follows from combining
Lemma

10.7
and
Lemma

10.2
.
∎

Lemma 10.10
.

Given
0<\tau,\delta<1
,
M\in\mathcal{H}_{m}^{\otimes n}
,
H\subseteq[n]
of size
\left|H\right|=h
, an integer
d>0
and standard orthonormal basis
\mathcal{B}=\left\{\mathcal{B}_{i}\right\}_{i=0}^{m^{2}-1}
, suppose
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|M\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}\leq 1
and
\mathrm{Inf}_{i}\left(M\right)\leq\tau
for all
i\notin H
. Set

\mathbf{M}=\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{n}:\left|\sigma\right|\leq d}\widehat{M}\left(\sigma\right)\prod_{i\notin H}\mathbf{g}_{i,\sigma_{i}}\left(\bigotimes_{i\in H}\mathcal{B}_{\sigma_{i}}\right).

Then it holds that

\left|\frac{1}{m^{h}}\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(\mathbf{M}\right)\right]-\frac{1}{m^{n}}\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(M^{\leq d}\right)\right|\leq O\left(\left(3^{d}m^{d/2}\sqrt{\tau}d\right)^{2/3}\right)

where
M^{\leq d}
is defined in
Definition

2.14
.

Proof.

Applying
Lemma

10.9
,

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{m^{h}}\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(\mathbf{M}\right)\right]-\frac{1}{m^{n}}\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(M^{\leq d}\right)\right|

(47)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O\left(\lambda^{2}+\frac{3^{d}m^{d/2}}{\lambda}\sum_{i\notin H}\mathrm{Inf}_{i}\left(M^{\leq d}\right)^{3/2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O\left(\lambda^{2}+\frac{3^{d}m^{d/2}\sqrt{\tau}}{\lambda}\mathrm{Inf}\left(M^{\leq d}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O\left(\lambda^{2}+\frac{3^{d}m^{d/2}\sqrt{\tau}d}{\lambda}\right),

where the last inequality comes from
Lemma

2.16
item 4.

Choosing
\lambda=\left(3^{d}m^{d/2}\sqrt{\tau}d\right)^{1/3}
, we conclude the result.
∎

Proof of
Lemma

10.5
.

From
Lemma

7.3
, we may let
\left\{\mathcal{A}_{i}\right\}_{i=0}^{m^{2}-1}
and
\left\{\mathcal{B}_{i}\right\}_{i=0}^{m^{2}-1}
be standard orthonormal bases in
\mathcal{M}_{m}
satisfying that

\mathrm{Tr}~\left(\mathcal{A}_{i}\otimes\mathcal{B}_{j}\right)\psi_{AB}=c_{i}\delta_{i,j}~\mbox{for}~0\leq i,j\leq m^{2}-1,

where
1=c_{0}>c_{1}=\rho\geq c_{2}\geq\dots\geq c_{m^{2}-1}\geq 0
. Recall that
\mathcal{G}_{\rho}
represents a two-dimensional Gaussian distribution
N\left(\begin{pmatrix}0\\
0\end{pmatrix},\begin{pmatrix}1&\rho\\
\rho&1\end{pmatrix}\right)
. We introduce random variables
\left(\left(\mathbf{g}_{i,j}^{(0)},\mathbf{h}_{i,j}^{(0)}\right)\right)_{(i,j)\in[n-h]\times[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}}
as follows.

\displaystyle\mathbf{g}_{i,0}^{(0)}=\mathbf{h}_{i,0}^{(0)}=1~\mbox{for $1\leq i\leq n-h$};

\displaystyle\left(\mathbf{g}_{i,j}^{(0)},\mathbf{h}_{i,j}^{(0)}\right)_{j\in[m^{2}-1]}\sim\mathcal{G}_{c_{1}}\otimes\ldots\otimes\mathcal{G}_{c_{m^{2}-1}}~\mbox{for $1\leq i\leq n-h$};

(48)

\left(\left(\mathbf{g}_{i,j}^{(0)},\mathbf{h}_{i,j}^{(0)}\right)_{j\in[m^{2}-1]}\right)_{1\leq i\leq n-h}
are independent across the indices
i
’s.

Define

\mathbf{P}^{(0)}=\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{n}:\left|\sigma\right|\leq d}\widehat{P}\left(\sigma\right)\left(\prod_{i\notin H}\mathbf{g}^{(0)}_{i,\sigma_{i}}\right)\mathcal{A}_{\sigma_{H}},

and

\mathbf{Q}^{(0)}=\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{n}:\left|\sigma\right|\leq d}\widehat{Q}\left(\sigma\right)\left(\prod_{i\notin H}\mathbf{h}^{(0)}_{i,\sigma_{i}}\right)\mathcal{B}_{\sigma_{H}}.

Then

N_{2}\left(\mathbf{P}^{(0)}\right)^{2}\leq\sum_{\sigma}\left|\widehat{P}\left(\sigma\right)\right|^{2}={\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|P\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2},~N_{2}\left(\mathbf{Q}^{(0)}\right)^{2}\leq\sum_{\sigma}\left|\widehat{Q}\left(\sigma\right)\right|^{2}={\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|Q\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2},

and

\displaystyle\left|\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(P\otimes Q\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes n}\right)-\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~\left(\left(\mathbf{P}^{(0)}\otimes\mathbf{Q}^{(0)}\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes h}\right)\right]\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=\left|\sum_{\sigma:\left|\sigma\right|>d}c_{\sigma}\widehat{P}\left(\sigma\right)\widehat{Q}\left(\sigma\right)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\sigma:\left|\sigma\right|>d}c_{\sigma}\left|\widehat{P}\left(\sigma\right)\right|^{2}\sum_{\sigma:\left|\sigma\right|>d}c_{\sigma}\left|\widehat{Q}\left(\sigma\right)\right|^{2}\quad\quad\mbox{(Cauchy-Schwartz)}

\displaystyle\leq{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|P^{>d}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|Q^{>d}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}\quad\quad\mbox{($c_{\sigma}\leq 1$ due to \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{lem:normofM})}

\displaystyle\leq\delta^{2}.

From
Lemma

10.10
,

\left|\frac{1}{m^{h}}\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(\mathbf{P}^{(0)}\right)\right]-\frac{1}{m^{n}}\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(P^{\leq d}\right)\right|\leq O\left(\left(3^{d}m^{d/2}\sqrt{\tau}d\right)^{2/3}\right)

(49)

\left|\frac{1}{m^{h}}\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(\mathbf{Q}^{(0)}\right)\right]-\frac{1}{m^{n}}\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(Q^{\leq d}\right)\right|\leq O\left(\left(3^{d}m^{d/2}\sqrt{\tau}d\right)^{2/3}\right)

Note that
\zeta\left(P\right)=0
since
0\leq P\leq 1
. Applying
Lemma

10.4
,

\displaystyle\left|\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(P^{\leq d}\right)\right|=\left|\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(P-P^{>d}\right)-\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(P\right)\right|

(50)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\left(\left\|P\right\|_{2}\left\|P^{>d}\right\|_{2}+\left\|P^{>d}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)\leq 4\sqrt{\delta}m^{n}.

Combined with Eq.
Eq.

49
we conclude that

\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(\mathbf{P}^{(0)}\right)\right]\leq O\left(m^{h}\left(\left(3^{d}m^{d/2}\sqrt{\tau}d\right)^{2/3}+\sqrt{\delta}\right)\right).

Similarly, we have

\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(\mathbf{Q}^{(0)}\right)\right]\leq O\left(m^{h}\left(\left(3^{d}m^{d/2}\sqrt{\tau}d\right)^{2/3}+\sqrt{\delta}\right)\right).

It remains to show that the distribution in Eq.
Eq.

48
can be obtained from
\mathcal{G}_{\rho}^{\otimes 2(m^{2}-1)(n-h)}
.
Given
\left(\mathbf{g}_{i},\mathbf{h}_{i}\right)_{1\leq i\leq 2(m^{2}-1)(n-h)}\sim\mathcal{G}_{\rho}^{\otimes 2(m^{2}-1)(n-h)}
, we perform the following substitutions in
\mathbf{P}^{(0)}
and
\mathbf{Q}^{(0)}

\mathbf{g}_{i,b}^{(0)}\leftarrow\begin{cases}1,&\mbox{if $b=0$}\\
\mathbf{g}_{(m^{2}-1)(i-1)+b},&\mbox{otherwise};\end{cases}

\mathbf{h}_{i,b}^{(0)}\leftarrow\begin{cases}1,&\mbox{if $b=0$}\\
\frac{c_{i}}{\rho}\mathbf{h}_{(m^{2}-1)(i-1)+b}\,+\,\sqrt{1-\left(\frac{c_{i}}{\rho}\right)^{2}}\mathbf{h}_{(m^{2}-1)(n-h+i-1)+b},&\mbox{otherwise}\end{cases}

to get
\mathbf{P}
and
\mathbf{Q}
, respectively. Then the distributions
\left(\mathbf{g}_{i,b}^{(0)},\mathbf{h}_{i,b}^{(0)}\right)_{1\leq i\leq n-h,0\leq b\leq 3}
are independent over the indices
\left(i,b\right)
’s.
\left(\mathbf{g}_{i,b}^{(0)}\right)_{1\leq i\leq n-h,1\leq b\leq 3}
and
\left(\mathbf{h}_{i,b}^{(0)}\right)_{1\leq i\leq n-h,1\leq b\leq 3}
are both i.i.d. standard normal distributions. For all
i\in[n-h],j\in[m^{2}-1]
, the correlation between
\mathbf{g}_{i,j}^{(0)}
and
\mathbf{h}_{i,j}^{(0)}
equals to

\displaystyle\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathbf{g}_{i,j}^{(0)}\mathbf{h}_{i,j}^{(0)}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathbf{g}_{(m^{2}-1)(i-1)+j}\left(\frac{c_{i}}{\rho}\mathbf{h}_{(m^{2}-1)(i-1)+j}\,+\,\sqrt{1-\left(\frac{c_{i}}{\rho}\right)^{2}}\mathbf{h}_{(m^{2}-1)(n-h+i-1)+j}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{c_{i}}{\rho}\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathbf{g}_{(m^{2}-1)(i-1)+j}\mathbf{h}_{(m^{2}-1)(i-1)+j}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c_{i}.

Thus,
\mathbf{P}^{(0)}
and
\mathbf{P}
have the same distribution. Same for
\mathbf{Q}^{(0)}
and
\mathbf{Q}
. Items 1 to 3 follow.

Item 4 immediately follows from the construction of the map.
∎

Reversely, the following lemma converts joint random operators to operators.

Lemma 10.11
.

Given
0<\tau,\delta,\rho<1
, integers
n>h\geq 0,d>0,m>1
, a noisy MES state
\psi_{AB}
with the maximal correlation
\rho=\rho\left(\psi_{AB}\right)
, there exist maps
f,g:L^{2}\left(\mathcal{H}_{m}^{\otimes h},\gamma_{n}\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{H}_{m}^{\otimes n+h}
such that for any degree-
d
multilinear joint random operators

\left(\mathbf{P},\mathbf{Q}\right)=\left(\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}p_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{g}\right)\mathcal{A}_{\sigma},\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}q_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{h}\right)\mathcal{B}_{\sigma}\right)_{\left(\mathbf{g},\mathbf{h}\right)\sim\mathcal{G}_{\rho}^{\otimes n}}\\
\in L^{2}\left(\mathcal{H}_{m}^{\otimes h},\gamma_{n}\right)\times L^{2}\left(\mathcal{H}_{m}^{\otimes h},\gamma_{n}\right),

satisfying that
N_{2}\left(\mathbf{P}\right)\leq 1
,
N_{2}\left(\mathbf{Q}\right)\leq 1
, and

(\forall i\in[n]):~\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}\mathrm{Inf}_{i}\left(p_{\sigma}\right)\leq\tau~\mbox{and}~\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}\mathrm{Inf}_{i}\left(q_{\sigma}\right)\leq\tau,

where
\left\{\mathcal{A}_{i}\right\}_{i=0}^{m^{2}-1}
and
\left\{\mathcal{B}_{i}\right\}_{i=0}^{m^{2}-1}
are standard orthonormal bases in
\mathcal{M}_{m}
satisfying

\mathrm{Tr}~\left(\mathcal{A}_{i}\otimes\mathcal{B}_{j}\right)\psi_{AB}=c_{i}\delta_{i,j}~\mbox{for}~0\leq i,j\leq m^{2}-1

and
1=c_{0}>c_{1}=\rho\geq c_{2}\geq\dots\geq c_{m^{2}-1}\geq 0
.
Let
\left(P,Q\right)=\left(f\left(\mathbf{P}\right),g\left(\mathbf{Q}\right)\right)
. The following holds.

1.

\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(P\otimes Q\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes(n+h)}\right)=\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(\mathbf{P}\otimes\mathbf{Q}\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes h}\right)\right];

2.

N_{2}\left(\mathbf{P}\right)={\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|P\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}~\mbox{and}~N_{2}\left(\mathbf{Q}\right)={\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|Q\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2};

3.

\left|\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\frac{1}{m^{h}}\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(\mathbf{P}\right)\right]-\frac{1}{m^{n}}\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(P\right)\right|\leq O\left(\left(3^{d}m^{d/2}d\sqrt{\tau}\right)^{2/3}\right)

and

\left|\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\frac{1}{m^{h}}\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(\mathbf{Q}\right)\right]-\frac{1}{m^{n}}\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(Q\right)\right|\leq O\left(\left(3^{d}m^{d/2}d\sqrt{\tau}\right)^{2/3}\right)

4.

The maps
f
and
g
are linear and unital.

Proof.

Since
\mathbf{P},\mathbf{Q}
are multilinear random Hermitian operators, we can assume that

p_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{g}\right)=\sum_{\mu\in\{0,1\}^{n}}p_{\sigma}(\mu)\prod_{j=1}^{n}\mathbf{g}_{j}^{\mu_{j}};

q_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{h}\right)=\sum_{\mu\in\{0,1\}^{n}}q_{\sigma}(\mu)\prod_{j=1}^{n}\mathbf{h}_{j}^{\mu_{j}},

where
p_{\sigma}(\mu),q_{\sigma}(\mu)\in{\mathbb{R}}
. Then
\mathbf{P}
and
\mathbf{Q}
can be expressed as

\mathbf{P}=\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}\sum_{\mu\in\{0,1\}^{n}}p_{\sigma}(\mu)\prod_{j=1}^{n}\mathbf{g}_{j}^{\mu_{j}}\mathcal{A}_{\sigma};

\mathbf{Q}=\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}\sum_{\mu\in\{0,1\}^{n}}q_{\sigma}(\mu)\prod_{j=1}^{n}\mathbf{h}_{j}^{\mu_{j}}\mathcal{B}_{\sigma}.

Define

P=\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}\sum_{\mu\in\{0,1\}^{n}}p_{\sigma}(\mu)\left(\bigotimes_{j=1}^{n}\mathcal{A}_{\mu_{j}}\right)\otimes\mathcal{A}_{\sigma};

Q=\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}\sum_{\mu\in\{0,1\}^{n}}q_{\sigma}(\mu)\left(\bigotimes_{j=1}^{n}\mathcal{B}_{\mu_{j}}\right)\otimes\mathcal{B}_{\sigma}.

Then

\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(P\otimes Q\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes(n+h)}\right)=\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(\mathbf{P}\otimes\mathbf{Q}\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes h}\right)\right]=\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}\sum_{\mu\in\{0,1\}^{n}}p_{\sigma}(\mu)q_{\sigma}(\mu)\rho^{\left|\mu\right|}c_{\sigma}.

\displaystyle N_{2}\left(\mathbf{P}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}\left|\sum_{\mu\in\{0,1\}^{n}}p_{\sigma}(\mu)\prod_{j=1}^{n}\mathbf{g}_{j}^{\mu_{j}}\right|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\left|\sum_{\mu\in\{0,1\}^{n}}p_{\sigma}(\mu)\prod_{j=1}^{n}\mathbf{g}_{j}^{\mu_{j}}\right|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}\sum_{\mu\in\{0,1\}^{n}}\left|p_{\sigma}(\mu)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle={\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|P\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}.

Similarly,

N_{2}\left(\mathbf{Q}\right)^{2}={\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|Q\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}.

To see item 3, it is critical to observe that for all
i\notin H

\mathrm{Inf}_{i}(P)=\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}\sum_{\mu:\mu_{i}=1}\left|p_{\sigma}(\mu)\right|^{2}=\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}\mathrm{Inf}_{i}\left(p_{\sigma}\right)\leq\tau.

Similarly,
\mathrm{Inf}_{i}\left(Q\right)\leq\tau
.

Then from
Lemma

10.10
, item 3 holds.

Item 4 follows immediately from the constructions of
f
and
g
.
∎

11
Dimension reduction for random operators

The following is the main lemma in this section.

Lemma 11.1
.

Given parameters
\rho\in[0,1],\delta,\alpha>0
, integers
d,n,h>0,m>1
, an
m
-dimensional noisy MES
\psi_{AB}
with the maximal correlation
\rho=\rho\left(\psi_{AB}\right)
, and degree-
d
multilinear joint random operators

\left(\mathbf{P},\mathbf{Q}\right)=\left(\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}p_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{g}\right)\mathcal{A}_{\sigma},\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}q_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{h}\right)\mathcal{B}_{\sigma}\right)_{\left(\mathbf{g},\mathbf{h}\right)\sim\mathcal{G}_{\rho}^{\otimes n}}\\
\in L^{2}\left(\mathcal{H}_{m}^{\otimes h},\gamma_{n}\right)\times L^{2}\left(\mathcal{H}_{m}^{\otimes h},\gamma_{n}\right),

where
\left\{\mathcal{A}_{i}\right\}_{i=0}^{m^{2}-1},\left\{\mathcal{B}_{i}\right\}_{i=0}^{m^{2}-1}
are both standard orthonormal bases in
\mathcal{M}_{m}
satisfying

\mathrm{Tr}~\left(\mathcal{A}_{i}\otimes\mathcal{B}_{j}\right)\psi_{AB}=c_{i}\delta_{i,j}~\mbox{for}~0\leq i,j\leq m^{2}-1

and
1=c_{0}>c_{1}=\rho\geq c_{2}\geq\dots\geq c_{m^{2}-1}\geq 0
, there exists an explicitly computable
n_{0}=n_{0}\left(d,h,\delta,m\right)
, maps

f_{M},g_{M}:L^{2}\left(\mathcal{H}_{m}^{\otimes h},\gamma_{n}\right)\rightarrow L^{2}\left(\mathcal{H}_{m}^{\otimes h},\gamma_{n_{0}}\right)

for
M\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times n_{0}}
, and joint random operators
\left(\mathbf{P}_{M}^{\left(1\right)},\mathbf{Q}_{M}^{\left(1\right)}\right)=\left(f_{M}(\mathbf{P}),g_{M}(\mathbf{Q})\right):

\left(\mathbf{P}_{M}^{\left(1\right)},\mathbf{Q}_{M}^{\left(1\right)}\right)=\left(\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}p^{\left(1\right)}_{\sigma,M}\left(\mathbf{x}\right)\mathcal{A}_{\sigma},\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}q^{\left(1\right)}_{\sigma,M}\left(\mathbf{y}\right)\mathcal{B}_{\sigma}\right)_{\left(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}\right)\sim\mathcal{G}_{\rho}^{\otimes n_{0}}},

such that if we sample
\mathbf{M}\sim\gamma_{n\times n_{0}}
, then with probability at least
1-\delta-2\alpha
, it holds that

1.

N_{2}\left(\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{M}}^{(1)}\right)\leq\left(1+\delta\right)N_{2}\left(\mathbf{P}\right)
and
N_{2}\left(\mathbf{Q}_{\mathbf{M}}^{(1)}\right)\leq\left(1+\delta\right)N_{2}\left(\mathbf{Q}\right)
;

2.

\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{P}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\left(1\right)}\right)\right]\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{\alpha}}\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{P}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(\mathbf{P}\right)\right]

and

\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{Q}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(\mathbf{Q}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\left(1\right)}\right)\right]\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{\alpha}}\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{Q}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(\mathbf{Q}\right)\right];

3.

\left|\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{P},\mathbf{Q}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\left(1\right)}\otimes\mathbf{Q}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\left(1\right)}\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes h}\right)\right]-\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{P},\mathbf{Q}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(\mathbf{P}\otimes\mathbf{Q}\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes h}\right)\right]\right|\\
\leq\delta N_{2}\left(\mathbf{P}\right)N_{2}\left(\mathbf{Q}\right);

4.

the maps
f_{M},g_{M}
are linear and unital for any nonzero
M\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times n_{0}}
.

In particular, one may take
n_{0}=\frac{m^{O(h)}d^{O\left(d\right)}}{\delta^{6}}
.

To prove
Lemma

11.1
, we make use of a recent result about the dimension reduction for low-degree polynomials in Gaussian spaces due to Ghazi, Kamath and Raghavendra
[
31
]
.

Fact 11.2
.

[
31
, Theorem 3.1]

Given parameters
n,d\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
,
\rho\in[0,1]
and
\delta>0
, there exists an explicitly computable
D=D\left(d,\delta\right)
such that the following holds.

For any
n
and any degree-
d
multilinear polynomials
\alpha,\beta:{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}
, and
M\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times D}
, define functions
\alpha_{M},\beta_{M}:{\mathbb{R}}^{D}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}
as

\alpha_{M}\left(x\right)=\alpha\left(\frac{Mx}{\left\|x\right\|_{2}}\right)~\mbox{and}~\beta_{M}\left(x\right)=\beta\left(\frac{Mx}{\left\|x\right\|_{2}}\right).

(51)

Then

\Pr_{\mathbf{M}\sim\gamma_{n\times D}}\left[\left|\left\langle\alpha_{\mathbf{M}},\beta_{\mathbf{M}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{G}_{\rho}^{\otimes D}}-\left\langle\alpha,\beta\right\rangle_{\mathcal{G}_{\rho}^{\otimes n}}\right|<\delta\left\|f\right\|_{2}\left\|g\right\|_{2}\right]\geq 1-\delta.

If
\alpha
and
\beta
are identical and
\rho=1
, we have

\displaystyle\Pr_{\mathbf{M}\sim\gamma_{n\times D}}\left[\left|\left\|\alpha_{\mathbf{M}}\right\|_{2}^{2}-\left\|\alpha\right\|_{2}^{2}\right|\leq\delta\left\|\alpha\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]\geq 1-\delta;

(52)

\displaystyle\Pr_{\mathbf{M}\sim\gamma_{n\times D}}\left[\left|\left\|\beta_{\mathbf{M}}\right\|_{2}^{2}-\left\|\beta\right\|_{2}^{2}\right|\leq\delta\left\|\beta\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]\geq 1-\delta.

(53)

In particular, one may take
D=\frac{d^{O\left(d\right)}}{\delta^{6}}
.

Fact 11.3
.

[
31
, Proposition 3.2]

Given integers
n,k,D>0
, let
\alpha\in L^{2}\left({\mathbb{R}}^{k},\gamma_{n}\right)
, and
\Lambda
be a closed convex set
4
4

4

In
[
31
]

\Lambda
is a simplex of probability distributions. It is not hard to verify that it also holds for closed convex sets.
in
{\mathbb{R}}^{k}
with the rounding map
\mathcal{R}
defined in
Section

2.5
. Let
\alpha_{M}:{\mathbb{R}}^{D}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{k}
be defined analogously to Eq.
Eq.

51
. It holds that,

\Pr_{\mathbf{M}\sim\gamma_{n\times D}}\left[\left\|\mathcal{R}\circ\alpha_{\mathbf{M}}-\alpha_{\mathbf{M}}\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{\delta}\left\|\mathcal{R}\circ\alpha-\alpha\right\|_{2}\right]\geq 1-\delta^{2},

for any
0<\delta<1
.

Before proving
Lemma

11.1
, we introduce a closed convex set in
{\mathbb{R}}^{m^{2h}}
for any given standard orthonormal basis
\left\{\mathcal{A}_{\sigma}\right\}_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}
.

Definition 11.4
.

For any integers
m,h>0
, let
\left\{\mathcal{A}_{\sigma}\right\}_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}}
be a standard orthonormal basis of
\mathcal{H}_{m}
. We define

\Lambda\left(\left\{\mathcal{A}_{\sigma}\right\}_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}\right)=\left\{x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{m^{2h}}:\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}x_{\sigma}\mathcal{A}_{\sigma}\geq 0\right\}.

(54)

We are now ready to prove
Lemma

11.1
.

Proof of
Lemma

11.1
.

From
Lemma

7.2

\displaystyle\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\left(\mathbf{g},\mathbf{h}\right)\sim\mathcal{G}_{\rho}^{\otimes n}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(P\left(\mathbf{g}\right)\otimes Q\left(\mathbf{h}\right)\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes n}\right)\right]=\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}c_{\sigma}\left\langle p_{\sigma},g_{\sigma}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{G}_{\rho}^{\otimes n}},

where
c_{\sigma}=c_{\sigma_{1}}\cdots c_{\sigma_{h}}
.

Let
n_{0}=\frac{m^{12h+12}d^{O\left(d\right)}}{\delta^{6}}
. Applying
11.2
by setting the parameters
\delta\leftarrow\frac{\delta}{m^{2h+2}}
,
D\leftarrow n_{0}
, and a union bound on
\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}
, it holds that

\Pr_{\mathbf{M}\sim\gamma_{n\times n_{0}}}\left[\left(\forall\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}\right)~\left|\left\langle p_{\sigma,\mathbf{M}},q_{\sigma,\mathbf{M}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{G}_{\rho}^{\otimes n_{0}}}-\left\langle p_{\sigma},q_{\sigma}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{G}_{\rho}^{\otimes n}}\right|\leq\delta\left\|p_{\sigma}\right\|_{2}\left\|q_{\sigma}\right\|_{2}\right]\\
\geq 1-\delta/m^{2},

(55)

and
