\Pr_{\mathbf{M}\sim\gamma_{n\times n_{0}}}\left[\left(\forall\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}\right):~\left|\left\|p_{\sigma,\mathbf{M}}\right\|_{2}^{2}-\left\|p_{\sigma}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right|\leq\delta\left\|p_{\sigma}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]\geq 1-\delta/m^{2},

(56)

and

\Pr_{\mathbf{M}\sim\gamma_{n\times n_{0}}}\left[\left(\forall\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}\right):~\left|\left\|q_{\sigma,\mathbf{M}}\right\|_{2}^{2}-\left\|q_{\sigma}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right|\leq\delta\left\|q_{\sigma}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]\geq 1-\delta/m^{2}.

(57)

Define

\displaystyle\left(f_{M}\left(\mathbf{P}\right),g_{M}\left(\mathbf{Q}\right)\right)=\left(\mathbf{P}^{\left(1\right)}_{M},\mathbf{Q}^{\left(1\right)}_{M}\right)

(58)

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}p_{\sigma,M}\left(\mathbf{g}\right)\mathcal{A}_{\sigma},\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}q_{\sigma,M}\left(\mathbf{h}\right)\mathcal{B}_{\sigma}\right)_{\left(\mathbf{g},\mathbf{h}\right)\sim\mathcal{G}_{\rho}^{\otimes n_{0}}}.

For any
M
satisfying Eq.
Eq.

55
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(\mathbf{P}_{M}^{\left(1\right)}\otimes\mathbf{Q}_{M}^{\left(1\right)}\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes h}\right)\right]-\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(\mathbf{P}\otimes\mathbf{Q}\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes h}\right)\right]\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}c_{\sigma}\left(\left\langle p^{\left(1\right)}_{\sigma,M},q^{\left(1\right)}_{\sigma,M}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{G}_{\rho}^{\otimes n_{0}}}-\left\langle p_{\sigma},q_{\sigma}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{G}_{\rho}^{\otimes n}}\right)\right|\quad\quad\mbox{(\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{lem:convariancetensor})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\delta\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}\left\|p_{\sigma}\right\|_{2}\left\|q_{\sigma}\right\|_{2}\quad\quad\mbox{(Eq.~\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{eqn:mpq} and $c_{\sigma}\leq 1$ due to \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{lem:normofM})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\delta\left(\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}\left\|p_{\sigma}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{1/2}\left(\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}\left\|q_{\sigma}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta N_{2}\left(\mathbf{P}\right)N_{2}\left(\mathbf{Q}\right)\quad\quad\mbox{(\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{lem:randoperator})}.

Thus

\Pr_{\mathbf{M}}\left[\left|\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{P},\mathbf{Q}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\left(1\right)}\otimes\mathbf{Q}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\left(1\right)}\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes h}\right)\right]-\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{P},\mathbf{Q}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(\mathbf{P}\otimes\mathbf{Q}\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes h}\right)\right]\right|\leq\delta N_{2}\left(\mathbf{P}\right)N_{2}\left(\mathbf{Q}\right)\right]\\
\geq 1-\delta/m^{2}.

(59)

For any
M
satisfying Eq.
Eq.

56
,

N_{2}\left(\mathbf{P}_{M}^{(1)}\right)^{2}=\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}\left\|p_{\sigma,M}\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq\left(1+\delta\right)\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}\left\|p_{\sigma}\right\|_{2}^{2}=\left(1+\delta\right)N_{2}\left(\mathbf{P}\right)^{2},

where both equalities are from
Lemma

2.23
. Hence

\Pr_{\mathbf{M}\sim\gamma_{n\times n_{0}}}\left[N_{2}\left(\mathbf{P}^{(1)}_{\mathbf{M}}\right)\leq\left(1+\delta\right)N_{2}\left(\mathbf{P}\right)\right]\geq 1-\delta/m^{2}.

(60)

Using the same argument and Eq. (
56
), we have

\Pr_{\mathbf{M}\sim\gamma_{n\times n_{0}}}\left[N_{2}\left(\mathbf{Q}^{(1)}_{\mathbf{M}}\right)\leq\left(1+\delta\right)N_{2}\left(\mathbf{Q}\right)\right]\geq 1-\delta/m^{2}.

(61)

Set
\Lambda=\Lambda\left(\left\{\mathcal{A}_{\sigma}\right\}_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}\right)
as defined in
Eq.

54
, which is a closed convex set. Let
\mathcal{R}
be a rounding map of
\Lambda
defined in
Section

2.5
. For any random operator
\mathbf{P}\in L^{2}\left(\mathcal{H}_{m}^{\otimes h},\gamma_{n}\right)
, let
p
be the associated vector-valued function under the basis
\left\{\mathcal{A}_{\sigma}\right\}_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}}
defined in
Definition

2.21
. Then we have

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{R}\circ p-p\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{g}\sim\gamma_{n}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\left(\mathcal{R}\circ p_{\sigma}-p_{\sigma}\right)^{2}(\mathbf{g})\right]

\displaystyle=\left(N_{2}\left(\theta(\mathbf{P})-\mathbf{P}\right)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m^{h}}\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{P}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(\mathbf{P}\right)\right],

where for
M\in\mathcal{H}_{m}^{\otimes n}
,

\theta(M)=\arg\min\left\{\left\|M-X\right\|_{2}:X\geq 0\right\},

and the equality follows from
Lemma

9.1
.
Hence
11.3
implies that

\Pr_{\mathbf{M}\sim\gamma_{n\times n_{0}}}\left[\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{P}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(\mathbf{P}^{\left(1\right)}\right)\right]\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{\alpha}}\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{P}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(\mathbf{P}\right)\right]\right]\geq 1-\alpha.

(62)

Applying the same argument to
\mathbf{Q}
and
\mathbf{Q}^{\left(1\right)}
, we have

\Pr_{\mathbf{M}\sim\gamma_{n\times n_{0}}}\left[\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{Q}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(\mathbf{Q}^{\left(1\right)}\right)\right]\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{\alpha}}\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{Q}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(\mathbf{Q}\right)\right]\right]\geq 1-\alpha.

(63)

Again applying a union bound on Eqs.
Eq.

59
Eq.

60
Eq.

61
Eq.

62
Eq.

63
, all the events in Eqs.
Eq.

59
Eq.

60
Eq.

61
Eq.

62
Eq.

63
occur with probability at least
1-\delta-2\alpha
over
\mathbf{M}\sim\gamma_{n\times D}
. Setting
p^{\left(1\right)}_{\sigma,\mathbf{M}}=p_{\sigma,\mathbf{M}}
and
q^{\left(1\right)}_{\sigma,\mathbf{M}}=q_{\sigma,\mathbf{M}}
, we conclude item 1 to 3.

For item 4, let

\mathbf{P}=\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}p_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{g}\right)\mathcal{A}_{\sigma}~\mbox{and}~\mathbf{P}^{\prime}=\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}p_{\sigma}^{\prime}\left(\mathbf{g}\right)\mathcal{A}_{\sigma}.

By the definition of map
f_{M}
in Eq.
Eq.

58
,

\displaystyle f_{M}\left(c\mathbf{P}+c^{\prime}\mathbf{P}^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}\left(cp_{\sigma,M}\left(\mathbf{g}\right)+c^{\prime}p^{\prime}_{\sigma,M}\left(\mathbf{g}\right)\right)\mathcal{A}_{\sigma}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}p_{\sigma,M}\left(\mathbf{g}\right)\mathcal{A}_{\sigma}+c^{\prime}\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}p^{\prime}_{\sigma,M}\left(\mathbf{g}\right)\mathcal{A}_{\sigma}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle cf_{M}\left(\mathbf{P}\right)+c^{\prime}f_{M}\left(\mathbf{P}^{\prime}\right),

for any constants
c
and
c^{\prime}
. It is easy to verify that
f_{M}
is unital.
∎

12
Smoothing random operators

The main result in this section is the following, which is a generalization of
Lemma

6.1
to random operators.

Lemma 12.1
.

Given integers
n,h>0,m>1
, an
m
-dimensional noisy MES
\psi_{AB}
with the maximal correlation
\rho=\rho\left(\psi_{AB}\right)<1
, and joint random operators

\left(\mathbf{P},\mathbf{Q}\right)=\left(\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}p_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{g}\right)\mathcal{A}_{\sigma},\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}q_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{h}\right)\mathcal{B}_{\sigma}\right)_{\left(\mathbf{g},\mathbf{h}\right)\sim\mathcal{G}_{\rho}^{\otimes n}}\\
\in L^{2}\left(\mathcal{H}_{m}^{\otimes h},\gamma_{n}\right)\times L^{2}\left(\mathcal{H}_{m}^{\otimes h},\gamma_{n}\right),

where
\left\{\mathcal{A}_{i}\right\}_{i=0}^{m^{2}-1},\left\{\mathcal{B}_{i}\right\}_{i=0}^{m^{2}-1}
are both standard orthonormal bases in
\mathcal{M}_{m}
satisfying

\mathrm{Tr}~\left(\mathcal{A}_{i}\otimes\mathcal{B}_{j}\right)\psi_{AB}=c_{i}\delta_{i,j}~\mbox{and}~0\leq i,j\leq m^{2}-1

and
1=c_{0}>c_{1}=\rho\geq c_{2}\geq\dots\geq c_{m^{2}-1}\geq 0
, there exists an explicitly computable
d=d\left(\rho,\delta\right)
and a map
f:L^{2}\left(\mathcal{H}_{m}^{\otimes h},\gamma_{n}\right)\rightarrow L^{2}\left(\mathcal{H}_{m}^{\otimes h},\gamma_{n}\right)
such that

\left(\mathbf{P}^{\left(1\right)},\mathbf{Q}^{\left(1\right)}\right)=\left(f(\mathbf{P}),f(\mathbf{Q})\right)\in L^{2}\left(\mathcal{H}_{m}^{\otimes h},\gamma_{n}\right)\times L^{2}\left(\mathcal{H}_{m}^{\otimes h},\gamma_{n}\right)

satisfies the following.

1.

\deg\left(\mathbf{P}^{(1)}\right)\leq d
and
\deg\left(\mathbf{Q}^{(1)}\right)\leq d
;

2.

N_{2}\left(\mathbf{P}^{(1)}\right)\leq N_{2}\left(\mathbf{P}\right)
and
N_{2}\left(\mathbf{Q}^{(1)}\right)\leq N_{2}\left(\mathbf{Q}\right)
;

3.

\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(\mathbf{P}^{(1)}\right)\right]\leq 2\left(\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(\mathbf{P}\right)\right]+\delta m^{h}N_{2}\left(\mathbf{P}\right)^{2}\right)
and

\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(\mathbf{Q}^{(1)}\right)\right]\leq 2\left(\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(\mathbf{Q}\right)\right]+\delta m^{h}N_{2}\left(\mathbf{Q}\right)^{2}\right)
;

4.

\left|\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(\mathbf{P}\otimes\mathbf{Q}\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes h}\right)\right]-\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(\mathbf{P}^{(1)}\otimes\mathbf{Q}^{(1)}\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes h}\right)\right]\right|\leq\delta N_{2}\left(\mathbf{P}\right)N_{2}\left(\mathbf{Q}\right)
;

5.

the map
f
is linear and unital.

In particular, one may take
d=O\left(\frac{\log^{2}\frac{1}{\delta}}{\delta\left(1-\rho\right)}\right)
.

To prove the main result, we again employ the following lemma about smoothing functions on Gaussian spaces in
[
31
]
.

Fact 12.2
.

[
31
, Lemma 4.1]
5
5

5

There are several differences between the statement here and Lemma 4.1 in

]
, which are listed below.

1.

In

,

is a simplex of probability distributions. The same proof also works for any closed convex sets

2.

In

and

Same for

and

.

we can conclude item 4.

The function
f^{(1)}
is obtained by applying the Ornstein-Uhlenbeck operator in Definition

to

and

.

Let
\rho\in[0,1),\delta>0,k,n\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
be any given constant parameters,
f,g\in L^{2}\left({\mathbb{R}}^{k},\gamma_{n}\right)
;
\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{2}\subseteq{\mathbb{R}}^{k}

and
\mathcal{R}_{2}
be rounding maps of
\Lambda_{1}
and
\Lambda_{2}
, respectively. Then there exists an explicitly computable
d=d\left(\rho,\delta\right)
and functions

,where

only depends on

and
g^{(1)}
only depends on
g
, such that the following holds.

1.

Both
f^{(1)}
and
g^{(1)}
are of degree at most
d
.

2.

For any
i\in[k]
, it holds that
\left\|f_{i}^{(1)}\right\|_{2}\leq\left\|f_{i}\right\|_{2}
and
\left\|g_{i}^{(1)}\right\|_{2}\leq\left\|g_{i}\right\|_{2}
.

3.

and

\left\|\mathcal{R}_{2}\circ g^{(1)}-g^{(1)}\right\|_{2}\leq\left\|\mathcal{R}_{2}\circ g-g\right\|_{2}+\delta\left\|g\right\|_{2}.

4.

For every
i\in[k]
,

\left|\left\langle f_{i}\left(\mathbf{x}\right),g_{i}\left(\mathbf{y}\right)\right\rangle_{\mathcal{G}_{\rho}^{\otimes n}}-\left\langle f_{i}^{(1)}\left(\mathbf{x}\right),g_{i}^{(1)}\left(\mathbf{y}\right)\right\rangle_{\mathcal{G}_{\rho}^{\otimes n}}\right|\leq\delta\left\|f_{i}\right\|_{2}\left\|g_{i}\right\|_{2};

5.

For any constants
c_{1},c_{2}
, it holds that
\left(c_{1}f+c_{2}g\right)^{(1)}=c_{1}f^{(1)}+c_{2}g^{(1)}
and
f^{(1)}=f
if
f
is a constant function.

In particular, one may take
d=O\left(\frac{\log^{2}\frac{1}{\delta}}{\delta\left(1-\rho\right)}\right)
.

Let’s proceed to the proof of
Lemma

12.1
.

Proof of
Lemma

12.1
.

Recall that
p
and
q
are the associated vector-valued functions of
\mathbf{P}
and
\mathbf{Q}
under the bases
\left\{\mathcal{A}_{i}\right\}_{i=0}^{m^{2}-1}
and
\left\{\mathcal{B}_{i}\right\}_{i=0}^{m^{2}-1}
, respectively.

Set

\Lambda_{1}=\Lambda\left(\left\{\mathcal{A}_{\sigma}\right\}_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}\right)~\mbox{and}~\Lambda_{2}=\Lambda\left(\left\{\mathcal{B}_{\sigma}\right\}_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}\right),

as defined in
Eq.

54
.

Applying
12.2
to
\left(p,q\right)
, we obtain

\left(p^{(1)},q^{(1)}\right)
.
Define

\mathbf{P}^{(1)}=\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}p^{(1)}_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{g}\right)\mathcal{A}_{\sigma}~\mbox{and}~\mathbf{Q}^{(1)}=\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}q^{(1)}_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{h}\right)\mathcal{B}_{\sigma}.

Item 1 follows directly.

To prove item 2, we have that

N_{2}\left(\mathbf{P}^{(1)}\right)=\left\|p^{(1)}\right\|_{2}\leq\left\|p\right\|_{2}=N_{2}\left(\mathbf{P}\right),

where both equalities are from
Lemma

2.23
; the inequality follows from
12.2
item 2. The second part of item 2 in
Lemma

12.1
follows by the same argument.

For item 3, Note that both
\Lambda_{1}
and
\Lambda_{2}
are closed convex sets. Let
\mathcal{R}_{1}
and
\mathcal{R}_{2}
be rounding maps of
\Lambda_{1}
and
\Lambda_{2}
, respectively. From
Lemma

9.2
,

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{R}_{1}\circ p-p\right\|_{2}^{2}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m^{h}}\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(\mathbf{P}\right)\right],

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{R}_{2}\circ q-q\right\|_{2}^{2}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m^{h}}\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(\mathbf{Q}\right)\right],

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{R}_{1}\circ p^{(1)}-p^{(1)}\right\|_{2}^{2}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m^{h}}\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(\mathbf{P}^{(1)}\right)\right],

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{R}_{2}\circ q^{(1)}-q^{(1)}\right\|_{2}^{2}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m^{h}}\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(\mathbf{Q}^{(1)}\right)\right].

The
12.2
item 3 implies that

\left(\frac{1}{m^{h}}\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(\mathbf{P}^{(1)}\right)\right]\right)^{1/2}\leq\left(\frac{1}{m^{h}}\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(\mathbf{P}\right)\right]\right)^{1/2}+\delta\left\|p\right\|_{2}.

Note that
\left\|p\right\|_{2}=N_{2}\left(\mathbf{P}\right)
by
Lemma

2.23
. Squaring both sides of the inequality above, we conclude the first inequality in
Lemma

12.1
item 3. The second inequality follows in the same way.

To prove item 4, consider

\displaystyle\left|\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(\mathbf{P}\otimes\mathbf{Q}\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes h}\right)\right]-\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(\mathbf{P}^{\left(1\right)}\otimes\mathbf{Q}^{\left(1\right)}\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes h}\right)\right]\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}c_{\sigma}\left(\left\langle p_{\sigma},q_{\sigma}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{G}_{\rho}^{\otimes n}}-\left\langle p^{(1)}_{\sigma},q^{(1)}_{\sigma}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{G}_{\rho}^{\otimes n}}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\delta\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}\left\|p_{\sigma}\right\|_{2}\left\|q_{\sigma}\right\|_{2}\quad\quad\mbox{(\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{fac:smoothgaussian} item 4 and $c_{\sigma}\leq 1$ due to \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{lem:normofM})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\delta\left(\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}\left\|p_{\sigma}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{1/2}\left(\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}\left\|q_{\sigma}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\delta N_{2}\left(\mathbf{P}\right)N_{2}\left(\mathbf{Q}\right)\quad\quad\mbox{(\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{lem:randoperator})}.

Item 5 is implied by Item 5 in Fact
12.2
.
∎

13
Multilinearization of random operators

The following is the main lemma in this section, which turns joint random operators
\left(\mathbf{P},\mathbf{Q}\right)
to multilinear joint random operators.

Lemma 13.1
.

Given
0\leq\rho<1
,
\delta>0
, integers
d,h,n>0,m>1
, an
m
-dimensional noisy MES
\psi_{AB}
with the maximal correlation
\rho=\rho\left(\psi_{AB}\right)
, there exists
t=O\left(\frac{d^{2}}{\delta^{2}}\right)
and a map
f:L^{2}\left(\mathcal{H}_{m}^{\otimes h},\gamma_{n}\right)\rightarrow L^{2}\left(\mathcal{H}_{m}^{\otimes h},\gamma_{n\cdot t}\right)
such that, for any degree-
d
joint random operators

\left(\mathbf{P},\mathbf{Q}\right)=\left(\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}p_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{g}\right)\mathcal{A}_{\sigma},\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}q_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{h}\right)\mathcal{B}_{\sigma}\right)_{\left(\mathbf{g},\mathbf{h}\right)\sim\mathcal{G}_{\rho}^{\otimes n}}\\
\in L^{2}\left(\mathcal{H}_{m}^{\otimes h},\gamma_{n}\right)\times L^{2}\left(\mathcal{H}_{m}^{\otimes h},\gamma_{n}\right),

where
\left\{\mathcal{A}_{i}\right\}_{i=0}^{m^{2}-1}
and
\left\{\mathcal{B}_{i}\right\}_{i=0}^{m^{2}-1}
are standard orthonormal bases in
\mathcal{M}_{m}
,

\displaystyle\left(\mathbf{P}^{(1)},\mathbf{Q}^{(1)}\right)=\left(f\left(\mathbf{P}\right),f\left(\mathbf{Q}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}p^{(1)}_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{x}\right)\mathcal{A}_{\sigma},\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}q^{(1)}_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{y}\right)\mathcal{B}_{\sigma}\right)_{\left(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}\right)\sim\mathcal{G}_{\rho}^{\otimes n\cdot t}}\in L^{2}\left(\mathcal{H}_{m}^{\otimes h},\gamma_{n\cdot t}\right)\times L^{2}\left(\mathcal{H}_{m}^{\otimes h},\gamma_{n\cdot t}\right)

are multilinear joint random operators. It further holds that

1.

Both
\deg\left(\mathbf{P}^{(1)}\right)
and
\deg\left(\mathbf{Q}^{(1)}\right)
are at most
d
.

2.

For all
\left(i,j\right)\in[n]\times[t]
and
\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}
,

\mathrm{Inf}_{(i-1)t+j}\left(p^{(1)}_{\sigma}\right)\leq\delta\cdot\mathrm{Inf}_{i}\left(p_{\sigma}\right)~\mbox{and}~\mathrm{Inf}_{(i-1)t+j}\left(q^{(1)}_{\sigma}\right)\leq\delta\cdot\mathrm{Inf}_{i}\left(q_{\sigma}\right);

3.

N_{2}\left(\mathbf{P}^{(1)}\right)\leq N_{2}\left(\mathbf{P}\right)~\mbox{and}~N_{2}\left(\mathbf{Q}^{(1)}\right)\leq N_{2}\left(\mathbf{Q}\right);

4.

\left|\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(\mathbf{P}^{(1)}\right)\right]-\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(\mathbf{P}\right)\right]\right|\leq 4\delta m^{h}N_{2}\left(\mathbf{P}\right)^{2}
and

\left|\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(\mathbf{Q}^{(1)}\right)\right]-\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(\mathbf{Q}\right)\right]\right|\leq 4\delta m^{h}N_{2}\left(\mathbf{Q}\right)^{2};

5.

\left|\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(\mathbf{P}^{(1)}\otimes\mathbf{Q}^{(1)}\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes h}\right)\right]-\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(\mathbf{P}\otimes\mathbf{Q}\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes h}\right)\right]\right|\leq\delta N_{2}\left(\mathbf{P}\right)N_{2}\left(\mathbf{Q}\right).

6.

The map
f
is linear and unital.

Definition 13.2
.

Suppose
f\in L^{2}\left({\mathbb{R}},\gamma_{n}\right)
is given with a Hermite expansion
f=\sum_{\mathbf{\sigma}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{n}}\widehat{f}\left(\mathbf{\sigma}\right)H_{\mathbf{\sigma}}
. The
multilinear truncation
of
f
is defined to be the function
f^{\mathpzc{ml}}\in L^{2}\left({\mathbb{R}},\gamma_{n}\right)
given by

f^{\mathpzc{ml}}=\sum_{\mathbf{\sigma}\in\left\{0,1\right\}^{n}}\widehat{f}\left(\mathbf{\sigma}\right)H_{\mathbf{\sigma}}\left(x\right).

Fact 13.3
.

[
31
, Lemma 5.1]
6
6

6

There are several differences between the statement here and Lemma 4.1 in
[
31
]
, which are listed below.

1.

The function
\bar{f}
is obtained by replacing each Gaussian random variable
\mathbf{x}
by
\frac{1}{\sqrt{t}}\left(\mathbf{x}^{(1)}+\cdots+\mathbf{x}^{(t)}\right)
for sufficiently large
t
, where
\mathbf{x}^{(1)},\ldots,\mathbf{x}^{(t)}
are
t
i.i.d. Gaussian variables. Thus,
f\left(\mathbf{x}\right)
and
\bar{f}\left(\bar{\mathbf{x}}\right)
have the same distribution. Same for
g\left(\mathbf{y}\right)
and
\bar{g}\left(\bar{\mathbf{y}}\right)
. This implies item 4, item 8 and the equalities in item 3.

2.

Item 5-7 follow from the analysis in
[
31
, page 47, arxiv version ]
.

Given parameters
\rho\in[0,1],\delta>0
and
d\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, there exists
t=t\left(d,\delta\right)
such that the following holds:

Let
f,g\in L^{2}\left({\mathbb{R}},\gamma_{n}\right)
be degree-
d
polynomials. There exist polynomials
\bar{f},\bar{g}\in L^{2}\left({\mathbb{R}},\gamma_{nt}\right)
over variables

\bar{x}=\left\{x_{j}^{\left(i\right)}:\left(i,j\right)\in[n]\times[t]\right\}~\mbox{and}~\bar{y}=\left\{y_{j}^{\left(i\right)}:\left(i,j\right)\in[n]\times[t]\right\}

satisfying the following.

1.

\bar{f}^{\mathpzc{ml}}
and
\bar{g}^{\mathpzc{ml}}
are multilinear with degree
d
.

2.

\mathrm{Var}\>\!\!\left[\bar{f}^{\mathpzc{ml}}\right]\leq\mathrm{Var}\>\!\!\left[f\right]
and
\mathrm{Var}\>\!\!\left[\bar{g}^{\mathpzc{ml}}\right]\leq\mathrm{Var}\>\!\!\left[g\right]
.

3.

\left\|\bar{f}^{\mathpzc{ml}}\right\|_{2}\leq\left\|\bar{f}\right\|_{2}=\left\|f\right\|_{2}
and
\left\|\bar{g}^{\mathpzc{ml}}\right\|_{2}\leq\left\|\bar{g}\right\|_{2}=\left\|g\right\|_{2}
.

4.

Given two independent random variables
\mathbf{g}\sim\gamma_{n}
and
\mathbf{x}\sim\gamma_{n\cdot t}
, the distributions of
f\left(\mathbf{g}\right)
and
\bar{f}\left(\mathbf{x}\right)
are identical and the distributions of
g\left(\mathbf{g}\right)
and
\bar{g}\left(\mathbf{x}\right)
are identical.

5.

\left\|\bar{f}-\bar{f}^{\mathpzc{ml}}\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{\delta}{2}\left\|f\right\|_{2}
and
\left\|\bar{g}-\bar{g}^{\mathpzc{ml}}\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{\delta}{2}\left\|g\right\|_{2}

6.

For all
\left(i,j\right)\in[n]\times[t]
, it holds that

\mathrm{Inf}_{x^{\left(i\right)}_{j}}\left(\bar{f}^{\mathpzc{ml}}\right)\leq\delta\cdot\mathrm{Inf}_{i}\left(f\right)~\mbox{and}~\mathrm{Inf}_{y^{\left(i\right)}_{j}}\left(\bar{g}^{\mathpzc{ml}}\right)\leq\delta\cdot\mathrm{Inf}_{i}\left(g\right).

7.

\left|\left\langle\bar{f}^{\mathpzc{ml}},\bar{g}^{\mathpzc{ml}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{G}^{\otimes n\cdot t}_{\rho}}-\left\langle f,g\right\rangle_{\mathcal{G}^{\otimes n}_{\rho}}\right|\leq\delta\left\|f\right\|_{2}\left\|g\right\|_{2}
.

8.

For any constants
c_{1},c_{2}
, it holds that
\left(\overline{c_{1}f+c_{2}g}\right)^{\mathpzc{ml}}=c_{1}\bar{f}^{\mathpzc{ml}}+c_{2}\bar{g}^{\mathpzc{ml}}
and
\bar{f}^{\mathpzc{ml}}=f
if
f
is a constant function.

In particular, we may take
t=O\left(\frac{m^{2}}{\delta^{2}}\right).

We are now ready to prove
Lemma

13.1
.

Proof of
Lemma

13.1
.

Applying
13.3
to
\left\{p_{\sigma}\right\}_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}
and
\left\{q_{\sigma}\right\}_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}
we get
\left\{\overline{p_{\sigma}}\right\}_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}
and
\left\{\overline{q_{\sigma}}\right\}_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}
.
Let
p^{(1)}_{\sigma}\left(\cdot\right)=\overline{p_{\sigma}}^{\mathpzc{ml}}\left(\cdot\right)
and

q^{(1)}_{\sigma}\left(\cdot\right)=\overline{q_{\sigma}}^{\mathpzc{ml}}\left(\cdot\right)
.
Define

\left(\mathbf{P}^{(1)},\mathbf{Q}^{(1)}\right)=\left(\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}p^{(1)}_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{x}\right)\mathcal{A}_{\sigma},\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}q^{(1)}_{\sigma}\left(\mathbf{y}\right)\mathcal{B}_{\sigma}\right)_{\left(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}\right)\sim\mathcal{G}_{\rho}^{\otimes n\cdot t}}

Item 1 and item 2 are implied by
13.3
item 1 and item 6, respectively.
Item 3 follows from
Lemma

2.23
and the item 3 in
13.3
.

We will prove the first inequality in item 4. The second inequality follows from the same argument. Set
\Lambda=\Lambda\left(\left\{\mathcal{A}_{\sigma}\right\}_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}\right)
as in
Eq.

54
, which is a closed convex set. Let
\mathcal{R}
be a rounding map of
\Lambda
. Note that
0\in\Lambda
. Thus from
2.25
, for all
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{m^{2h}}
, we have

\left\|\mathcal{R}(x)\right\|_{2}\leq\left\|x\right\|_{2}

(64)

Define vector-valued functions

p=\left(p_{\sigma}\right)_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}},\bar{p}=\left(\bar{p}_{\sigma}\right)_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}~\mbox{and}~p^{(1)}=\left(p^{(1)}_{\sigma}\right)_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}.

By
Lemma

9.2
,

\left\|p-\mathcal{R}\circ p\right\|_{2}^{2}=\frac{1}{m^{h}}\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(\mathbf{P}\right)\right]~\mbox{and}~\left\|p^{(1)}-\mathcal{R}\circ p^{(1)}\right\|_{2}^{2}=\frac{1}{m^{h}}\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(\mathbf{P}^{(1)}\right)\right].

Hence

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m^{h}}\left|\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(\mathbf{P}^{(1)}\right)\right]-\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~\zeta\left(\mathbf{P}\right)\right]\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\left\|p^{(1)}-\mathcal{R}\circ p^{(1)}\right\|_{2}^{2}-\left\|p-\mathcal{R}\circ p\right\|_{2}^{2}\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\left\|\bar{p}^{\mathpzc{ml}}-\mathcal{R}\circ\bar{p}^{\mathpzc{ml}}\right\|_{2}^{2}-\left\|\bar{p}-\mathcal{R}\circ\bar{p}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right|\quad\quad\mbox{(\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{fac:mulilinear} item 4)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\left(\left\|\bar{p}^{\mathpzc{ml}}-\mathcal{R}\circ\bar{p}^{\mathpzc{ml}}\right\|_{2}-\left\|\bar{p}-\mathcal{R}\circ\bar{p}\right\|_{2}\right)\left(\left\|\bar{p}^{\mathpzc{ml}}-\mathcal{R}\circ\bar{p}^{\mathpzc{ml}}\right\|_{2}+\left\|\bar{p}-\mathcal{R}\circ\bar{p}\right\|_{2}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|\left(\left\|\bar{p}^{\mathpzc{ml}}-\mathcal{R}\circ\bar{p}^{\mathpzc{ml}}\right\|_{2}-\left\|\bar{p}-\mathcal{R}\circ\bar{p}\right\|_{2}\right)\left(\left\|\bar{p}^{\mathpzc{ml}}\right\|_{2}+\left\|\mathcal{R}\circ\bar{p}^{\mathpzc{ml}}\right\|_{2}+\left\|\bar{p}\right\|_{2}+\left\|\mathcal{R}\circ\bar{p}\right\|_{2}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4\left\|p\right\|_{2}\left|\left\|\bar{p}^{\mathpzc{ml}}-\mathcal{R}\circ\bar{p}^{\mathpzc{ml}}\right\|_{2}-\left\|\bar{p}-\mathcal{R}\circ\bar{p}\right\|_{2}\right|\quad\quad\mbox{(\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{fac:mulilinear} item 3 and Eq.~\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{eqn:contraction})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4\left\|p\right\|_{2}\left(\left\|\bar{p}-\bar{p}^{\mathpzc{ml}}\right\|_{2}+\left\|\mathcal{R}\circ\bar{p}-\mathcal{R}\circ\bar{p}^{\mathpzc{ml}}\right\|_{2}\right)\quad\quad\mbox{(Triangle inequality)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 8\left\|p\right\|_{2}\left\|\bar{p}-\bar{p}^{\mathpzc{ml}}\right\|_{2}\quad\quad\mbox{(\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{fac:rounding})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4\delta\left\|p\right\|_{2}^{2}\quad\quad\mbox{(\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{fac:mulilinear} item 5)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4\delta N_{2}\left(\mathbf{P}\right)^{2}\quad\quad\mbox{(\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{lem:randoperator})}.

To prove item 5, consider

\displaystyle\left|\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(\mathbf{P}\otimes\mathbf{Q}\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes h}\right)\right]-\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(\mathbf{P}^{\left(1\right)}\otimes\mathbf{Q}^{\left(1\right)}\right)\psi_{AB}^{\otimes h}\right)\right]\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}c_{\sigma}\left(\left\langle p_{\sigma},q_{\sigma}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{G}_{\rho}^{\otimes n}}-\left\langle p^{(1)}_{\sigma},q^{(1)}_{\sigma}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{G}_{\rho}^{\otimes n\cdot t}}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\delta\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}\left\|p_{\sigma}\right\|_{2}\left\|q_{\sigma}\right\|_{2}\quad\quad\mbox{(\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{fac:mulilinear} item 7)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\delta\left(\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}\left\|p_{\sigma}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{1/2}\left(\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}^{h}}\left\|q_{\sigma}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta N_{2}\left(\mathbf{P}\right)N_{2}\left(\mathbf{Q}\right)\quad\quad\mbox{(\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{lem:randoperator})}.

Item 6 is implied by Item 8 in Fact
13.3
.
∎

Appendix A
Facts on Fréchet derivatives

In this section, we summarize some basic facts on Fréchet derivatives.

Fact A.1
.

Given
f,g:\mathcal{M}_{m}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{m}
and
P,Q_{1},\ldots,Q_{k}\in\mathcal{M}_{m}
, it holds that

1.

D\left(f+g\right)\left(P\right)\left[Q\right]=Df\left(P\right)\left[Q\right]+Dg\left(P\right)\left[Q\right]
.

2.

D\left(f\cdot g\right)\left(P\right)\left[Q\right]=Df\left(P\right)\left[Q\right]\cdot g\left(P\right)+f\left(P\right)\cdot Dg\left(P\right)\left[Q\right]
.

3.

D\left(g\circ f\right)\left(P\right)\left[Q\right]=\left(Dg\left(f\left(P\right)\right)\circ Df\left(P\right)\right)\left[Q\right]
. Here we are treating
Df(P)
as
a function mapping a matrix
Q
to a matrix
Df(P)(Q)
, and
\circ
means composition.

4.

D^{k}f\left(P\right)\left[Q_{1},\ldots,Q_{k}\right]=D^{k}f\left(P\right)\left[Q_{\sigma\left(1\right)},\ldots,Q_{\sigma\left(k\right)}\right]
for any integer
k>0
and permutation
\sigma\in S_{k}
.

The following fact follows from elementary matrix calculations. Readers who are interested may refer to
[
14
, Chapter X.4]
.

Fact A.2
.

[
14
, Page 311, Example X.4.2]

1.

Let
f\left(x\right)=x^{2}
. Then

Df\left(P\right)\left[Q\right]=\left\{P,Q\right\},

where
\left\{P,Q\right\}=PQ+QP
is the anticommutator of
A
and
B
.

2.

Let
f\left(x\right)=x^{-1}
. Then for any invertible
P
,

Df\left(P\right)\left[Q\right]=-P^{-1}QP^{-1}.

Fact A.3
.

[
14
, Page 124, Theorem V.3.3]

Let
f\in\mathcal{C}^{1}({\mathbb{R}})
and
P,Q\in\mathcal{H}_{m}
. Suppose that
P
has a spectral decomposition
U\Lambda U^{\dagger}
, where
\Lambda=\mathrm{diag}\left(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2},\dots,\lambda_{m}\right)
and
U
is unitary. Then

Df(P)[Q]=U\left(f^{[1]}(\Lambda)\circ\left(U^{\dagger}QU\right)\right)U^{\dagger}

where

f^{[1]}(\Lambda)_{ij}=\begin{cases}\frac{f\left(\lambda_{i}\right)-f\left(\lambda_{j}\right)}{\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j}}&\mbox{if $\lambda_{i}\neq\lambda_{j}$}\\
f^{\prime}\left(\lambda_{i}\right)&\mbox{otherwise}\end{cases}

Lemma A.4
.

Let
f
be a real function on
[a,b]
such that
f^{(n-1)}
is continuous on
[a,b]
and
f^{\left(n\right)}\left(t\right)
exists for all
t\in\left(a,b\right)
except for a finite number of points
\left\{t_{1},\ldots,t_{m}\right\}\linebreak\subseteq(a,b)
. Moreover, assume that
\left|f^{\left(n\right)}\left(t\right)\right|\leq M
for all
t\in\left(a,b\right)
and
t\notin\left\{t_{1},\ldots,t_{m}\right\}
. Then for any distinct points
\alpha,\beta
in
[a,b]
, we have

\left|f\left(\beta\right)-P\left(\beta\right)\right|\leq\frac{M}{n!}\left|\beta-\alpha\right|^{n},

where

P\left(t\right)=\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\frac{f^{\left(k\right)}\left(\alpha\right)}{k!}\left(t-\alpha\right)^{k}.

Proof.

Let
L
be the number satisfying that

f\left(\beta\right)=P\left(\beta\right)+\frac{L}{n!}\left(\beta-\alpha\right)^{n}.

It suffices to show that
\left|L\right|\leq M
. Set

g\left(t\right)=f\left(t\right)-P\left(t\right)-\frac{L}{n!}\left(t-\alpha\right)^{n}.

We assume that
t_{1}<t_{2}<\ldots<t_{m}
, without loss of generality.
Then

g\left(\alpha\right)=g^{\prime}\left(\alpha\right)=\ldots=g^{\left(n-1\right)}\left(\alpha\right)=0.

Note that
g\left(\beta\right)=0
. By the mean value theorem,
g^{\prime}\left(\beta_{1}\right)=0
for some
\beta_{1}\in(\alpha,\beta)
. Repeat this for
n-1
steps, we get
\beta_{n-1}\in\left(\alpha,\beta\right)
such that
g^{\left(n-1\right)}\left(\beta_{n-1}\right)=0
.
Note that

g^{\left(n-1\right)}\left(t\right)=f^{\left(n-1\right)}\left(t\right)-f^{\left(n-1\right)}\left(\alpha\right)-L\left(t-\alpha\right).

Set
t_{0}=\alpha
.
Let
i_{0}
be the largest integer such that
t_{i_{0}}<\beta_{n-1}
. Then

g^{\left(n-1\right)}\left(\beta_{n-1}\right)=\left(f^{\left(n-1\right)}\left(\beta_{n-1}\right)-f^{\left(n-1\right)}\left(t_{i_{0}}\right)\right)\\
+\sum_{i=0}^{i_{0}-1}\left(f^{\left(n-1\right)}\left(t_{i+1}\right)-f^{\left(n-1\right)}\left(t_{i}\right)\right)-L\left(t-\alpha\right).

Applying the mean value theorem, we have

g^{\left(n-1\right)}\left(\beta_{n-1}\right)=f^{\left(n\right)}\left(\xi_{i_{0}}\right)\left(\beta_{n-1}-t_{i_{0}}\right)+\sum_{i=0}^{i_{0}-1}f^{\left(n\right)}\left(\xi_{i}\right)\left(t_{i+1}-t_{i}\right)-L\left(\beta-\alpha\right),

where
\xi_{i_{0}}\in[t_{i_{0}},\beta]
and
\xi_{i}\in[t_{i},t_{i+1}]
.
As
g^{\left(n-1\right)}\left(\beta_{n-1}\right)=0
and
\left|f^{\left(n\right)}\left(t\right)\right|\leq M
for any
t
where
f^{\left(n\right)}\left(t\right)
is defined, we have

\left|L\right|\left(\beta-\alpha\right)\leq\left|M\left(\beta-\alpha\right)\right|.

Thus
\left|L\right|\leq M
.
∎

Appendix B
Proofs of
Lemma

10.3
and
Lemma

10.4

Before proving
Lemma

10.3
and
Lemma

10.4
, we first introduce the Lyapunov equation, a well studied equation in control theory
[
58
]
.

Definition B.1
.

Let
P,Q
be two Hermitian matrices in
\mathcal{H}_{m}
. We define the Lyapunov equation.

PX+XP=Q.

(65)

The solution to Eq.
Eq.

65
is denoted by
L\left(P,Q\right)
.

Lemma B.2
.

Given Hermitian matrices
P,Q\in\mathcal{H}_{m}
, the Lyapunov equation
Eq.

65
has a unique solution if and only if
I_{m}\otimes P+P\otimes I_{m}
is invertible. Note that the eigenvalues of
I_{m}\otimes P+P\otimes I_{m}
are
\left\{\lambda_{i}\left(P\right)+\lambda_{j}\left(P\right)\right\}_{1\leq i,j\leq m}
. Thus, it is equivalent to the fact that
P
and
-P
have no common eigenvalues.

Moreover, let
P=UDU^{\dagger}
be a spectral decomposition of
P
, where
D=\textsf{Diag}\left(d_{1},\ldots,d_{n}\right)
satisfies that
d_{i}+d_{j}\neq 0
for any
0\leq i,j\leq n
. Then Eq.
Eq.

65
has a unique solution
X_{0}
and it satisfies that

\left(U^{\dagger}X_{0}U\right)_{i,j}=\frac{\left(U^{\dagger}QU\right)_{i,j}}{d_{i}+d_{j}}.

Proof.

Let
X^{\prime}=U^{\dagger}XU
and
Q^{\prime}=U^{\dagger}QU
. Then we have

DX^{\prime}+X^{\prime}D=Q^{\prime},

which is equivalent to

\left(d_{i}+d_{j}\right)X^{\prime}_{ij}=Q^{\prime}_{ij},

for
1\leq i,j\leq n
.
Hence it has a unique solution if and only if
d_{i}+d_{j}\neq 0
for all
i,j
.
∎

Fact B.3
.

[
14
, Page 205, Theorem VII.2.3]

Let
P
be a positive definite matrix. Then

L\left(P,Q\right)=\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-tP}Qe^{-tP}dt.

Definition B.4
.

For any Hermitian matrices
P,Q
such that
P
is invertible, we define

\ell_{Q}\left(P\right)=L\left(\left|P\right|,PQ+QP\right),

where
\left|P\right|=\sqrt{P^{2}}
.

It is easy to verify that
\ell_{Q}\left(P\right)=Q
if
P>0
.

Definition B.5
.

Given
P,Q\in\mathcal{H}_{m}
, suppose
P
has a spectral decomposition
U\Lambda U^{\dagger}
, where
\Lambda=\mathrm{diag}\left(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2},\dots,\lambda_{m}\right)
and
U
is unitary. Then define

\kappa_{Q}\left(P\right)=U\left(\Lambda^{\prime}\circ\left(U^{\dagger}QU\right)\right)U^{\dagger}

where
\circ
represents the Hadamard product (a.k.a. entry-wise product) and
\Lambda^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H}_{m}
is defined as

\Lambda^{\prime}_{ij}=\begin{cases}\frac{\left(\lambda_{i}+\lambda_{j}\right)^{2}}{\left|\lambda_{i}\right|+\left|\lambda_{j}\right|}&\mbox{if $\lambda_{i}\neq 0$ or $\lambda_{j}\neq 0$}\\
0&\lambda_{i}=\lambda_{j}=0.\end{cases}

(66)

Recall that the anticommutator of
A
and
B
is
\left\{A,B\right\}=AB+BA
.

Lemma B.6
.

For any
P,Q\in\mathcal{H}_{m}
, we have

\kappa_{Q}(P)=\int_{0}^{\infty}\left\{P,e^{-t\left|P\right|}\left(PQ+QP\right)e^{-t\left|P\right|}\right\}dt.

In particular, if
P=\mathrm{diag}\left(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2},\dots,\lambda_{m}\right)
is diagonal, then

\kappa_{Q}\left(P\right)_{ij}=\Lambda^{\prime}_{ij}\cdot Q_{ij},

where
\Lambda^{\prime}
is defined in Eq.
Eq.

66
.

Proof.

Suppose that
P
has a spectral decomposition
U\Lambda U^{\dagger}
, where
\Lambda=
\mathrm{diag}\left(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2},\dots,\lambda_{m}\right)
and
U
is unitary. Let
Q^{\prime}=U^{\dagger}QU
, then

\displaystyle\left(U^{\dagger}\int_{0}^{\infty}\left\{P,e^{-t\left|P\right|}\left(PQ+QP\right)e^{-t\left|P\right|}\right\}dtU\right)_{ij}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}\left\{\Lambda,e^{-t\left|\Lambda\right|}\left(\Lambda Q^{\prime}+Q^{\prime}\Lambda\right)e^{-t\left|\Lambda\right|}\right\}dt\right)_{ij}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(\lambda_{i}+\lambda_{j}\right)^{2}e^{-t\left(\left|\lambda_{i}\right|+\left|\lambda_{j}\right|\right)}Q^{\prime}_{ij}~dt

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Lambda^{\prime}_{ij}Q^{\prime}_{ij}.

We conclude the result.

∎

Lemma B.7
.

Let
P,Q
be Hermitian matrices. The following holds.

1.

Let
f\left(x\right)=\sqrt{x}
for
x\geq 0
. Then

Df\left(P\right)\left[Q\right]=L\left(\sqrt{P},Q\right)
if
P
is positive definite.

2.

Let
f\left(x\right)=\left|x\right|
. Then

Df\left(P\right)\left[Q\right]=\ell_{Q}\left(P\right)
when
P
is invertible.

3.

Let
f\left(x\right)=x\left|x\right|
. Then
Df\left(P\right)\left[Q\right]=\frac{1}{2}\left(\left\{\left|P\right|,Q\right\}+\kappa_{Q}\left(P\right)\right).

4.

Let
p\left(x\right)=\begin{cases}x^{2}~\mbox{if $x\geq 0$}\\
0~\mbox{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Then

Dp\left(P\right)\left[Q\right]=\frac{1}{2}\left\{P,Q\right\}+\frac{1}{4}\left\{\left|P\right|,Q\right\}+\frac{1}{4}\kappa_{Q}\left(P\right).

Proof.

1.

Let
g\left(x\right)=x^{2}
for
x\in{\mathbb{R}}
and
X=Df\left(P\right)\left[Q\right]
. Applying the composition rule in
A.1
, we have

Q=\left(Dg\left(f\left(P\right)\right)\circ Df\left(P\right)\right)\left[Q\right]=Dg\left(\sqrt{P}\right)\left[X\right]=\left\{\sqrt{P},X\right\}.

By
Definition

B.1
,
X=L\left(\sqrt{P},Q\right)
.

2.

Let
g\left(x\right)=x^{2}
for
x\in{\mathbb{R}}
and
h\left(x\right)=\sqrt{x}
for
x\geq 0
. Then
f=h\circ g
. Again applying the composition rule in
A.1
, we have

Df\left(P\right)\left[Q\right]=\left(Dh\left(g\left(P\right)\right)\circ Dg\left(P\right)\right)\left[Q\right]=Dh\left(P^{2}\right)\left[PQ+QP\right]=\ell_{Q}\left(P\right),

where the last equality follows from
Definition

B.4
.

3.

We assume that
P=\mathrm{Diag}(\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{n})
is a diagonal matrix without loss of generality. From
A.3
,

\displaystyle f^{[1]}\left(P\right)_{ij}=\begin{cases}\frac{\lambda_{i}\left|\lambda_{i}\right|-\lambda_{j}\left|\lambda_{j}\right|}{\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j}},&\mbox{if $\lambda_{i}\neq\lambda_{j}$ }\\
2\left|\lambda_{i}\right|,&\mbox{otherwise}.\end{cases}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\frac{1}{2}\left(\left|\lambda_{i}\right|+\left|\lambda_{j}\right|+\frac{\left(\lambda_{i}+\lambda_{j}\right)^{2}}{\left|\lambda_{i}\right|+\left|\lambda_{j}\right|}\right),&\mbox{if $\lambda_{i}\neq\lambda_{j}$}\\
2\left|\lambda_{i}\right|,&\mbox{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Combing with
Lemma

B.6
, we conclude the result.

4.

It follows from the fact that
f\left(x\right)=\frac{1}{2}x^{2}+\frac{1}{2}x\left|x\right|
, the previous item in this lemma,
A.2
item 1 and the linearity of Fréchet derivatives guaranteed by
A.1
item 1.

∎

Fact B.8
.

[
14
, Page 215, Corollary VII.5.6]

\forall A,B\in\mathcal{H}_{m}
,

\left\|\left|A\right|-\left|B\right|\right\|_{2}\leq\left\|A-B\right\|_{2}.

Remark B.9
.

Since for all
A\in\mathcal{H}_{m}

\left\|A\right\|\leq\left\|A\right\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{m}\left\|A\right\|,

we have

\left\|\left|A\right|-\left|B\right|\right\|\leq\sqrt{m}\left\|A-B\right\|

(67)

Fact B.10
.

[
69
, Page 502, Theorem 6.5.29]

\forall A,B\in\mathcal{H}_{m}
,

\left\|e^{A+B}-e^{A}\right\|\leq\left(e^{\left\|B\right\|}-1\right)\left\|e^{A}\right\|

Lemma B.11
.

For all
P,Q\in\mathcal{H}_{m}
that
P
is invertible,
D\ell_{Q}(P)[Q]
exists.

Proof.

\ell_{Q}\left(P\right)=L\left(\left|P\right|,PQ+QP\right)=\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-x\left|P\right|}(PQ+QP)e^{-x\left|P\right|}dx

Since
P
is invertible,
P+tQ
has eigenvalue 0 only for finite choices of
t
and let
\delta
be the minimum of their absolute values. Let
\left\{a_{n}\right\}
be any sequence that converges to zero and
a_{n}\in(-\delta,0)\cup(0,\delta)
for all
n
,

f_{n}(x)=\frac{e^{-x\left|P+a_{n}Q\right|}\left\{P+a_{n}Q,Q\right\}e^{-x\left|P+a_{n}Q\right|}-e^{-x\left|P\right|}\left\{P,Q\right\}e^{-x\left|P\right|}}{a_{n}}

Let
f_{n,i,j}(x)
be the
(i,j)
-entry of
f_{n}(x)
. We have
\left|f_{n,i,j}(x)\right|\leq\left\|f_{n}(x)\right\|
. Thus by Lebesgue’s Dominated Convergence Theorem
[
72
, Page 26, 1.34]
and Heine’s Theorem
[
16
, Page 186, Theorem 1]
, it suffices to show that there exists a function
g
satisfying
\int_{0}^{\infty}\left|g\right|d\mu<\infty
such that for all
x\in(0,+\infty)

\left\|f_{n}(x)\right\|\leq g(x)

\displaystyle\left\|f_{n}(x)\right\|=

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{e^{-x\left|P+a_{n}Q\right|}\left\{P+a_{n}Q,Q\right\}e^{-x\left|P+a_{n}Q\right|}-e^{-x\left|P\right|}\left\{P,Q\right\}e^{-x\left|P\right|}}{a_{n}}\right\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{e^{-x\left|P+a_{n}Q\right|}\left\{P,Q\right\}e^{-x\left|P+a_{n}Q\right|}-e^{-x\left|P\right|}\left\{P,Q\right\}e^{-x\left|P\right|}}{a_{n}}\right.

\displaystyle+\left.2e^{-x\left|P+a_{n}Q\right|}Q^{2}e^{-x\left|P+a_{n}Q\right|}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{e^{-x\left|P+a_{n}Q\right|}\left\{P,Q\right\}\left(e^{-x\left|P+a_{n}Q\right|}-e^{-x\left|P\right|}\right)}{a_{n}}\right\|

\displaystyle+\left\|\frac{\left(e^{-x\left|P+a_{n}Q\right|}-e^{-x\left|P\right|}\right)\left\{P,Q\right\}e^{-x\left|P\right|}}{a_{n}}\right\|

\displaystyle+2\left\|e^{-x\left|P+a_{n}Q\right|}\right\|^{2}\left\|Q\right\|^{2}\quad\quad\mbox{$\left(\left\|AB\right\|\leq\left\|A\right\|\left\|B\right\|\right)$}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\left\|P\right\|\left\|Q\right\|\left\|\frac{\left(e^{-x\left|P+a_{n}Q\right|}-e^{-x\left|P\right|}\right)}{a_{n}}\right\|\left(\left\|e^{-x\left|P+a_{n}Q\right|}\right\|+\left\|e^{-x\left|P\right|}\right\|\right)

\displaystyle+2\left\|e^{-x\left|P+a_{n}Q\right|}\right\|^{2}\left\|Q\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\frac{e^{x\left\|\left|P+a_{n}Q\right|-\left|P\right|\right\|}-1}{\left|a_{n}\right|}\left\|P\right\|\left\|Q\right\|\left\|e^{-x\left|P\right|}\right\|\left(\left\|e^{-x\left|P+a_{n}Q\right|}\right\|+\left\|e^{-x\left|P\right|}\right\|\right)

\displaystyle+2\left\|e^{-x\left|P+a_{n}Q\right|}\right\|^{2}\left\|Q\right\|^{2}\quad\quad\mbox{(\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{fac:exp})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\frac{e^{x\sqrt{m}\left|a_{n}\right|\left\|Q\right\|}-1}{\left|a_{n}\right|}\left\|P\right\|\left\|Q\right\|\left\|e^{-x\left|P\right|}\right\|\left(\left\|e^{-x\left|P+a_{n}Q\right|}\right\|+\left\|e^{-x\left|P\right|}\right\|\right)

\displaystyle+2\left\|e^{-x\left|P+a_{n}Q\right|}\right\|^{2}\left\|Q\right\|^{2}\quad\quad\mbox{(Eq.~\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{eqn:abs})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4x\sqrt{m}\left\|P\right\|\left\|Q\right\|^{2}\left\|e^{-x\left|P\right|}\right\|\left(\left\|e^{-x\left|P+a_{n}Q\right|}\right\|+\left\|e^{-x\left|P\right|}\right\|\right)

\displaystyle+2\left\|e^{-x\left|P+a_{n}Q\right|}\right\|^{2}\left\|Q\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4x\sqrt{m}\left\|P\right\|\left\|Q\right\|^{2}e^{-x\lambda_{\min}\left(P\right)}\left(e^{-x\lambda_{min}\left(P+a_{n}Q\right)}+e^{-x\lambda_{\min}\left(P\right)}\right)

\displaystyle+2e^{-2x\lambda_{\min}\left(P+a_{n}Q\right)}\left\|Q\right\|^{2}.

It is easy to see

\int_{0}^{\infty}\left|4x\sqrt{m}\left\|P\right\|\left\|Q\right\|^{2}e^{-x\lambda_{\min}\left(P\right)}\left(e^{-x\lambda_{min}\left(P+a_{n}Q\right)}+e^{-x\lambda_{\min}\left(P\right)}\right)\atop+2e^{-2x\lambda_{\min}\left(P+a_{n}Q\right)}\left\|Q\right\|^{2}\right|d\mu<\infty.

Note that
\lambda_{\min}\left(P\right)>0
and
\lambda_{\min}\left(P+a_{n}Q\right)>0
. By Lebesgue’s Dominated Convergence Theorem, we conclude the result.
∎

Lemma B.12
.

Let
P,Q
be Hermitian matrices where
P
is invertible. It holds that

D\ell_{Q}\left(P\right)\left[Q\right]=L\left(\left|P\right|,2Q^{2}-2\ell_{Q}\left(P\right)^{2}\right).

(68)

Moreover, if
P=\textsf{Diag}\left(a_{1},\ldots,a_{m}\right)
is diagonal, then

\left(\ell_{Q}\left(P\right)\right)_{i,j}=\frac{Q_{ij}\left(a_{i}+a_{j}\right)}{\left|a_{i}\right|+\left|a_{j}\right|}.

(69)

\left(D\ell_{Q}\left(P\right)\left[Q\right]\right)_{i,j}=2\frac{\sum_{k}Q_{ik}Q_{kj}\left(1-\frac{\left(a_{i}+a_{k}\right)\left(a_{k}+a_{j}\right)}{\left(\left|a_{i}\right|+\left|a_{k}\right|\right)\left(\left|a_{k}\right|+\left|a_{j}\right|\right)}\right)}{\left|a_{i}\right|+\left|a_{j}\right|}.

(70)

Proof.

From the definition of
\ell_{Q}\left(\cdot\right)
in
Definition

B.4
, we have

\left|P\right|\ell_{Q}\left(P\right)+\ell_{Q}\left(P\right)\left|P\right|=PQ+QP.

Taking the Fréchet derivatives on both sides with respect to
Q
, we have

\left|P\right|D\ell_{Q}\left(P\right)\left[Q\right]+D\ell_{Q}\left(P\right)\left[Q\right]\left|P\right|=2Q^{2}-2\ell_{Q}\left(P\right)^{2}.

We conclude Eq.
Eq.

68
.
∎

Fact B.13
.

[
23
]
[Corollary 5]
Given
a_{1},\ldots,a_{m},b_{1},\ldots b_{m}>0
, let
M
be a
d\times d
matrix defined to be
M_{ij}=\frac{a_{i}-b_{j}}{a_{i}+b_{j}}
. For any
d\times d
matrix
A
, it holds that

\left\|A\circ M\right\|_{4}\leq c\left\|A\right\|_{4},

for some absolute constant
c
.

Lemma B.14
.

Given nonzero reals
a_{1},\ldots,a_{m}
, let
M
be a
d\times d
Hermitian matrix defined to be
M_{ij}=\frac{a_{i}+a_{j}}{\left|a_{i}\right|+\left|a_{j}\right|}
. For any
d\times d
Hermitian matrix
A
, it holds that

\left\|M\circ A\right\|_{2}\leq\left\|A\right\|_{2},

and

\left\|M\circ A\right\|_{4}\leq c\left\|A\right\|_{4},

where
c\geq 1
is an absolute constant.

Proof.

Note that
\left\|A\right\|_{2}^{2}=\sum_{ij}\left|A\left(i,j\right)\right|^{2}
. The first inequality follows from the fact that
\left|M\left(i,j\right)\right|\leq 1
for all
i,j
.

To prove the second inequality, we may assume without loss of generality that
a_{1},\ldots,a_{s}>0
and
a_{s+1},\ldots,a_{m}<0
.
Let
A=\begin{pmatrix}A_{1}&A_{2}\\
A_{2}^{\dagger}&A_{3}\end{pmatrix},

where
A_{1},A_{2},A_{3}
are of size
s\times s
,
s\times(d-s)
and
(d-s)\times(d-s)
, respectively. Let
M=\begin{pmatrix}M_{1}&M_{2}\\
M_{2}^{\dagger}&M_{3}\end{pmatrix}
be the same block structure.
Let
P
be a
d\times d
matrix defined to be

P_{ij}=\frac{\left|a_{i}\right|-\left|a_{j}\right|}{\left|a_{i}\right|+\left|a_{j}\right|}.

Then

\begin{pmatrix}0&A_{2}\\
A_{2}^{\dagger}&0\end{pmatrix}\circ P=\begin{pmatrix}0&A_{2}\circ M_{2}\\
-A_{2}^{\dagger}\circ M_{2}^{\dagger}&0\end{pmatrix}.

B.13
implies that

\left\|\begin{pmatrix}0&A_{2}\circ M_{2}\\
-A_{2}^{\dagger}\circ M_{2}^{\dagger}&0\end{pmatrix}\right\|_{4}\leq c\left\|\begin{pmatrix}0&A_{2}\\
A_{2}^{\dagger}&0\end{pmatrix}\right\|_{4},

for some absolute constant
c
. Thus

\left\|\begin{pmatrix}0&A_{2}\circ M_{2}\\
A_{2}^{\dagger}\circ M_{2}^{\dagger}&0\end{pmatrix}\right\|_{4}\leq c\left\|\begin{pmatrix}0&A_{2}\\
A_{2}^{\dagger}&0\end{pmatrix}\right\|_{4}.

Then

\displaystyle\left\|A\circ M\right\|_{4}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\begin{pmatrix}A_{1}&0\\
0&A_{3}\end{pmatrix}\right\|_{4}+\left\|\begin{pmatrix}0&A_{2}\circ M_{2}\\
A_{2}^{\dagger}\circ M_{2}^{\dagger}&0\end{pmatrix}\right\|_{4}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\begin{pmatrix}A_{1}&0\\
0&A_{3}\end{pmatrix}\right\|_{4}+c\left\|\begin{pmatrix}0&A_{2}\\
A_{2}^{\dagger}&0\end{pmatrix}\right\|_{4}

\displaystyle\leq(c+1)\left\|A\right\|_{4},

where the last inequality follows from the fact that

\left\|\begin{pmatrix}A_{1}&0\\
0&A_{3}\end{pmatrix}\right\|_{4}\leq\left\|A\right\|_{4}~\mbox{and}~\left\|\begin{pmatrix}0&A_{2}\\
A_{2}^{\dagger}&0\end{pmatrix}\right\|_{4}\leq\left\|A\right\|_{4}.

∎

Lemma B.15
.

Let
P
and
Q
be Hermitian matrices where
P
is invertible. It holds that

\left\|\ell_{Q}\left(P\right)\right\|_{2}\leq\left\|Q\right\|_{2},

and

\left\|\ell_{Q}\left(P\right)\right\|_{4}\leq c\left\|Q\right\|_{4},

for some absolute constant
c\geq 1
.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we assume that
P=\mathsf{Diag}\left(a_{1},\ldots,a_{m}\right)
is a diagonal matrix. Define
M
be a
d\times d
matrix such that
M_{ij}=\frac{a_{i}+a_{j}}{\left|a_{i}\right|+\left|a_{j}\right|}
. Then by Eq.
Eq.

69
and
Lemma

B.14
,

\displaystyle\left\|\ell_{Q}\left(P\right)\right\|_{2}=\left\|Q\circ M\right\|_{2}\leq\left\|Q\right\|_{2};

\displaystyle\left\|\ell_{Q}\left(P\right)\right\|_{4}=\left\|Q\circ M\right\|_{4}\leq c\left\|Q\right\|_{4}

for some absolute constant
c\geq 1
.
∎

Lemma B.16
.

For any Hermitian matrices
P
and
Q
, it holds that

1.

\mathrm{Tr}~\kappa_{Q}\left(P\right)=2\mathrm{Tr}~\left|P\right|Q
.

2.

\mathrm{Tr}~P\kappa_{Q}\left(P\right)=2\mathrm{Tr}~\left|P\right|PQ
.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we assume that
P=\mathsf{Diag}\left(a_{1},\ldots,a_{m}\right)
is a diagonal matrix. Then
\kappa_{Q}\left(P\right)_{i,j}=\frac{Q_{ij}\left(a_{i}+a_{j}\right)^{2}}{\left|a_{i}\right|+\left|a_{j}\right|}
by
Lemma

B.6
. Thus we have

\mathrm{Tr}~\kappa_{Q}\left(P\right)=2\sum_{i}\left|a_{i}\right|Q_{ii}=2\mathrm{Tr}~\left|P\right|Q.

For the second equality, consider

\mathrm{Tr}~P\kappa_{Q}\left(P\right)=2\sum_{i}\left|a_{i}\right|a_{i}Q_{ii}=2\mathrm{Tr}~\left|P\right|PQ.

∎

Before proving
Lemma

10.3
, we need to compute the first three orders of Fréchet derivatives of the function

q\left(x\right)=\begin{cases}x^{3}~&\mbox{if $x\geq 0$}\\
0~&\mbox{otherwise}.\end{cases}

(71)

Lemma B.17
.

Given integers
d,m>0
and
P,Q\in\mathcal{H}_{m}
, let
f(t)=\mathrm{Tr}~q\left(P+tQ\right)
. Then
f^{\prime},f^{\prime\prime}
exist on
{\mathbb{R}}
and
f^{\prime\prime\prime}
exists except for a finite number of points.

Moreover, it holds that

\displaystyle f^{\prime}\left(0\right)=\mathrm{Tr}~\left(Qp\left(P\right)+P^{2}Q+P\left|P\right|Q\right);

(72)

\displaystyle f^{\prime\prime}\left(0\right)=\mathrm{Tr}~\left(4PQ^{2}+\frac{3}{2}\left|P\right|Q^{2}+\frac{3}{4}Q\kappa_{Q}\left(P\right)\right);

(73)

If
P
is invertible, then

\displaystyle f^{\prime\prime\prime}\left(0\right)=\mathrm{Tr}~\left(4Q^{3}+3Q^{2}\ell_{Q}\left(P\right)+\frac{3}{4}Q\left\{P,D\ell_{Q}\left(P\right)\left[Q\right]\right\}\right).

(74)

Proof.

Notice that

\displaystyle f^{\prime}(0)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Tr}~Dq\left(P\right)\left[Q\right]

\displaystyle f^{\prime\prime}(0)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Tr}~D^{2}q\left(P\right)\left[Q,Q\right]

\displaystyle f^{\prime\prime\prime}(0)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Tr}~D^{3}q\left(P\right)\left[Q,Q,Q\right]

Note that
q\left(x\right)=xp\left(x\right)
, where
p\left(\cdot\right)
is defined in
Lemma

B.7
item 4.

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}~Dq\left(P\right)\left[Q\right]=\mathrm{Tr}~Qp\left(P\right)+\mathrm{Tr}~P^{2}Q+\frac{1}{2}P\left|P\right|Q+\frac{1}{4}\mathrm{Tr}~P\kappa_{Q}\left(P\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathrm{Tr}~Qp\left(P\right)}_{g_{1,Q}\left(P\right)}+\underbrace{\mathrm{Tr}~P^{2}Q}_{g_{2,Q}\left(P\right)}+\underbrace{\mathrm{Tr}~P\left|P\right|Q}_{g_{3,Q}\left(P\right)},

(75)

where the second equality follows from
Lemma

B.16
.

Further taking derivatives of
g_{1,Q}
,
g_{2,Q}
, and
g_{3,Q}
we have

\displaystyle Dg_{1,Q}\left(P\right)\left(Q\right)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Tr}~\left(\frac{1}{2}Q\left\{P,Q\right\}+\frac{1}{4}Q\left\{\left|P\right|,Q\right\}+\frac{1}{4}Q\kappa_{Q}\left(P\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Tr}~PQ^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{Tr}~\left|P\right|Q^{2}+\frac{1}{4}\mathrm{Tr}~Q\kappa_{Q}\left(P\right).

(76)

And

Dg_{2,Q}\left(P\right)\left[Q\right]=2\mathrm{Tr}~PQ^{2}.

(77)

By
Lemma

B.7

Dg_{3,Q}\left(P\right)\left[Q\right]=\mathrm{Tr}~\left(\left|P\right|Q^{2}+\frac{1}{2}Q\kappa_{Q}\left(P\right)\right).

(78)

Combining Eqs.
Eq.

76
Eq.

77
Eq.

78
we conclude

f^{\prime\prime}\left(t\right)=\underbrace{4\mathrm{Tr}~PQ^{2}}_{g_{4,Q}\left(P\right)}+\underbrace{\frac{3}{2}\mathrm{Tr}~\left|P\right|Q^{2}}_{g_{5,Q}\left(P\right)}+\underbrace{\frac{3}{4}\mathrm{Tr}~Q\kappa_{Q}\left(P\right)}_{g_{6,Q}\left(P\right)}.

(79)

Taking the derivative of
g_{4,Q}\left(P\right)
, we have

Dg_{4,Q}\left(P\right)\left[Q\right]=4\mathrm{Tr}~Q^{3}.

(80)

Applying
Lemma

B.7
item 2,

Dg_{5,Q}\left(P\right)\left[Q\right]=\frac{3}{2}\mathrm{Tr}~\ell_{Q}\left(P\right)Q^{2}.

(81)

From
Definition

B.5
,

D\kappa_{Q}\left(P\right)\left[Q\right]=\left\{Q,\ell_{Q}\left(P\right)\right\}+\left\{P,D\ell_{Q}\left(P\right)\left[Q\right]\right\}.

Thus

Dg_{6,Q}\left(P\right)\left[Q\right]=\mathrm{Tr}~\left(\frac{3}{2}Q^{2}\ell_{Q}\left(P\right)+\frac{3}{4}Q\left\{P,D\ell_{Q}\left(P\right)\left[Q\right]\right\}\right).

(82)

Combining Eqs.
Eq.

80
Eq.

81
Eq.

82
, we conclude Eq.
Eq.

74
.
∎

Lemma B.18
.

Given integers
d,m>0
and
P,Q\in\mathcal{H}_{m}
, where
P
is invertible, let
f(t)=\mathrm{Tr}~q\left(P+tQ\right)
. It holds that

\left|f^{\prime\prime\prime}\left(0\right)\right|\leq c\left\|Q\right\|_{2}\left\|Q\right\|_{4}^{2},

for some absolute constant
c
.

Proof.

We upper bound each term in Eq. (
74
).
For the first term, consider

\left|4\mathrm{Tr}~Q^{3}\right|\leq 4\left\|Q\right\|_{2}\left\|Q^{2}\right\|_{2}=4\left\|Q\right\|_{2}\left\|Q\right\|_{4}^{2}.

(83)

For the second term,

\left|3\mathrm{Tr}~Q^{2}\ell_{Q}\left(P\right)\right|\leq 3\left\|Q\right\|_{4}^{2}\left\|\ell_{Q}\left(P\right)\right\|_{2}\leq 3\left\|Q\right\|_{4}^{2}\left\|Q\right\|_{2},

(84)

where the second inequality follows from
Lemma

B.15
.
For the final term, assuming that
P=\mathsf{Diag}\left(a_{1},\ldots,a_{m}\right)
is a diagonal matrix and applying
Lemma

B.12
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\frac{3}{4}\mathrm{Tr}Q\left\{P,D\ell_{Q}\left(P\right)\left[Q\right]\right\}\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{3}{4}\left|\sum_{ijk}Q_{ij}Q_{jk}Q_{ki}\left(\frac{a_{i}+a_{j}}{\left|a_{i}\right|+\left|a_{j}\right|}-\frac{\left(a_{i}+a_{j}\right)\left(a_{j}+a_{k}\right)\left(a_{k}+a_{i}\right)}{\left(\left|a_{i}\right|+\left|a_{j}\right|\right)\left(\left|a_{j}\right|+\left|a_{k}\right|\right)\left(\left|a_{k}\right|+\left|a_{i}\right|\right)}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{3}{4}\left|\sum_{ijk}Q_{ij}Q_{jk}Q_{ki}\frac{a_{i}+a_{j}}{\left|a_{i}\right|+\left|a_{j}\right|}\right|

\displaystyle+\frac{3}{4}\left|\sum_{ijk}Q_{ij}Q_{jk}Q_{ki}\frac{\left(a_{i}+a_{j}\right)\left(a_{j}+a_{k}\right)\left(a_{k}+a_{i}\right)}{\left(\left|a_{i}\right|+\left|a_{j}\right|\right)\left(\left|a_{j}\right|+\left|a_{k}\right|\right)\left(\left|a_{k}\right|+\left|a_{i}\right|\right)}\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{3}{4}\left|\mathrm{Tr}~\left(\ell_{Q}\left(P\right)Q^{2}\right)\right|+\frac{3}{4}\left|\mathrm{Tr}~\ell_{Q}\left(P\right)^{3}\right|\quad\quad\mbox{(Eq.~\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{eqn:hb})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{3}{4}\left\|\ell_{Q}\left(P\right)\right\|_{2}\left\|Q\right\|_{4}^{2}+\frac{3}{4}\left\|\ell_{Q}\left(P\right)\right\|_{2}\left\|\ell_{Q}\left(P\right)\right\|_{4}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c\left\|Q\right\|_{2}\left\|Q\right\|_{4}^{2}\quad\quad\mbox{(\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{lem:dlq3})},

(85)

for some constant
c>1
. Combining Eqs.
Eq.

83
Eq.

84
Eq.

85
, the result follows.

∎

We are now ready to prove
Lemma

10.3
.

Proof of
Lemma

10.3
.

We assume that
P
is invertible. The general case follows by continuity. Then
P+tQ
is invertible except for finite number of
t
’s.

From the definition of
\zeta_{\lambda}
, we have

\zeta_{\lambda}\left(x\right)=x^{2}+\frac{\lambda^{2}}{3}-\frac{q\left(\lambda+x\right)}{6\lambda}+\frac{q\left(x-\lambda\right)}{6\lambda}.

(86)

Note that
q\left(\cdot\right)
is twice differentiable.
q^{\prime\prime\prime}\left(\cdot\right)
exists except for a finite number of points. Thus from
Lemma

A.4
, it suffices to upper bound
\mathrm{Tr}~D^{3}\zeta_{\lambda}\left(P\right)\left(Q\right)
, which is directly implied by
Lemma

B.18
.
∎

Proof of
Lemma

10.4
.

Note that
\zeta\left(x\right)=p\left(-x\right)
. Then from item 4 of
Lemma

B.7

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}~D\zeta\left(P\right)\left[Q\right]=\mathrm{Tr}PQ-\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{Tr}~\left|P\right|Q-\frac{1}{4}\mathrm{Tr}~\kappa_{Q}\left(-P\right)=\mathrm{Tr}\left(P-\left|P\right|\right)Q

where the second equality follows from
Lemma

B.16
.

Assuming that
P=\mathrm{Diag}\left(a_{1},\ldots,a_{d}\right)
is a diagonal matrix, we have

\displaystyle\left|\mathrm{Tr}~D\zeta\left(P\right)\left[Q\right]\right|=\left|\sum_{i}\left(a_{i}-\left|a_{i}\right|\right)Q_{ii}\right|\leq 2\sum_{i}\left|a_{i}Q_{ii}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\left(\sum_{i}\left|a_{i}\right|^{2}\right)^{1/2}\left(\sum_{i}\left|Q_{ii}\right|^{2}\right)^{1/2}\leq 2\left\|P\right\|_{2}\left\|Q\right\|_{2}.

Then by the mean value theorem, there exists
\theta\in[0,1]
such that

\displaystyle\left|\mathrm{Tr}~\left(\zeta\left(P+Q\right)-\zeta\left(P\right)\right)\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\mathrm{Tr}~D\zeta\left(P+\theta Q\right)\left[Q\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq 2\left\|P+\theta Q\right\|_{2}\left\|Q\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|P\right\|_{2}\left\|Q\right\|_{2}+\left\|Q\right\|_{2}^{2}.

∎

Appendix C
Proof of
10.8

Before proving
10.8
, we need the following claim.

Claim C.1
.

It holds that

\displaystyle\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~\mathbf{B}f\left(\mathbf{A}\right)\right]=\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~\mathbf{D}f\left(\mathbf{C}\right)\right]=0;

(87)

\displaystyle\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~\mathbf{B}f\left(\mathbf{A}\right)\mathbf{B}g\left(\mathbf{A}\right)\right]=m\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~\mathbf{D}f\left(\mathbf{C}\right)\mathbf{D}g\left(\mathbf{C}\right)\right]

(88)

for any
f,g\in L^{2}\left({\mathbb{R}},\gamma_{1}\right)
, where
\mathbf{A},\mathbf{B},\mathbf{C},\mathbf{D}
are defined in the proof of
Lemma

10.7
.

Proof.

A crucial observation is that
\mathbf{A},\mathbf{B}
and
\mathbf{C}
can be expressed as

\displaystyle\mathbf{A}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathds{1}_{m}\otimes\mathbf{C}

\displaystyle\mathbf{B}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}:\sigma\neq 0}\mathcal{B}_{\sigma}\otimes\mathbf{X}_{\sigma}

\displaystyle\mathbf{D}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}:\sigma\neq 0}\mathbf{g}_{i+1,\sigma}\mathbf{X}_{\sigma}

for some random operators
\mathbf{X}_{\sigma}
’s, where
\mathds{1}_{m},\mathbf{B}_{\sigma}
’s and
\mathbf{g}_{i+1,\sigma}
’s are in the
\left(i+1\right)
-th register.

\mathbf{B}
and
\mathbf{D}
only differ in the
(i+1)
-th register. For the first equality,

\displaystyle\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~\mathbf{B}f\left(\mathbf{A}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~\mathbf{B}\left(\mathds{1}_{m}\otimes f\left(\mathbf{C}\right)\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}:\sigma\neq 0}\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~\left(\mathcal{B}_{\sigma}\otimes\mathbf{X}_{\sigma}\right)\left(\mathds{1}_{m}\otimes f\left(\mathbf{C}\right)\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}:\sigma\neq 0}\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~\mathcal{B}_{\sigma}\otimes\left(\mathbf{X}_{\sigma}f\left(\mathbf{C}\right)\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}:\sigma\neq 0}\mathrm{Tr}~\mathcal{B}_{\sigma}\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{X}_{\sigma}f\left(\mathbf{C}\right)\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0.

The last equality follows from the orthogonality of
\left\{\mathcal{B}_{i}\right\}_{i\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}}
and
\mathcal{B}_{0}=\mathds{1}_{m}
, which implies that
\mathrm{Tr}~\mathcal{B}_{\sigma}=\mathrm{Tr}~\mathcal{B}_{\sigma}\mathcal{B}_{0}=0
.
And

\displaystyle\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~\mathbf{D}f\left(\mathbf{C}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}:\sigma\neq 0}\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~\left(\mathbf{g}_{i+1,\sigma}\mathbf{X}_{\sigma}\right)f\left(\mathbf{C}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\sigma\in[m^{2}]_{\geq 0}:\sigma\neq 0}\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathbf{g}_{i+1,\sigma}\right]\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~\left(\mathbf{X}_{\sigma}f\left(\mathbf{C}\right)\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0.

For the second equality,

\displaystyle\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~\mathbf{B}f\left(\mathbf{A}\right)\mathbf{B}g\left(\mathbf{A}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~\mathbf{B}\left(\mathds{1}_{m}\otimes f\left(\mathbf{C}\right)\right)\mathbf{B}\left(\mathds{1}_{m}\otimes g\left(\mathbf{C}\right)\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\sigma,\tau\neq 0}\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~\left(\mathcal{B}_{\sigma}\otimes\mathbf{X}_{\sigma}\right)\left(\mathds{1}_{m}\otimes f\left(\mathbf{C}\right)\right)\left(\mathcal{B}_{\tau}\otimes\mathbf{X}_{\tau}\right)\left(\mathds{1}_{m}\otimes g\left(\mathbf{C}\right)\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\sigma,\tau\neq 0}\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~\left(\mathcal{B}_{\sigma}\mathcal{B}_{\tau}\right)\otimes\left(\mathbf{X}_{\sigma}f\left(\mathbf{C}\right)\mathbf{X}_{\tau}g\left(\mathbf{C}\right)\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\sigma,\tau\neq 0}\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~\left(\mathcal{B}_{\sigma}\mathcal{B}_{\tau}\right)\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{X}_{\sigma}f\left(\mathbf{C}\right)\mathbf{X}_{\tau}g\left(\mathbf{C}\right)\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m\sum_{\sigma\neq 0}\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~\left(\mathbf{X}_{\sigma}f\left(\mathbf{C}\right)\mathbf{X}_{\sigma}g\left(\mathbf{C}\right)\right)\right].

And

\displaystyle\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~\mathbf{D}f\left(\mathbf{C}\right)\mathbf{D}g\left(\mathbf{C}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\sigma,\tau\neq 0}\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~\left(\mathbf{g}_{i+1,\sigma}\mathbf{X}_{\sigma}\right)f\left(\mathbf{C}\right)\left(\mathbf{g}_{i+1,\tau}\otimes\mathbf{X}_{\tau}\right)g\left(\mathbf{C}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\sigma,\tau\neq 0}\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathbf{g}_{i+1,\sigma}\mathbf{g}_{i+1,\tau}\right]\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{X}_{\sigma}f\left(\mathbf{C}\right)\mathbf{X}_{\tau}g\left(\mathbf{C}\right)\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\sigma\neq 0}\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~\left(\mathbf{X}_{\sigma}f\left(\mathbf{C}\right)\mathbf{X}_{\sigma}g\left(\mathbf{C}\right)\right)\right].

∎

Proof of
10.8
.

To prove the first equality, it suffices to show that

\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~t\left(\mathbf{A},\mathbf{B}\right)\right]=m\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~t\left(\mathbf{C},\mathbf{D}\right)\right],

(89)

for

\displaystyle t\left(A,B\right)\in\left\{p\left(A\right)B,A^{2}B,A\cdot\left|A\right|\cdot B\right\},

due to Eqs.
Eq.

75
Eq.

86
. It directly follows from Eq.
Eq.

87
in
C.1
.

To prove the second equality it suffices to prove that

\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~t\left(\mathbf{A},\mathbf{B}\right)\right]=m\bigE_{\begin{subarray}{c}\end{subarray}}\>\!\!\left[\mathrm{Tr}~t\left(\mathbf{C},\mathbf{D}\right)\right]

for

\displaystyle t\left(A,B\right)\in\left\{AB^{2},\left|A\right|B^{2},B\kappa_{B}\left(A\right)\right\},

due to Eqs.
Eq.

86
Eq.

79
. Then the second equality in
10.8
follows from the continuity of
D^{2}\zeta_{\lambda}\left(\cdot\right)
due to
Lemma

B.17
.

The first two cases directly follow from Eq.
Eq.

88
in
C.1
. To prove the final case, we use
Lemma

B.6
and have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}~B\kappa_{B}\left(A\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}~\left(AB+BA\right)\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-t\left|A\right|}\left(AB+BA\right)e^{-t\left|A\right|}dt

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{Tr}~\left(Ae^{-t\left|A\right|}BAe^{-t\left|A\right|}B+A^{2}e^{-t\left|A\right|}Be^{-t\left|A\right|}B\right)~dt

Then the result follows from Eq.
Eq.

88
in
C.1
.
∎
